# Validation of RocksDb.Managed against the C++ sources

Audit of the managed port (`src/RocksDb.Managed`) against the C++ tree in the repository root (RocksDB 11.14, `main`).
Each managed file was read side by side with the C++ files it cites. The audit checked:

- on-disk constants, magic numbers, tags and enum values
- byte order and field order
- boundary conditions: empty input, truncated input, overflow
- error paths: every C++ `Corruption` / `InvalidArgument` path has a managed equivalent
- option defaults against `include/rocksdb/*options*.h` and `table.h`
- algorithm fidelity
- concurrency

Status values used in the tables:

- **verified**: matches upstream. Any differences are listed and harmless.
- **fixed**: a discrepancy was found and corrected in this audit (regression tests in
  `tests/RocksDb.Managed.Tests/Validation/AuditRegressionTests.cs` unless noted).
- **known deviation**: intentional or accepted difference, with the reason.
- **not ported**: an upstream feature a user could hit that the port lacks.

The existing tests and the native compat tests back many "verified" entries. These include the byte-identical
WriteBatch, SST, MANIFEST and filter comparisons and the WAL read/write tests in both directions. Ports of upstream
test vectors added by this audit:

- `db/db_iter_test.cc`: DBIterator1..14, SeekToLastOccurrenceSeq0, the lower-bound tests and a SeekForPrev
  upper-bound clamp. See `Iterators/DbIterUpstreamTests.cs`.
- `util/coding_test.cc`: fixed-width encodings, varints, overflow and truncation, length-prefixed slices, zigzag.
- `db/dbformat_test.cc`: value-type validation in ParseInternalKey.

| `Util/Crc32C.cs` | `util/crc32c.{h,cc}` | verified (Mask/Unmask delta `0xa282ead8`, 3-way interleave equals the serial CRC; existing tests cover every length and alignment) |
| `Util/Hash.cs` | `util/hash.{h,cc}`, `util/fastrange.h` | verified. `Hash()` sign-extends the tail bytes, seeds 0xbc9f1d34 / 397, Bijective(Un)Hash2x64 constants match. |

| `Util/ByteCompare.cs` | (memcmp) | verified (sign-equivalent to `SequenceCompareTo`, tested) |
| `Util/PooledBufferWriter.cs` | (std::string buffers) | verified (no format impact) |

## Compression

| Managed file | C++ source(s) | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `Compression/CompressionType.cs` | `include/rocksdb/compression_type.h` | verified (byte values) |
| `Compression/BlockCompression*.cs` | `util/compression.{h,cc}` (BuiltinCompressionManagerV2, StartCompressBlockV2, ExtractUncompressedSize), `block_based_table_builder.cc` (max_compressed_bytes_per_kb capped at 1023) | verified for framing, level sanitization and the LZ4/LZ4HC level mapping. Known deviations: (1) a configured BZip2/Xpress type stores blocks uncompressed, where upstream falls back to the default compressor; (2) LZ4 acceleration > 1 runs with acceleration 1; (3) zlib output differs from native's (not byte-identical): inputs above 16 KiB are deflated in independent 16 KiB chunks (sync flush, no final block, then an empty final block) so a native inflater with window_bits -14 accepts them (.NET's deflater has a 32 KiB window on both target frameworks; .NET 11's DeflateEncoder could set the window but .NET 10 has no such API) -- compat: `BlobCompatTests.LargeZlibBlobsAndBlocksReadableBothWays` (64 KiB zlib data blocks and 16 KiB..150 KB zlib blobs written by managed, read, relocated and extended by native, read back); (4) the ZSTD frame checksum option is ignored. All output stays readable by native. |
| `Compression/BlockCompression.Dictionary.cs`, `CompressionDictionary.cs` | `util/compression.{h,cc}` (CompressionDict / UncompressionDict, ZSTD_TrainDictionary / ZSTD_FinalizeDictionary, BuiltinZSTDCompressorV2 / BuiltinLZ4CompressorV2WithDict dictionary paths, Zlib_DecompressBlock) | **ported.** ZSTD with raw, trained (ZDICT_trainFromBuffer) and finalized (ZDICT_finalizeDictionary) dictionaries via ZstdSharp's port of the same zstd: dictionaries and dictionary-compressed blocks are byte-identical to native. Reading LZ4, zlib (dictionary replayed as a stored deflate block) and Snappy (dictionary ignored, as upstream) dictionary tables. Known deviations: LZ4HC with a dictionary compresses with fast LZ4 (same wire format); zlib writes without the dictionary (.NET has no preset-dictionary deflate); both stay readable by native. Parallel compression: see "Parallel compression and per key-value protection" below. |
| `Compression/Lz4BlockEncoder.cs` | `lz4.c` (LZ4_compress_generic, byU16 / noDict / limitedOutput, LZ4_count) as linked into RocksDB | **ported.** Fast LZ4 for inputs below 64 KiB + 11: byte-identical to native LZ4_compress_fast (compat test), 1.4 - 1.7x faster than K4os (docs/perf.md "LZ4 encoder and parallel compression"). Acceleration > 1 is not supported (as before); LZ4HC, larger inputs and decoding use K4os. |

## Table and filters

| Managed file | C++ source(s) | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `Table/BlockBuilder.cs` | `table/block_based/block_builder.{h,cc}`, `data_block_footer.cc` | verified, including the fv8 common-prefix rewrite and the size estimate. **Ported:** separated key/value storage (separate_key_value_in_data_block: value-offset varint at restart entries, values section, values_section_offset footer word, bit 28) and the uniformity hint (ScanForUniformity / UniformDataTracker / ReadBe64FromKey, bit 29); data and index blocks are byte-identical to native 11.12 (FormatFeatureCompatTests). |
| `Table/Block.cs`, `BlockIter.cs` | `block.cc` (Block ctor, BlockIter::ParseNextKey), `data_block_footer.cc` | verified (footer bits 28..31, restart validation against the keys section). Separated key/value blocks are read: restart entries take their value from values_section + value_offset, the others follow the previous value. Deviation: restart entries are recognized by their offset (restart array) instead of entry index modulo block_restart_interval (equivalent for well-formed blocks, no table property needed). `is_uniform` is exposed (Block.IsUniform) and used by index_block_search_type kAuto; kInterpolation / kAuto index search is ported (see "Small gaps closed (2026-11-03)"). |
| `Table/BlockIter.cs` | `block.cc`, `block_util.h` (DecodeEntry / DecodeKeyV4) | verified (3-byte minimum, shared/non-shared bounds, value-delta decoding) |
| `Table/BlockHandle.cs` | `table/format.cc` (BlockHandle, IndexValue incl. fv8 escape) | verified |
| `Table/Footer.cs` | `table/format.{h,cc}` (Footer, FooterBuilder) | verified (fv2..8, extended magic, context checksum, metaindex gap, legacy magic is NotSupported) |
| `Table/BlockChecksum.cs` | `table/format.cc`, `reader_common.cc` | verified (XXH3 last-byte modifier, context modifier) |
| `Table/BlockFetcher.cs` | `table/block_fetcher.cc` | verified (truncated read and checksum mismatch raise Corruption) |
| `Table/DataBlockHashIndex.cs` | `data_block_hash_index.{h,cc}` | verified (sentinels 255/254, odd bucket count, Initialize bounds) |
| `Table/IndexBuilder.cs` | `index_builder.{h,cc}` (ShortenedIndexBuilder, PartitionedIndexBuilder), `flush_block_policy.cc` | verified by the byte-identical index-block compat tests; partitioned indexes (kTwoLevelIndexSearch, metadata_block_size, coupled and decoupled filter partitions) are byte-identical to native SstFileWriter for fv2..8 (partitions, top level, index properties). kHashSearch (HashIndexBuilder: `rocksdb.hashindex.prefixes` / `rocksdb.hashindex.metadata`, written before the index like BuiltinIndexFactoryBuilder::FinishAndWrite) is byte-identical to native for fv3..8; out-of-domain keys reproduce upstream's unchecked Transform (a fixed-length prefix read from the internal key). Index entries after super block alignment padding force a full key/value (fv &lt;= 7) or use the value-delta escape (fv8); the uniformity hint is counted in `rocksdb.num.uniform.blocks`. |
| `Table/FilterBlockBuilder.cs`, `PinnedFilterPartitions.cs` | `full_filter_block.cc`, `partitioned_filter_block.cc`, `filter_block_reader_common.cc` | **ported.** Full and partitioned filter builders with prefix extractors (whole key + prefix as "alt", boundary prefixes at partition cuts, keys_per_partition), decouple_partitioned_filters on/off; byte-identical to native. The reader uses partitioned filters (partition index seek, partitions pinned without cache_index_and_filter_blocks, block cache otherwise), RangeMayExist / IsFilterCompatible for prefix seeks. |
| `Table/TableProperties.cs` | `table/table_properties.cc`, `table/meta_blocks.cc` | verified. Property names match, unsorted keys raise Corruption, and malformed u64 values are skipped. Trivial difference: a malformed `rocksdb.deleted.keys` value is not copied to the user properties. |
| `Table/BlockBasedTableBuilder.cs`, `BlockBasedTableReader.cs`, `BlockBasedTableIterator.cs`, `TableFormatOptions.cs`, `BlockCacheStatistics.cs` | `block_based_table_{builder,reader,iterator}.cc` | verified at the format level by the byte-identical SST compat tests (fv2..8 × checksums × compressions). Ported in this round: prefix extractor (`rocksdb.prefix.extractor.name`, prefix filtering property, table extractor recreated from its id when the configured one differs, PrefixExtractorChanged), prefix seek in the table iterator (CheckPrefixMayMatch on Seek/SeekForPrev), point lookups through the prefix filter when whole_key_filtering is off, partitioned index writing and partitioned filter reading/writing, pin_top_level_index_and_filter, dictionary buffering/sampling (kBuffered state, prime-stride sampling, `rocksdb.compression_dict`; like upstream, `num_data_blocks` does not count blocks replayed from the buffer). User-defined timestamps: see "User-defined timestamps". Ported later: block_align (padding + FlushBlockBySizePolicy align rule, index value delta encoding off), super_block_alignment_size / _space_overhead_ratio (WriteMaybeCompressedBlockImpl padding, skip_delta_encoding), UseCommonPrefixForIndex gate, kHashSearch write + HashIndexReader / BlockPrefixIndex (point lookups and MultiGet prefix-seek the index when the prefix extractor is unchanged; iterators keep total-order index seeks), user table properties collectors (`TablePropertiesCollector`, see below). Parallel compression: see "Parallel compression and per key-value protection" below. **Not ported:** pin_l0_filter_and_index_blocks_in_cache and partition pinning tiers. |
| `Table/Filters/FilterBitsReaders.cs` | `filter_policy.cc` (GetBuiltinFilterBitsReader) | verified. Every reserved or unknown encoding maps to AlwaysTrue, and an empty filter maps to AlwaysFalse. A Ribbon filter shorter than its metadata raises Corruption (undefined behavior upstream). |
| `Table/Filters/BloomImpl.cs`, `FilterBitsBuilders.cs`, `Standard128Ribbon.cs`, `BuiltinFilterPolicies.cs`, `FilterContracts.cs` | `util/bloom_impl.h`, `filter_policy.cc`, `util/ribbon_*.h` | verified by the byte-identical filter compat tests. `optimize_filters_for_memory` (AllocateMaybeRounding, aggregate_rounding_balance_, GetNumProbes after rounding) is ported with a model of the allocator's `malloc_usable_size` (`Filters/FilterAllocationModel.cs`): glibc on Linux (the NuGet librocksdb.so), none elsewhere (native builds without ROCKSDB_MALLOC_USABLE_SIZE). Known deviation: glibc is not fully deterministic (a free chunk up to 24 bytes larger may be returned unsplit; requests of 128 KiB+ depend on mmap vs heap top), so Ribbon filters (16-byte granularity) can occasionally be one rounding step smaller than native's; Bloom filters below 128 KiB match byte for byte. Readers are unaffected. |

It now implements `IsExtendedValueType` exactly, and `IsValueType` / `IsExtendedValueType` helpers were added. (low) |

Exceptions from AddUserKey / BlockAdd are ignored and from Finish drop that collector's properties (upstream logs a non-OK Status). CompactForTieringCollector ported later (see "Small gaps closed (2026-11-03)"). Not ported: readable properties logging. |
| `Db/InternalKeyComparator.cs` | `db/dbformat.{h,cc}`, `index_builder.cc` (FindShortestInternalKeySeparator) | verified |
| `Db/VersionEdit.cs` | `db/version_edit.{h,cc}`, `db/wal_edit.cc` | verified. All tags match, as do the NewFile4 custom-tag order, the forward-compat masks (`1<<13` and custom `1<<6`), the signed path_id, the u64 kNewFile seqno defaults and the error messages. `IsValidInternalKey` now delegates to `DbFormat.IsExtendedValueType`. Known deviation: string fields (db id, comparator, CF name) go through UTF-8, so CF names containing invalid UTF-8 bytes would not round-trip byte for byte. |
| `Db/FileMetaData.cs` (+ BlobFileAddition/Garbage, WalAddition) | `db/version_edit.h`, `db/blob/blob_file_{addition,garbage}.cc`, `db/wal_edit.cc` | verified (blob additions/garbage are now applied to versions, see "Integrated BlobDB") |
| `Db/FileNames.cs` | `file/filename.cc`, `util/string_util.cc` (ConsumeDecimalNumber), `db/manifest_ops.cc` | verified (ParseFileName branch for branch, SetCurrentFile protocol, CURRENT parsing). The deviation for an info-log prefix match is documented in the file. |
| `Db/Manifest.cs`, `Db/ManifestLog.cs` | `db/version_edit_handler.cc`, `db/version_set.cc` (AtomicGroupReadBuffer), `db/version_builder.cc`, `db/wal_edit.cc` (WalSet) | verified (atomic groups, CF add/drop, monotonic per-CF log numbers, max min-log, NewestFirstByEpochNumber and BySmallestKey). Comparator/num_levels validation ported (see "User features"). full_history_ts_low is applied, kept in new-MANIFEST snapshots and recovered. UDT boundary padding (persist_user_defined_timestamps = false) ported, see "User-defined timestamps in memtable only". Best-efforts recovery and WAL tracking: see "Recovery, WAL tracking, space and I/O limits" below. |
| `Db/WriteBatch.cs`, `Db/WriteBatchReader.cs`, `Db/IWriteBatchHandler.cs` | `db/write_batch.cc` | verified. Every tag and error string of ReadRecordFromWriteBatch is covered, along with the count check and TimedPut unpacking. A TimedPut value shorter than 8 bytes raises Corruption (undefined behavior upstream). |
| `Db/MemTable.cs` | `db/memtable.{h,cc}` | **fixed.** Concurrency (medium): the fragmented range-tombstone cache published the list and its version as two separate fields. A reader racing a rebuild could pair the previous list with the new version and miss a tombstone visible to its snapshot (a deleted key could reappear). The list and version are now published together as one immutable object. The regression test is a stress test, so it may not catch the race on every run. Get/SaveValue and ShouldFlushNow were checked and match. **Ported:** memtable bloom (DynamicBloom in the arena, `memtable_prefix_bloom_size_ratio` / `memtable_whole_key_filtering`; Get and MultiGet skip the skiplist on a bloom miss, prefix-mode iterators on Seek/SeekForPrev), insert hints (`memtable_insert_with_hint_prefix_extractor`, InlineSkipList::InsertWithHint with a per-prefix splice), inplace_update_support (MemTable::Update / UpdateCallback with striped reader/writer locks, the inserter's DB-read fallback for inplace_callback, CheckConcurrentWritesSupported). Deviations: the locks are spin locks over an int array; Get copies an in-place-updatable value under the read lock into a per-thread buffer; during WAL recovery an inplace_callback for a key not in the memtable sees no existing value (upstream fails the replay with NotSupported). |
| `Db/MemTableSupport.cs`, `Db/MemTableRangeTombstones.cs` | `db/column_family.cc` (arena sanitize), `db/range_tombstone_fragmenter.cc` | verified. The fragmenter is a simplified port that keeps every seqno and applies snapshot striping at query time. |
| `Db/GetContext.cs` | `table/get_context.cc` | verified (range-deletion override of value types); timestamp of the first matched entry (UDT) |
| `Db/MergeHelper.cs`, `Db/MergeOperator.cs` | `db/merge_helper.{h,cc}`, `db/merge_context.h` | verified. Key choice and type rewrite after a full merge, the PartialMergeMulti fold, compaction-filter skip-until and the range-delete check per operand all match. Wide-column base values ported (TimedFullMergeWithEntity, see "User features"). full_history_ts_low handling of MergeUntil ported (UDT). OpFailureScope (kMustMerge keeps the operands, kDefault = kTryMerge) and FullMergeV3 ported in the third pass, see "TTL merges, merge failure scope, FullMergeV3, transaction APIs". Blob base values are fetched through the compaction's blob fetcher (see "Integrated BlobDB"). |
| `Db/MergeHelper.BuiltinOperators.cs` | `utilities/merge_operators/uint64add.cc`, `string_append/stringappend.cc`, `db/merge_operator.cc` | verified (names, malformed values count as 0, ',' delimiter). The other built-ins: see "Built-in merge operators". |
| `Db/RangeDelAggregator.cs` | `db/range_del_aggregator.{h,cc}` (incl. TruncatedRangeDelIterator) | verified (truncation with kTypeMaxValid and seq-1, read and compaction stripes) |
| `Memtable/InlineSkipList.cs` | `memtable/inlineskiplist.h` | verified. Node layout, splice logic and CAS insert match, with acquire/release on links. The random height uses 32-bit xorshift; this only affects performance. |
| `Memory/Arena.cs`, `ConcurrentArena.cs`, `BlockBufferPool.cs` | `memory/arena.{h,cc}`, `memory/concurrent_arena.{h,cc}` | verified (OptimizeBlockSize clamp and alignment) |

## Db: iterators

| Managed file | C++ source(s) | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `Db/Iterators/DbIter.cs` | `db/db_iter.cc` | verified. FindNextUserEntry with the reseek, MergeValuesNewToOld, ReverseToForward / ReverseToBackward, PrevInternal, FindValueForCurrentKey(+UsingSeek), FindUserKeyBeforeSavedKey and bounds clamping were compared line by line, and 14 upstream tests were added. Range tombstones: every visible entry is checked against the RangeDelAggregator (pre-7.x scheme) and a covered entry makes the child that produced it skip the covered range in one seek where its files are older than the tombstone (`SkipCoveredForward` / `SkipCoveredBackward`, the effect of upstream's MergingIterator SkipNextDeleted / SkipPrevDeleted reseeks). Deviation: tombstones are not heap entries and level iterators emit no sentinel keys; skipping uses the files' seqno ranges instead of the level-order invariant, so only entries the per-entry check would drop are skipped (same results as before; the deleted-range benchmark is faster than native, see perf.md). Reverse steps pin data blocks (`PinnedIteratorsManager`, TempPinData / ReleaseTempPinnedData / IsValuePinned, ReadOptions.pin_data) instead of copying values; FindValueForCurrentKey's merge operands are referenced in place too (forward merges still copy). The reverse functions run as one resumable state machine shared by the sync and the async API (PrevAsync / SeekForPrevAsync / SeekToLastAsync await block reads); the ReverseToForward of NextAsync awaits as well. Prefix seek ported: prefix_same_as_start (PrefixCheck / ShouldSetPrefix), expect_total_order_inner_iter_ re-seeks on direction changes, max_skip_ disabled for SeekToFirst/SeekToLast in prefix mode, total_order_seek / auto_prefix_mode; `LevelIterator` invalidates past the seek prefix after skipping a file (LevelIterator::Seek). Identical results to native on native- and managed-written DBs (compat tests). Wide-column entities ported (IsEntity / Columns, merges onto entities). Blob values ported (see "Integrated BlobDB"). User-defined timestamps ported (see "User-defined timestamps"). **Not ported:** read callbacks, tombstone-run conversion. |

| `Db/Iterators/MergingIterator.cs` | `table/merging_iterator.cc`, `util/heap.h` | verified (SwitchToForward / SwitchToBackward protocol; async positioning in both directions, async direction switches; range tombstones: see DbIter) |
| `Db/Iterators/PinnedIteratorsManager.cs` | `db/pinned_iterators_manager.h`, `BlockBasedTableIterator` cleanup delegation (`DelegateCleanupsTo`), `InternalIterator::IsValuePinned` | **ported** for data blocks: the table iterator hands the block it leaves to the manager while pinning; memtable values are always pinned (arena memory at fixed addresses). Not ported: pinning of whole iterators (PinIterator), key pinning (IsKeyPinned). |
| `Db/Iterators/LevelIterator.cs` | `db/version_set.cc` (LevelIterator) | verified. SeekForPrev before the first file reads file 0 instead of returning early; the result is the same. |
| `Db/Iterators/Iterator.cs`, `DbIteratorFactory.cs`, `InternalIterator.cs`, `EmptyIterator.cs`, `MemTableInternalIterator.cs`, `TableInternalIterator.cs` | `db/arena_wrapped_db_iter.cc`, `table/internal_iterator.h` | verified (wrappers) |

## Options

| Managed file | C++ source(s) | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `Options/ColumnFamilyOptions.cs` | `include/rocksdb/{options,advanced_options}.h`, `db/column_family.cc` | **fixed.** `ArenaBlockSize` defaulted to 1 MiB, while upstream defaults to 0 and derives min(1 MiB, write_buffer_size/8), 4 KiB aligned. With a small `WriteBufferSize` a fresh memtable already asked for a flush: this is the todo item "small WriteBufferSize with default ArenaBlockSize trips Debug.Assert", and in release builds it caused flush churn. The default is now 0, which `MemTableOptions.GetEffectiveArenaBlockSize` already maps to the upstream derivation. (medium) Known deviation: OPTIONS files now carry `arena_block_size=0` (native writes the sanitized value); both sides load it. Other defaults checked: LZ4 compression, level0 20/36 triggers, 64 MiB target file size, 256 MiB level base, dynamic level bytes, max_sequential_skip 8. `ttl` stores the sanitized 30 days instead of the `0xfffffffffffffffe` sentinel. |
| `Options/BlockBasedTableOptions.cs` | `include/rocksdb/table.h` | verified (fv7, XXH3, 4 KiB blocks, restart 16/1, decouple_partitioned_filters, optimize_filters_for_memory, readahead sizes) |

Audit #3 compared every default with `options.h`, `advanced_options.h`, `advanced_compression.h` and `universal_compaction.h` (all match; `delayed_write_rate` stores the sanitized 16 MiB) and the enum values and names. |

## Db: database layer (audit #2)

Audit of the DB layer against `db/db_impl/*.cc`, `db/version_set.cc`, `db/version_builder.cc`, `db/column_family.cc`,
`db/write_thread.cc`, `db/flush_job.cc`, `db/memtable_list.cc`, `db/table_cache.cc`, `db/compaction/*`,
`db/external_sst_file_ingestion_job.cc`, `db/transaction_log_impl.cc`, `db/wal_manager.cc`, `utilities/checkpoint` and
`db/db_impl/db_impl_secondary.cc`. The focus was durability ordering, crash consistency, concurrency and semantics
rather than formats (audit #1). Regression tests are in `tests/RocksDb.Managed.Tests/Db/DbLayerAuditTests.cs` and
`Db/DbCrashConsistencyTests.cs` unless noted.

New test infrastructure:

- `Db/TestHooks.cs` (src): a lite TEST_SYNC_POINT. Named callbacks, invoked only in DEBUG builds
  (`[Conditional("DEBUG")]`), at `DBImpl::RunCompaction:Start` / `:BeforeLogAndApply`,
  `DBImpl::FlushMemTables:Start` / `:BeforeLogAndApply`, `VersionSet::LogAndApply:BeforeManifestWrite` and
  `DBImpl::GetImpl:AfterSuperVersion`.
- `tests/.../Db/FaultInjectionFileSystem.cs`: wraps `MemoryFileSystem`. It fails the N-th mutating operation (and
  every later one, or only that one), optionally with a torn append, can fail the next append to a named file, and
  counts open file handles and locks for leak checks.
- `DbCrashConsistencyTests`: a deterministic workload (puts, deletes, range deletes, sync writes, manual flushes,
  background flushes and compactions, two column families) crashed at about 300 operation indices. It covers power
  loss (unsynced data and directory entries dropped) and process kill with torn writes. After each crash the test
  checks that the DB reopens, that synced (or, for a kill, all acknowledged) writes survive, that the state equals
  some prefix of the issued writes in both CFs, that Get and the iterator agree, and that the DB keeps working
  afterwards. It also asserts that no file handle or LOCK leaks after a failed close or a failed open.
- `ConcurrentStressWithInvariants` runs 2 x 2.5 s. Writers (async and batched) overwrite keys while readers do Get,
  snapshot re-reads and full scans, a thread runs flushes and CompactRange, and another creates, flushes and drops
  column families while one of their iterators is open. It checks that a key is never missing or mis-valued, that
  a snapshot's view does not change, that iterator counts are right, that no background error occurs, and that
  everything survives a reopen.

| Area (managed) | C++ source(s) | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `VersionSet.LogAndApplyAsync`: edits for dropped column families | `version_set.cc` ProcessManifestWrites / LogAndApply (`IsDropped` -> ColumnFamilyDropped) | **fixed (high).** A flush or compaction that checked `Dropped` before calling LogAndApply could append its edit after the drop record. The next open then failed with "MANIFEST record referencing unknown column family", making the DB unopenable. LogAndApply now rejects the edit under the MANIFEST lock, which also serializes drops. |
| `VersionSet.LogAndApplyAsync`: failed MANIFEST write | ProcessManifestWrites (`descriptor_log_.reset()`, keep the new MANIFEST when only the CURRENT update failed) | **fixed (high).** After a failed, possibly torn, append, later edits were appended to the same MANIFEST behind the torn record, and recovery dropped them (a column family created afterwards was lost). The next edit now rolls to a new MANIFEST. If writing a new MANIFEST succeeded but the CURRENT update failed, that MANIFEST is no longer deleted, since CURRENT may already name it. |
| `VersionSet` last_sequence in edits | LogAndApplyHelper (`max_last_sequence` / `descriptor_last_sequence_`) | **fixed (medium, with ingestion below).** The recorded last sequence never decreases and covers the largest seqno of the added files. |
| `DbImpl.Ingest` sequence publication | `DBImpl::IngestExternalFiles` (SetLastSequence after LogAndApply) | **fixed (medium).** The last sequence was published before the MANIFEST write, so a snapshot taken during that window had the ingested seqno but did not see the files, and its view changed once they were installed. It is now published together with the super version install. |
| `DbImpl.Read` Get / GetAsync / NewIterator | `DBImpl::GetImpl`, `NewIteratorImpl` (reference SV, then read the sequence) | **fixed (medium).** The read sequence was taken before the super version. A flush followed by a (non-bottommost) compaction in between could drop the version visible at that sequence, so a Get returned an older, overwritten value. The deterministic test reproduces this: it returned `v0` after `v1` and `v2` were written. Snapshot validation now also happens before the reference, so a bad snapshot no longer leaks a super version reference. |
| Drop CF during flush | `DBImpl::DropColumnFamilyImpl`, MemTableList refs, FlushJob | **fixed (medium).** Drop released the CF's immutable memtables while a flush still read them (arena freed under the reader). It also disposed the CF's table cache under readers, iterators and compactions that still held its versions. The flush also unreferenced the memtables a second time, which tripped an assert in debug builds. Now the flush holds its own memtable references, a drop that commits right after the flush's MANIFEST write is detected, and dropped CFs keep their table cache until close (their files are evicted as they become obsolete). |
| `DbImpl.Open` WAL replay, point-in-time recovery | `RecoverLogFiles` / `MaybeReviseStopReplayForCorruption` / `MaybeHandleStopReplayForCorruptionForInconsistency` | **fixed (high).** After a corrupted record, PIT recovery stopped reading every later WAL. Upstream keeps reading and resumes when a record continues the sequence, which is the case for writes made after an earlier PIT recovery with `avoid_flush_during_recovery`. Those synced writes were lost on the next restart. An IO error is now fatal in PIT mode, and the "SST file contains data beyond the point of corruption" consistency check was added. Read-only open follows the same rules. |
| `DbImpl.Write` group commit: cancellation | WriteImpl (no cancellation) | **fixed (medium).** The leader passed its own CancellationToken to the WAL append and sync. Cancelling the leader's call could abandon a half-written group record in the middle of the WAL (subsequent records then follow a torn one) and failed followers that never asked to be cancelled. WAL I/O is no longer cancellable once the group is committed, and FlushWAL is likewise not cancellable. |
| `DbImpl.Write` SwitchMemtable failure | `DBImpl::SwitchMemtable` (SetBGError on failure) | **fixed (medium).** A failure creating the new WAL or syncing the old one leaked the new WAL's file handle and left writes enabled. The handle is now closed, the file deleted, and a background error set. Found by the crash sweep's handle-leak check. |
| Close vs queued writers / unbatched operations | `DBImpl::CloseHelper`, WriteThread | **fixed (medium).** A writer or flush queued behind close became leader after close. Writers failed with a NullReferenceException wrapped as IOError (and set a background error); a queued flush could create a new WAL after the LOCK was released. Both now fail with ObjectDisposedException. |
| `DbImpl.Open` column family options | `SanitizeCfOptions` (db/column_family.cc) | **fixed (medium).** Out-of-range options were used as given. They are now sanitized like upstream: write_buffer_size clipped to [64 KiB, 64 GiB], max_write_buffer_number >= 2, min_write_buffer_number_to_merge, num_levels (>= 2 for leveled, >= 3 for universal with ingest-behind), level0 trigger ordering (stop >= slowdown >= compaction trigger), FIFO L0 triggers, max_bytes_for_level_multiplier, soft/hard pending-compaction limits, dynamic level bytes only for leveled with one path, and max_compaction_bytes = 25 x target. The OPTIONS file records the sanitized values, like native. Two existing stall tests relied on unsanitized triggers and now block compaction with a test hook instead. Not ported: memtable factory and ttl/periodic adjustments (the managed defaults are already the sanitized values). |
| WAL sync, MANIFEST + CURRENT ordering | `DBImpl::WriteToWAL`, `SyncWAL`, `SetCurrentFile`, `ProcessManifestWrites` | verified. The WAL is synced (plus a directory fsync for a new WAL) before a sync write is acknowledged. Unlike upstream, the managed SwitchMemtable syncs the old WAL eagerly instead of on the next sync write (stricter). A MANIFEST edit is synced before its version is installed. A new MANIFEST is fully written and synced, then CURRENT is written to a temp file, synced, renamed, and the directory fsynced. Table files are synced, and their directory fsynced, before the MANIFEST edit that adds them. |
| Obsolete file deletion | `FindObsoleteFiles` / `PurgeObsoleteFiles` | verified. Table files become obsolete only when the last version referencing them is released, which happens after the MANIFEST edit that dropped them is durable and every reader (super version, iterator, compaction input version) is gone. A file moved trivially is referenced before the old version is released. WALs are deleted only below the minimum log number of all live CFs, never the current WAL. MANIFESTs are deleted only after the new one is installed. Deletions are deferred while `DisableFileDeletions` is in effect (checkpoints). |
| `min_log_number_to_keep` (multi-CF, non-2PC) | `PrecomputeMinLogNumberToKeepNon2PC`, SwitchMemtable empty-CF log number bump | verified. Matches upstream, including the in-memory-only log number advance of empty CFs. Covered by the two-CF crash sweep. |
| Sequence publication vs memtable visibility | WriteImpl, PipelinedWriteImpl, `SetLastSequence` | verified. Sequences are allocated before the WAL write and published only after every memtable insert of the group succeeded (with parallel memtable inserts by the writer that finishes last, after every writer counted down). A failed memtable insert (e.g. a per-key checksum mismatch) leaves the group unpublished and sets a fatal background error with reason kMemTable (HandleMemTableInsertFailure), so readers never see a published partial group; the entries inserted before the failure stay in the memtable above the last sequence until the DB is reopened (see "Recovery and write path (second review)"). With pipelined writes the sequences are allocated by the WAL stage and each memtable group publishes its last sequence after its inserts; memtable groups run one at a time in WAL order, so publication stays monotonic and a write is never visible before every earlier write (test PipelinedWalStageOverlapsMemtableStage). After recovery the last sequence is the maximum of the MANIFEST value and the last replayed record. |
| `Db/SuperVersionCache.cs`, `DbImpl.GetAndRefSuperVersion` / `ReturnAndCleanupSuperVersion` | `ColumnFamilyData::GetThreadLocalSuperVersion`, `ReturnThreadLocalSuperVersion`, `ResetThreadLocalSuperVersions` (kSVInUse / kSVObsolete) | **ported** with per-processor slots instead of thread-local ones. Slots are reset under the mutex before the old super version's CF reference is released (InstallSuperVersion) and when the DB closes (a dropped CF keeps its last super version for reads through its handle until then). Because two threads can share a slot, a reader that finds kSVInUse takes a plain reference, and a cached super version that is no longer current is released instead of used (`PointReadsNeverUseAnObsoleteCachedSuperVersion`). |
| `TableCache.GetReaderForVersion`, `TableCacheStore.TryGetPinned` | `Version::Get` on the pinned `fd.table_reader` (max_open_files = -1) | **ported.** While the DB-wide table cache is unbounded, the first lookup through a referenced version pins the reader with one extra reference (kept until the file is evicted as obsolete or the CF / DB closes, also if SetDBOptions bounds the cache later, like upstream's pinned handles); later lookups take no per-call reference. Bounded caches reference as before. |
| Torn MANIFEST / WAL tails | `log::Reader`, VersionSet::Recover | verified by the process-kill sweep (torn appends at about 120 points). |
| `WriteThread` (pipelined writes) | `db/write_thread.cc` (LinkGroup, the pipelined branch of ExitAsBatchGroupLeader, EnterAsMemTableWriter, ExitAsMemTableWriter, WaitForMemTableWriters, newest_memtable_writer_), `db/db_impl/db_impl_write.cc` (PipelinedWriteImpl, WaitForPendingWrites) | **ported.** The memtable writer queue is a locked linked list like the batch queue (upstream: lock-free list). The WAL leader completes the writers that do not write to the memtables (failed callback, WAL-only prepares, WAL errors), links the others to the memtable queue and only then hands WAL leadership on, so memtable groups follow WAL order; memtable groups are bounded like upstream (size cap of max_write_batch_group_size_bytes / 8 without concurrent memtable writes; a batch with merge operands alone with them). Memtable groups of two or more plain writers insert in parallel, as in the non-pipelined path and upstream. Write stats are recorded by the WAL stage. The memtable stage is drained (WaitForMemTableWriters) before PreprocessWrite switches memtables or WALs (flush scheduler, max_total_wal_size), by every unbatched leader (flush, ingestion, column family changes, error recovery, close) and by a leader whose callback disallows batching. Rejections as upstream: two_write_queues ("pipelined_writes is not compatible with concurrent prepares") and seq_per_batch with NotSupported at write time; unordered_write, atomic_flush with InvalidArgument at open (DBImpl::ValidateOptions, also unordered_write with !allow_concurrent_memtable_write); blob direct write with NotSupported (column family validation). unordered_write has its own path (next row). Tests: overlap with a held memtable stage, concurrent sync/async writers (with and without parallel inserts), memtable / WAL switches with flushes, merges and per-key protection, WAL termination points, fault-injection sweeps (sequential, and concurrent writers with per-writer prefix checks), native recovery both ways (PipelinedWriteCompatTests). |
| `DbImpl.UnorderedWrite.cs` (unordered_write) | `db/db_impl/db_impl_write.cc` (the unordered branch of WriteImpl, WriteImplWALOnly with kDoAssignOrder + kDoPublishLastSeq on write_thread_, UnorderedWriteMemtable), `db/db_impl/db_impl.h` (WaitForPendingWrites, pending_memtable_writes_, switch_cv_ / switch_mutex_), `db/column_family.cc` (max_successive_merges check), `utilities/transactions/pessimistic_transaction_db.cc` (TransactionDB::Open checks) | **ported.** The WAL group runs PreprocessWrite, write callbacks, sequence allocation and the merged WAL record (under the WAL write lock with two write queues), the pre-release callbacks, publishes the group's last sequence, then adds its memtable writers to pending_memtable_writes_ before handing leadership on; every writer inserts its own batch with concurrent memtable inserts (merge operands included, as upstream) and counts down; the writer reaching 0 completes the waiter (switch_cv_ -> a TaskCompletionSource with asynchronous continuations, so async leaders do not block a thread). WaitForPendingWrites runs before PreprocessWrite's WAL / memtable switches and in every EnterUnbatched (upstream: in the EnterUnbatched callers that touch memtables -- flush, ingestion, CF drop, error recovery, GetApproximateMemTableStats; waiting in all of them is stricter). Snapshot semantics as documented in DBOptions::unordered_write: a snapshot may change while earlier-sequence inserts land, writes after it stay invisible, read-your-own-write holds. Rejections with upstream's codes/messages: !allow_concurrent_memtable_write and enable_pipelined_write (InvalidArgument at open), max_successive_merges > 0 (InvalidArgument), blob direct write (NotSupported), TransactionDB with WriteCommitted / WriteUnprepared / WritePrepared without two_write_queues (NotSupported). Deviation: a failed memtable insert is reported to its writer but does not set a background error (consistent with the port's ordered path; upstream's WriteStatusCheck sets one with paranoid_checks). Tests: UnorderedWriteTests (option checks, concurrent writers with one/two write queues under memtable/WAL switches and flushes, held insert: published-before-insert, snapshot relaxation, read-your-own-write, flush drain; WritePrepared on two queues), DbCrashConsistencyTests (serial power-loss/process-kill sweeps and concurrent-writer sweeps), UnorderedWriteCompatTests (native WAL recovery both ways via rocksdb_options_set_unordered_write, native reads the option from a managed OPTIONS file). |
| `WriteThread` | `db/write_thread.cc` | verified. Grouping rules match: no sync write under a non-sync leader, no mixing WAL-disabled or no_slowdown writers, size cap of leader size + 128 KiB. Leader handoff and pooled writers are correct. Known deviation: a leader that is cancelled while delayed by a write stall fails its whole group with OperationCanceledException (upstream has no cancellation). |
| `FlushJob`, recovery flush | `flush_job.cc`, `builder.cc`, `WriteLevel0TableForRecovery` | verified. Like upstream's flush-time CompactionIterator, versions hidden by a newer Put / Delete / blob index / entity of the same key in the same snapshot stripe are dropped (rule (A); snapshots taken when the flush starts). Known deviations: merge operands are not merged, single-delete pairs and keys covered by range tombstones of the same flush are kept, and CFs with user-defined timestamps keep every version (all correct, left to compaction). Recovery writes the L0 file and the new log number in one edit per CF; a crash between CFs is handled because each CF skips WALs below its own log number. |
| `TableCache` | `db/table_cache.cc` | verified. Ref-counted handles: eviction and Evict on deletion close a reader only after the last lease is returned. Failed opens are not cached. |
| `GetFromFile` unclipped tombstones | `TableCache::Get` | verified. Upstream also applies a file's tombstones unclipped for keys inside the file's range. Compaction outputs and the iterator/compaction aggregators truncate (`CompactionJob.CollectTruncated` at user-key granularity, which is equivalent for outputs because the point keys at the boundary are resolved by the seq-1 truncated aggregator). |
| Compaction | `compaction_job.cc`, `compaction_outputs.cc`, `compaction_iterator.cc` | verified: snapshots captured at pick time, input version referenced, outputs pending until installed and deleted on failure, edits refused for dropped CFs (above). The CompactionIterator rules and ShouldStopBefore were compared line by line in audit #3. |
| `ExternalSstFileIngestionJob` | `external_sst_file_ingestion_job.cc` | verified: level and seqno assignment (overlap means a new seqno), memtable overlap check with blocking flush, writes stopped during Run. Sequence publication was fixed (above). Overlapping files, ingest_behind, allow_db_generated_files and write_global_seqno: see "Remaining gaps closed" below. |
| Checkpoint | `checkpoint_impl.cc`, `db_filesnapshot.cc` | verified: file deletions are disabled during the copy, the MANIFEST is trimmed to the installed size, CURRENT is replacement contents, the last WAL is trimmed, and the tmp directory is renamed with directory fsyncs. |

Remaining risks:

- The read-sequence race is fixed in DbImpl.Read.cs, which is currently the only place that computes a read
  sequence. New read paths (for example a MultiGet batch, or prefix-seek iterators) must keep the same order:
  super version first, then the sequence.
- Close does not fsync the WAL (like upstream). Unsynced writes acknowledged before a close can be lost on power loss.
- ~~No automatic recovery from background errors~~: ported, see "Background errors, listeners and backups" below.
- ~~Secondary instances re-read the MANIFEST in full on every catch-up~~: they tail it (ReactiveVersionSet /
  ManifestTailer port in `Db/DbImpl.ReadOnly.cs`) and replay a MANIFEST from its start only when CURRENT names a new
  one. Remaining difference: new versions are installed without the point-in-time check that their table files exist.

## Background errors, listeners and backups

Tests: `tests/RocksDb.Managed.Tests/Db/ErrorHandlerTests.cs` (ports of the error_handler_fs_test.cc scenarios),
`Db/EventListenerTests.cs` (listener_test.cc scenarios), `Features/BackupEngineTests.cs` (backup_engine_test.cc
scenarios), `tests/RocksDb.Managed.CompatTests/Api/BackupCompatTests.cs` (native librocksdb 11.12 through the
`rocksdb_backup_engine_*` C API) and the round-trip scenario `FeatureRoundTripTests.Backup_RestoredAndExtendedByOther`.
`FaultInjectionFileSystem` (tests) gained `FailMatching(fragment, ops, count, kind)` to fail creates / appends / syncs /
renames / directory syncs of matching paths with a plain, retryable, file-scoped, no-space or data-loss IOError.

| Managed file | C++ source(s) | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `Core/Status.cs` (`ErrorSeverity`, `IOErrorScope`, `RocksDbException.Retryable/DataLoss/Scope/Severity`, `FromException`) | `include/rocksdb/status.h`, `io_status.h` | **ported.** Severity and scope values are identical. `FromException` maps an out-of-space `IOException` (ENOSPC / EDQUOT / ERROR_DISK_FULL) to IOError(NoSpace), other I/O errors to plain IOError, like a posix Env. |
| `Db/ErrorHandler.cs` | `db/error_handler.{h,cc}`, `DBImpl::Resume` / `ResumeImpl` (`db/db_impl/db_impl.cc`), `EventHelpers::NotifyOnBackgroundError` / `NotifyOnErrorRecoveryEnd` | **ported.** The three severity maps (reason, code, subcode, paranoid_checks); data loss is unrecoverable; manual_wal_flush WAL errors are fatal; retryable / file-scoped IO errors: compaction errors are not recorded (the compaction is retried after 1 s, like BackgroundCallCompaction), flush and MANIFEST errors of a flush are soft with background work paused (kFlushNoWAL / kManifestWriteNoWAL, writes continue), everything else hard; without auto resume everything is hard. Writes fail while the severity is hard or worse (lock-free `is_db_stopped` check); flush / compaction scheduling stops while background work is stopped. Automatic recovery (`max_bgerror_resume_count`, `bgerror_resume_retry_interval`, retried while the new error is retryable, ShutdownInProgress / Aborted("Exceeded resume retry count") reported to OnErrorRecoveryEnd) and `RocksDatabase.Resume/ResumeAsync` (Busy during an automatic recovery, fatal errors refused) share ResumeImpl: wait for background jobs, dummy edit to start a new MANIFEST after a MANIFEST write error, switch and flush all column families (or only retry the pending flushes for kErrorRecoveryRetryFlush), purge obsolete files, clear the error and reschedule. Listeners can replace or suppress an error handled by HandleKnownErrors (OnBackgroundError; data-loss, manual_wal_flush WAL and retryable / file-scoped I/O errors are set before listeners see them, see "Audit #4") and veto automatic recovery (OnErrorRecoveryBegin). Close ends automatic recovery first. Statistics: ERROR_HANDLER_* tickers and the retry-count histogram; `rocksdb.background-errors` now counts errors. Out-of-space errors (NoSpace / SpaceLimit) recover automatically through the SstFileManager (OverrideNoSpaceError, RecoverFromNoSpace; see below). WAL write error recovery: the failed WAL is never written or synced again, the failed write's sequence range is published and persisted as a reservation, every column family is switched to a new WAL and flushed with force_atomic_flush (the per-CF flush edits and the min-log/WAL-deletion edit are one MANIFEST atomic group, `AtomicFlushGroup`), and the recovery checks that all advanced past the failed WAL; manual (non retry-flush) recovery flushes are atomic too, like upstream. The failed write group's sequence range is fenced from GetUpdatesSince iterators (WalRecoverySequenceTracker). Known deviations: WAL-error recovery is not refused for the configurations upstream excludes (track_and_verify_wals_in_manifest, WAL recycling etc.; for recycling see "WAL write error recovery with recycling on"); no file quarantine or AsyncFileOpen errors; two_write_queues: the second queue's WAL writes set the background error like the main queue's, recovery blocks both queues (EnterUnbatched). |
| `Db/EventListener.cs`, `Db/EventListener.Notifications.cs` | `include/rocksdb/listener.h`, `db/event_helpers.cc`, `DBImpl::NotifyOn*` | **ported**: OnFlushBegin / OnFlushCompleted (FlushJobInfo with reason, seqnos, stall flags, table properties), OnCompactionBegin / OnCompactionCompleted (inputs, outputs, reason, records, status; also trivial moves and failures), OnTableFileCreated (flush / compaction outputs) / OnTableFileDeleted (obsolete files), OnBackgroundError / OnErrorRecoveryBegin / OnErrorRecoveryEnd, OnStallConditionsChanged, OnMemTableSealed, OnColumnFamilyHandleDeletionStarted, OnExternalFileIngested. Enums `BackgroundErrorReason`, `FlushReason`, `TableFileCreationReason`, `CompactionReason`, `WriteStallCondition` keep the upstream values (WriteStallCondition keeps the managed order Normal/Delayed/Stopped). Events are delivered without the DB mutex on the background job's thread; stall and memtable-sealed events (raised under the mutex by writers) go through an ordered notification queue so user code never runs on the write path. Listener exceptions are caught and logged. Deviations: column family handles live as long as the DB, so OnColumnFamilyHandleDeletionStarted fires on DropColumnFamily and at close; no OnFlushBegin for recovery flushes at open, no OnTableFileCreationStarted, OnSubcompaction*, OnBlobFile*, OnFile*Finish (file I/O tracing) or OnManualFlushScheduled. |
| `Utilities/Backup/BackupEngine.cs`, `BackupEngineReadOnly.cs`, `BackupMeta.cs`, `BackupEngineOptions.cs` | `utilities/backup/backup_engine.cc`, `include/rocksdb/utilities/backup_engine.h` | **ported.** Directory layout and meta files are byte-compatible: `meta/<id>` (schema 1, or "schema_version 2.1"; `metadata` hex field, `crc32` decimal crc32c per file, unknown ignorable fields skipped and `ni::` fields refused when reading), `private/<id>/` for MANIFEST (trimmed), CURRENT (from contents), OPTIONS and WALs, `shared_checksum/<number>_s<db_session_id>_<size>.sst` (kUseDbSessionId \| kFlagIncludeFileSize) or `<number>_<crc32c>_<size>.<ext>` (legacy naming, blob files, SSTs without a session id), `shared/` without checksum naming, temporary `.<name>.tmp` copies renamed into place. Incremental backups reuse files the directory already references (with their known checksum), unreferenced leftovers are overwritten, sizes and checksums of shared files are cross-checked. CreateNewBackup(Async) with app metadata, flush_before_backup (always with 2PC), progress callback (exceptions abort), StopBackup, failed backups deleted and garbage collected; GetBackupInfo (with file details and name_for_open), GetCorruptedBackups, VerifyBackup (sizes, optional crc32c), RestoreDbFromBackup / RestoreDbFromLatestBackup (keep_log_files incl. archive move, kPurgeAllFiles, kVerifyChecksum and kKeepLatestDbSessionIdFiles, crc32c checked while copying, CURRENT.tmp renamed last), PurgeOldBackups, DeleteBackup (also corrupt backups), GarbageCollect, destroy_old_data, max_valid_backups_to_open, BackupEngineReadOnly. Rate limiting: `BackupRateLimiter` hook (token bucket from backup_rate_limit / restore_rate_limit). Compat: native backups are listed, verified, restored and extended by managed; managed backups by native; native and managed backups of the same SST files produce identical meta lines (names and checksums) and share the files. **Resolved (test harness):** under .NET 11 the native librocksdb 11.12 wrote empty `meta/<id>` files. Root cause (gdb: `catch throw` in librocksdb; strace: the meta temp file is opened and renamed with no write): the packaged `librocksdb.so` links libstdc++ statically but exports its `std::` symbols, and its iostreams only work when those are interposed consistently. The .NET 10 apphost and the `dotnet` muxer NEED `libstdc++.so.6`, which is then in the global symbol scope; the .NET 11 apphost no longer does, so the system library is only loaded locally and every numeric `operator<<` on a `std::ostream` inside librocksdb throws `std::bad_cast` (use_facet), leaving BackupMeta::StoreToFile's ostringstream empty (also the numbers of EventLogger / stats lines in the native LOG). Minimal repro: the same program writes a valid meta through `dotnet app.dll` and an empty one through the net11 apphost. Fix: `tests/Shared/NativeStdCxxPreload.cs` (module initializer in the compat, round-trip, replication test and benchmark executables) dlopens `libstdc++.so.6` with RTLD_GLOBAL before librocksdb is loaded; the native-backup compat and round-trip tests now pass on net11.0 and fail (instead of being inconclusive) if the symptom returns. Applications hosting the native package in a .NET 11 apphost need the same workaround (or a librocksdb linked with `-Wl,--exclude-libs,ALL`); the managed port is unaffected. Table and blob files are recognized by file type, so blob files reported by GetLiveFilesStorageInfo are backed up and shared with the legacy naming. exclude_files_callback / alternate_dirs and parallel copies (max_background_operations): see "Remaining gaps closed" below. Not ported: temperatures, BackupEngine on db_paths / cf_paths (NotSupported, as upstream). The DB's crc32c file checksums are used since the "Atomic replace range, blob checksums and histograms, backup checksums, scans" pass. |

## Recovery, WAL tracking, space and I/O limits

Tests: `tests/RocksDb.Managed.Tests/Db/WalTrackingTests.cs`, `SstFileManagerTests.cs`, `RateLimiterTests.cs`,
`VersionEditReplayTests.cs` (PointInTime*), `ErrorHandlerTests.WalErrorRecoveryFlushEditsAreOneAtomicGroup`,
`DbColumnFamilyAndConcurrencyTests.TableCacheIsSharedAcrossColumnFamiliesAndBoundedByMaxOpenFiles`,
`Cache/HyperClockCacheTests.AutoTable*`, `Features/SetOptionsTests`; compat (native librocksdb 11.12):
`Api/BestEffortsRecoveryCompatTests.cs`, `Api/WalTrackingCompatTests.cs`, `Api/SetOptionsCompatTests.MutableTableOptionsLikeNative`.

| Managed file | C++ source(s) | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `Db/PointInTimeRecovery.cs`, `Db/Manifest.cs` (`ManifestReader.TryRecoverPointInTime`), `Db/DbImpl.BestEfforts.cs` | `VersionEditHandlerPointInTime` (`db/version_edit_handler.cc`), VersionBuilder found/missing file tracking (`ValidVersionAvailable`, `OnlyMissingL0Suffix`, `MissingL0FilesAreL0Suffix`, `RemainingSstFilesNotMissingBlobFiles`, `AddBlobFileIfNeeded`), `VersionSet::TryRecover` / `ManifestPicker` / `VerifyFileMetadata`, `DBImpl::Recover` | **ported.** best_efforts_recovery ignores CURRENT (a non-empty MANIFEST means the DB exists), tries the MANIFESTs newest first, verifies every added table file (exists, recorded size, and with verify_sst_unique_id_in_manifest the table opens) and blob file (opens), and gives each column family its latest valid point in time: no missing file, or only a suffix of the newest L0 files missing (with blob files only they reference), missing blob files older than every referenced one. Atomic groups: valid states are saved at group start, no version inside a group, versions after an incomplete group are only used once every CF of the group has one, and a CF edited in a group only accepts complete versions. WALs are not replayed (their numbers are marked used; they become obsolete), the recovered state is written to a new MANIFEST. Equivalent formulation: the state before each file-adding edit of a valid CF is remembered, instead of creating versions at negative edges. Compat: native and managed DBs with the newest / oldest L0 file deleted, a middle file truncated, the newest file's footer zeroed, or the L1 compaction output deleted (12 cases) recover to identical contents in native and managed, and the result reopens normally in both. The former deviation (a non-best-efforts open of a DB with a truncated table file failed only at the first read, after writing a new MANIFEST) is fixed: see "Small gaps closed (2026-11-03)". |
| `Db/DbImpl.WalTracking.cs`, `Db/VersionSet.cs` (WalSet), `Db/Manifest.cs` (`WalSet`) | `db/wal_edit.cc` (WalSet::AddWal / DeleteWalsBefore / CheckWals), `DBImpl::MarkLogsSynced` / `SyncClosedWals` / `ApplyWALToManifest`, `GetDBRecoveryEditForObsoletingMemTables`, `DBImpl::Recover`, `WriteCurrentStateToManifest` | **ported.** With track_and_verify_wals_in_manifest: the closed WALs of this instance are recorded as WalAddition(number, synced size) once they are actually synced, whichever path syncs them (memtable switch with several column families, the lazy closed-WAL sync of either write queue's sync writes, SyncWAL / FlushWAL(sync); switches made by SwitchWAL for max_total_wal_size included), at the next flush (upstream: right after that sync; a lazily synced WAL of a single column family is often obsolete by then and never recorded); a flush writes DeleteWalsBefore(min WAL to keep) in a separate edit of the same MANIFEST write; any WAL file deletion is preceded by a durable WalDeletion (if that MANIFEST write fails the WAL stays); new MANIFESTs carry the tracked WALs and the deletion marker; open verifies the tracked WALs (missing: "Missing WAL with log number: N.", shrunk: "Size mismatch: WAL (log number: N) in MANIFEST is X bytes , but actually is Y bytes on disk.", Corruption) unless best_efforts_recovery; with the option off, tracked WALs are untracked (DeleteWalsBefore(max + 1)). Compat: native opens managed MANIFESTs with tracked WALs and vice versa (option on both sides), and both report the same Corruption for a missing tracked WAL. |
| `Db/VersionSet.cs` (`LogAndApplyAsync(IReadOnlyList<ManifestEditEntry>, atomicGroup, ...)`), `Db/DbImpl.WalTracking.cs` (`AtomicFlushGroup`, `CommitAtomicFlushAsync`) | multi-edit `VersionSet::LogAndApply` / `ProcessManifestWrites`, `InstallMemtableAtomicFlushResults`, `PrecomputeMinLogNumberToKeepNon2PC` | **ported.** Several edits are written with one sync, optionally as an atomic group (kInAtomicGroup with descending remaining counts), and installed together. An atomic flush writes all members' L0 files, then commits their edits plus the min_log_number_to_keep / WAL deletion edit as one group; a member failing before the commit fails the group. |
| `SstFileManager.cs`, `Db/DbImpl.SstFileManager.cs`, `Db/ErrorHandler.cs`, `Env/FileContracts.cs` (`GetFreeSpace`) | `file/sst_file_manager_impl.cc`, `file/delete_scheduler.cc`, `ErrorHandler::OverrideNoSpaceError` / `RecoverFromNoSpace` / `CancelErrorRecoveryForShutDown`, `SanitizeOptions`, `DBImpl::Open` (TrackExistingDataFiles, ReserveDiskBuffer, CleanupDirectory), `DeleteDBFile` | **ported.** Public `SstFileManager` (`DbOptions.SstFileManager`, shareable; every DB gets a private one by default like SanitizeOptions): tracks table and blob files (live files at open, files added by installed edits), max allowed space (after a flush / compaction the DB stops with SpaceLimit "Max allowed space was reached"; Flush() reports it like WaitForFlushMemTables), compaction buffer and reservation accounting, deletion rate limit through `*.trash` renames and a background deletion task (max_trash_db_ratio), trash cleanup at open, and out-of-space recovery: NoSpace / SpaceLimit background errors start ClearError, which polls the free space (`IFileSystem.GetFreeSpace`, statvfs for the local file system) every 5 s and resumes the DB once it exceeds the reserved disk buffer (hard errors) or the compaction trigger (soft errors); listeners can veto it; close cancels it. Automatically picked (non trivial-move) compactions reserve their input size and are cancelled (COMPACTION_CANCELLED) when the space limit with the compaction buffer, or after an out-of-space error the free space, does not allow them (EnoughRoomForCompaction / OnCompactionCompletion). Known deviations: manual compactions are not checked; the space limit is checked after a compaction installs (upstream fails the output file); files are always deleted whole (no bytes_max_delete_chunk truncation, no hard-link count check); ingested files are tracked when their edit installs; no DeleteScheduler statistics or trash buckets. |
| `Env/GenericRateLimiter.cs`, `DbOptions.RateLimiter`, `Db/DbImpl.Flush.cs` (`TableFileWriteOptions`), `Db/Compaction/CompactionJob.cs` | `util/rate_limiter.cc` (GenericRateLimiter, NewGenericRateLimiter), `WritableFileWriter` rate limiting | **ported.** Token bucket refilled every refill period (default 100 ms; no carry-over), single burst bytes, priorities with IO_USER first and the fairness lottery (GeneratePriorityIterationOrderLocked), partial grants, auto-tuning (TuneLocked), SetBytesPerSecond / SetSingleBurstBytes, GetTotalBytesThrough / GetTotalRequests / GetTotalPendingRequests, modes. Waiters do not hold a thread: a one-shot timer performs the refill duty while requests are queued, and RequestAsync waits asynchronously. Flush (and recovery flush) table and blob files are charged at IO_HIGH, compaction outputs at IO_LOW, WAL / MANIFEST not at all (IO_TOTAL). Reads are charged and the RATE_LIMITER_* statistics recorded since the 2026-11-03 pass (see "Small gaps closed (2026-11-03)"). |
| `Db/TableCache.cs` (`TableCacheStore`, per-CF `TableCache` view) | `db/table_cache.cc`, `DBImpl::table_cache_` (db/db_impl/db_impl.cc) | **ported.** One reader cache per DB shared by all column families (handles keyed by file number, each opened with its column family's reader options), bounded by max_open_files - 10 (max_open_files is clipped to at least 20 at open, like SanitizeOptions; unbounded with -1), resized by SetDBOptions(max_open_files); least recently used unreferenced readers are evicted by last-use stamps copied before sorting (lookups stamp them without the lock; `DbColumnFamilyAndConcurrencyTests.BoundedTableCacheEvictsUnderConcurrentLookups`). Blob file readers keep their own per-CF bound. |
| `Cache/HyperClockCache.cs` (automatic table) | `cache/clock_cache.{h,cc}` AutoHyperClockTable | **partial (behavioral port).** estimated_entry_charge = 0 selects a table that starts at 64 slots per shard and doubles while it is more than 70 % occupied and the usage is below capacity, up to capacity / min_avg_entry_charge (default 450) slots; growth moves the slot objects to a new array under a mutator gate (inserts, evictions and erases wait; lookups stay lock-free and may miss during a move), and with full metadata charging the grown slots are charged. Not ported: upstream's lock-free linear-hashing chains (head pointers with shift, incremental splitting). Benchmarks: see docs/perf.md. |

## Transactions (utilities/transactions)

Ported in `src/RocksDb.Managed/Utilities/Transactions/` with small hooks in `Db/` (`TransactionWriteContext.cs`,
`DbImpl.Transactions.cs`, the write group leader and `MemTableInserter` in `DbImpl.Write.cs`, WAL recovery in
`DbImpl.Open.cs`, the 2PC minimum WAL in `DbImpl.Flush.cs` / `DbImpl.cs`). Tests:
`tests/RocksDb.Managed.Tests/Transactions/` (ports of optimistic_transaction_test.cc and transaction_test.cc, 49 cases;
the transaction_test.cc ports also run with the WritePrepared and WriteUnprepared policies) and
`tests/RocksDb.Managed.CompatTests/Api/TransactionCompatTests.cs` (native TransactionDB through the C API). WritePrepared /
WriteUnprepared: see "WritePrepared and WriteUnprepared transactions" below.

| Managed file | C++ source(s) | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `Db/TransactionWriteContext.cs`, leader in `DbImpl.Write.cs` | `DBImpl::WriteImpl` (callback, disable_memtable, log_ref, log_used, pre-release callback), `WriteThread::EnterAsBatchGroupLeader`, `WriteCommittedTxn::CommitInternal` (WAL termination point) | verified. Write callbacks run after PreprocessWrite and before sequence allocation; a failed callback excludes its writer from the WAL record and the memtables. Writers whose callback disallows batching are not grouped behind another leader. Group formation follows EnterAsBatchGroupLeader's r_list since the second review: incompatible writers stay queued in order and compatible writers behind them still join. A prepare writes the WAL only and consumes no sequence numbers; the WAL that received it is marked as holding a prepared section before the write is released (MarkLogCallback). The commit of a prepared transaction writes `[Commit(xid)]` to the WAL and inserts the prepared batch into the memtables at the same sequence numbers (modelled as a separate memtable batch instead of a WAL termination point; the WAL bytes are identical). |
| `MemTableInserter` 2PC markers | `MemTableInserter::MarkBeginPrepare/MarkEndPrepare/MarkCommit/MarkRollback/MarkNoop` (db/write_batch.cc) | verified (write-after-commit only). Live writes ignore the markers. Recovery rebuilds each prepared section (no memtable insert, no sequence numbers), applies it at its commit marker with `log_number_ref` = prepare WAL, drops it at a rollback marker, and fails with NotSupported ("Open with TransactionDB::Open()") without allow_2pc. The seq_per_batch (WritePrepared / WriteUnprepared) mode is described below; begin-prepare markers of another write policy fail with NotSupported (WriteBatchInternal::Iterate's WriteAfterCommit / WriteBeforePrepare checks, same messages). |
| `DbImpl.Open` WAL replay with 2PC | `RecoverLogFiles`, `SetupLogFilesRecovery`, `InsertLogRecordToMemtable` (next_sequence), `SanitizeOptions` | **fixed (high; reachable once 2PC markers are replayed).** The recovered last sequence came from the record header (seq + count - 1). A prepare record counts its entries but consumes no sequence numbers and a commit record has count 0 but consumes the prepared batch's, so the header under-estimated the last sequence after a commit and the next writes would have reused sequence numbers. It now comes from the inserter (next_sequence), also for point-in-time resumption and secondary catch-up. With allow_2pc replay starts at the persisted min_log_number_to_keep (WALs below the CF log numbers may hold prepared sections) and the recovered memtables are always flushed (avoid_flush_during_recovery is ignored, like upstream). |
| `DbImpl.Transactions.cs` prep tracking | `LogsWithPrepTracker`, `FindMinPrepLogReferencedByMemTable`, `PrecomputeMinLogNumberToKeep2PC`, `MemTable::RefLogContainingPrepSection` | verified. WAL deletion (purge, full scan, checkpoint WAL set) keeps WALs with outstanding prepared sections and the prepare WALs of committed data still in memtables; 2PC flushes always persist min_log_number_to_keep. Known deviation: deletion uses the live minimum, which can be newer than the persisted value; this is safe because a WAL is only dropped once no prepared section and no unflushed data needs it. |
| Memtable history | `MemTableList` history, `TrimHistory`, max_write_buffer_size_to_maintain | partial. Flushed memtables are kept (oldest first, referenced) while mutable + immutable + history memory stays below max_write_buffer_size_to_maintain; trimming runs at flush completion and memtable switch (upstream also trims on inserts through the trim-history scheduler). Only conflict checks read the history (as upstream). |
| `DbImpl.Transactions.cs` conflict checks | `TransactionUtil::CheckKey/CheckKeysForConflicts`, `DBImpl::GetEarliestMemTableSequenceNumber`, `GetLatestSequenceForKey` | verified. Busy on a write after the tracked sequence, TryAgain when cache_only and the memtables (with history) do not reach back to it. The table-file lookup records the newest visible entry or covering tombstone (the max of the two is used, which can only report more conflicts). |
| `OptimisticTransactionDatabase.cs` | `optimistic_transaction.cc`, `optimistic_transaction_db_impl.cc` | verified. kValidateSerial runs the check as a non-batching write callback in the group; kValidateParallel (default) takes striped bucket locks in ascending order (async waits) and checks before writing. History is enabled at open. |
| `PessimisticTransaction.cs`, `TransactionDatabase.cs` | `pessimistic_transaction.cc` (WriteCommittedTxn), `pessimistic_transaction_db.cc`, `transaction_base.cc` | verified: TryLock (lock upgrade, snapshot validation after locking, unlock on failed validation), state machine incl. expiration / lock stealing, SetName rules, Prepare / Commit / Rollback (prepared, started, committed), savepoints (unlocking keys locked since the save point), UndoGetForUpdate, direct DB writes wrapped in internal transactions with default_lock_timeout, `Write` locking sorted keys, recovered prepared transactions rebuilt with skip_concurrency_control. Transaction names are written as UTF-8 bytes. The policy-specific steps (PrepareInternal, CommitWithoutPrepareInternal, CommitInternal, RollbackInternal, ValidateSnapshot, RebuildFromWriteBatch) are virtual, as upstream. Commit-time batches (GetCommitTimeWriteBatch): WriteCommitted writes the batch and the commit marker to the WAL and inserts the batch, then the prepared batch, into the memtables (InvalidArgument for a commit without prepare with a non-empty batch). Range locks: see "Range locking" below; entities: see "Wide-column entities in transactions" below. Not ported: commit_bypass_memtable, secondary indices. User-defined timestamps: see "User-defined timestamps in transactions and WriteBatchWithIndex". |
| `PointLockManager.cs` | `lock/point/point_lock_manager.cc` (PointLockManager, not PerKeyPointLockManager) | verified: striped lock maps, shared/exclusive locks, lock limits, expiration and stealing, NotifyAll wake-up on unlock, deadlock detection with the same BFS and path order, bounded deadlock buffer. **Fixed:** the lock limit failed with Busy(LockLimit); upstream Status::LockLimit() is Aborted(LockLimit). Waiting is asynchronous (TaskCompletionSource per wait) for async callers and blocks only synchronous callers. Lock entries (LockInfo + key buffer) are recycled per stripe (up to 256 free entries), so an uncontended lock/unlock allocates nothing. |
| `LockTracker.cs` | `lock/point/point_lock_tracker.cc` | verified: Track / Untrack / Merge / Subtract with read/write counts. Keys live in recycled buffers (`PooledKey` + `KeyBufferPool`, lookups by span), so tracking and clearing keys allocates nothing in steady state. |
| `IndexedWriteBatch.cs`, `TransactionIterator.cs` | `write_batch_with_index.cc` (overwrite_key), `BaseDeltaIterator` | consolidated: thin adapters over the public `WriteBatchWithIndex` (overwrite_key mode, 2PC Noop placeholder) and `BaseDeltaIterator` (see "User features"). |

Compat (native librocksdb 11.12 via the C API): native prepared / committed / rolled back 2PC transactions (including
one left prepared at close) recover in the managed TransactionDB and vice versa; the prepare, commit and rollback WAL
records written by both are byte-identical; managed optimistic commits are readable natively.

Remaining risks: a crashed test instance keeps running its background jobs against `MemoryFileSystem` after
`SimulateCrash()` (tests must quiesce compactions first, see `TransactionTests.Crash`); the parallel validator creates
its bucket semaphores lazily.

Allocations (BenchmarkDotNet `TransactionBenchmarks`, [MemoryDiagnoser], Begin reusing the previous transaction + 4
puts + Commit, 16 B keys, 100 B values): a transaction allocates what the same 4-put WriteBatch written directly
allocates (the memtable arena growth, ~557 B), for Optimistic, Pessimistic, WritePrepared and WritePrepared with
two_write_queues, sync and async (pooled async state machines on the commit paths). Before: Optimistic +160 B,
Pessimistic / WritePrepared +768 B per transaction (lock entries, tracked keys, a closure in AssignCommitTimestamp);
plain writes into a WritePrepared DB +840 B (WriteInternal state is now pooled; SubBatchCounter compares bytewise keys
in a reusable set). A named 2PC cycle still allocates the WriteOptions clone of a prepare with disableWAL.

### Range locking

Tests: `tests/RocksDb.Managed.Tests/Transactions/RangeLockingTests.cs` (ports of range_locking_test.cc -- BasicRangeLocking,
PointRangeLockWithReverseComparator, RangeLockWithTimestampComparator, MyRocksLikeUpdate, UpgradeLockAndGetConflict,
SnapshotValidation, MultipleTrxLockStatusData, BasicLockEscalation, LockEscalationBarrier, LockWaitCount,
LockWaiteeAccess, KilledCallback* -- and of the AnyLockManagerTest cases instantiated for the range lock manager, plus
prefix ranges, endpoint ordering, single transaction mode reporting, lock memory exhaustion, deadlocks through three
transactions and ranges, cancellation, save point / undo semantics, column families, WritePrepared, and two concurrency
stress tests: random two-range transfers that check mutual exclusion directly and conserve a total, and point-lock
increments under escalation pressure). No native compat: the C API has no range lock functions.

| Managed | C++ | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `LockManager.cs`, `TransactionDatabase.cs`, `TransactionOptions.cs` (`RangeLockManager`) | `lock_manager.h`, `TransactionDBOptions::lock_mgr_handle`, `PessimisticTransactionDB` lock manager selection | **ported.** `TransactionDbOptions.RangeLockManager` selects range locking; point locks are single-key ranges (RangeLockManagerBase::TryLock). One manager serves one open database at a time (InvalidArgument otherwise; upstream does not check). WriteUnprepared is rejected with NotSupported (upstream asserts IsPointLockSupported there). |
| `RangeLockManager.cs` (`RangeLockManager`, `RangeLockManagerImpl`), `RangeLockTypes.cs` | `RangeLockManagerHandle` / `RangeTreeLockManager` (`range_tree_lock_manager.cc`), `Endpoint`, `RangeLockInfo`, `RangeDeadlockPath` | **ported**: endpoint serialization (suffix byte SUFFIX_INFIMUM / SUFFIX_SUPREMUM + key), CompareDbtEndpoints (common-prefix compare without timestamps, then suffix; no reverse-comparator compensation: callers flip ranges, as upstream), TryLock result mapping (TimedOut(LockTimeout), Busy(Deadlock) with the cycle recorded at the request's start time, Aborted when killed, Aborted(LockLimit)), lock timeout rounding to ms and one year for "no timeout", the kill callback polled every 100 ms, UnLock of one key / a tracker, GetRangeLockStatusData, GetPointLockStatus and GetDeadlockInfoBuffer reporting start keys, deadlock buffer (initial size 10), SetMaxLockMemory (EDOM -> false), escalation barrier, GetStatus counters. Deviations: lock trees are created lazily per column family with its comparator (no AddColumnFamily hook); transaction ids are the managed ids (upstream uses the transaction's address in range mode); LockWaitCount counts every request, like LTM_WAIT_COUNT (the header comment says only waits). |
| `RangeLockTree.cs` (`RangeLockTree`, `RangeLockRequest`, `RangeLockList`) | toku `locktree.cc` (acquire_lock_consolidated, single transaction optimization, release_locks, escalate, dump_locks, get_conflicts), `keyrange.cc`, `treenode.cc` (shared owners), `lock_request.cc` (start, wait, retry, retry_all_lock_requests, deadlock_exists), `wfg.cc`, `manager.cc` (memory accounting, check_current_lock_constraints, escalator), `range_tree_lock_tracker.cc` (RangeLockList Append / ReleaseLocks / ReplaceLocks) | **ported** with an idiomatic interval structure: the disjoint ranges are a list sorted by endpoint searched by binary search (upstream: a concurrent binary tree with per-node latches) under one mutex per column family; same conflict rules (any overlap with another transaction conflicts, shared locks included, unless a shared request has the exact endpoints of the single overlapping shared lock), consolidation of a transaction's own overlapping locks, STO (STO_SCORE_THRESHOLD 100, STO_BUFFER_MAX_SIZE 50K, buffer reported unmerged), escalation in batches of 128 extracted locks merging consecutive exclusive locks of one transaction unless a barrier separates them (the barrier is checked against the first lock of the run, as upstream), pending requests sorted by transaction id and retried on every release, wait-for graph built from the pending requests of the tree with the same DFS and report order. Memory accounting uses LP64 estimates of the native structure sizes (keyrange 88 B + treenode 208 B + endpoints per lock, 10 B + keys per STO record), so escalation triggers at slightly different lock counts than native. Waits are asynchronous (a TaskCompletionSource per wait) and honour cancellation. Deviation: a transaction already in a shared lock's owner list is not added (and counted) twice. |
| `PessimisticTransaction.cs`, `Transaction.cs` (`GetRangeLock(Async)`) | `PessimisticTransaction::GetRangeLock`, `TryLock` with a range tracker (IsPointLockSupported() == false), `RangeTreeLockTracker` | **ported**: every write / GetForUpdate asks the lock manager (re-locking an owned range is granted at once), validates against the snapshot each time and on failure releases the transaction's locks overlapping the key (upstream behaviour); locks are released only at commit / rollback / dispose (UndoGetForUpdate and RollbackToSavePoint keep them, the range tracker cannot untrack). Optimistic transactions: NotSupported, as the base Transaction::GetRangeLock. |

Both queues allocate sequence numbers and append to the WAL under one lock (a `SemaphoreSlim`, so async leaders do not block a thread), so WAL order is sequence order; the main queue takes the same lock while SwitchMemtable replaces the WAL. With seq_per_batch readers and snapshots use the published sequence, advanced by the commit callbacks of the second queue. Adaptations: EnterUnbatched (flush / ingestion / column family changes / close) always blocks both queues and, while holding them, syncs the last sequence with the allocated one and publishes it (upstream does this only at specific call sites); a sync write on the second queue syncs the WAL under the WAL lock instead of calling SyncWAL; WriteRecoverableState runs at every memtable switch (upstream: also selects otherwise empty CFs for a flush when only the recoverable state is pending). |
