| `Db/FlushJob.cs` | flush with snapshot_checker / DisableGCSnapshotChecker (use_custom_gc_) | **deviation (conservative).** With a snapshot checker (and during recovery of a seq_per_batch DB) the flush keeps every version instead of evaluating the checker; compaction garbage-collects them later. |
| `Utilities/Transactions/WritePreparedTxnDb.cs` | `write_prepared_txn_db.{h,cc}`, `snapshot_checker.cc` | **ported**: CommitEntry64b format and commit cache (lock-free CAS slots), IsInSnapshot (all branches incl. delayed_prepared_commits_ and old_commit_map, snap_released), PreparedHeap (deque + erased min-heap), AddPrepared / CheckPreparedAgainstMax / RemovePrepared, AddCommitted / AdvanceMaxEvictedSeq (INC_STEP_FOR_MAX_EVICTED, future_max_evicted_seq), snapshot cache + list (UpdateSnapshots, CheckAgainstSnapshots, MaybeUpdateOldCommitMap, CleanupReleasedSnapshots), SmallestUnCommittedSeq, GetSnapshotInternal, AssignMinMaxSeqs / ValidateSnapshot, Initialize after recovery (prepared batches added, everything else committed, sequence advanced by one), the pre-release callbacks. The reader/writer mutexes are plain locks (the read side only runs in rare cases). Options: wp_commit_cache_bits, wp_snapshot_cache_bits, rollback_merge_operands. |
| `Utilities/Transactions/WritePreparedTxn.cs` | `write_prepared_txn.cc` | **ported**: PrepareInternal (kTypeBeginPersistedPrepareXID into WAL + memtables, AddPreparedCallback with the first-prepare-batch log mark), commit without prepare (WriteInternal), CommitInternal (WAL-only commit marker, commit entries), RollbackInternal (old values read with a max-snapshot callback, rollback marker, one write), ValidateSnapshot (CheckKey with the callback and min_uncommitted), RebuildFromWriteBatch. two_write_queues: CommitBatchInternal / RollbackInternal write the data on the main queue with an AddPreparedCallback and commit it with a data-less second write on the WAL-only queue (WAL-less for WriteInternal, PutLogData("") + Noop for rollback) whose WritePreparedCommitEntryPreReleaseCallback (aux batch) / WritePreparedRollbackPreReleaseCallback publishes the commit and removes the prepared entries. Commit-time batches only with use_only_the_last_commit_time_batch_for_recovery (InvalidArgument otherwise, so includes_data is never true, as upstream): written WAL-only with the commit marker and cached as the DB's latest persistent state. Not ported: rollback_deletion_type_callback (always Delete). GetEntity / MultiGetEntity: WritePrepared reads go through the DB-wide hooks (no specialization needed); WriteUnprepared reads entities with its read callback (`DbImpl.GetEntityWithCallback`). |
| `Utilities/Transactions/WriteUnpreparedTxn.cs`, recovery in `TransactionDatabase.cs` | `write_unprepared_txn.cc`, `write_unprepared_txn_db.cc` | **ported**: HandleWrite / MaybeFlushWriteBatchToDB (write_batch_flush_threshold, default_write_batch_flush_threshold), FlushWriteBatchToDBInternal (kTypeBeginUnprepareXID, unprep_seqs_, untracked keys, name required), FlushWriteBatchWithSavePointToDB (flushed save points with snapshots), RollbackToSavePoint(Internal) / PopSavePoint, Prepare / CommitWithoutPrepare / CommitInternal / RollbackInternal (WriteRollbackKeys over tracked and untracked keys), destructor rollback, WriteUnpreparedTxnReadCallback (Get, iterators with the "unvalidated writes" check, conflict checks), WriteUnpreparedTxnDB::Initialize (prepared transactions rebuilt from all their batches, unprepared ones rolled back newest batch first). two_write_queues and commit-time batches as WritePrepared (WriteUnpreparedCommitEntryPreReleaseCallback publishes from the second queue; a rollback's second write commits the rollback batch as another unprepared batch). Deviations: iterators of a finished transaction are not invalidated; the recovery rollback batches are added to the commit cache (upstream relies on min_uncommitted to make them visible); the order of keys in a rollback batch follows the managed lock tracker (upstream: unordered_map order; not part of the format). |
| `TransactionDatabase.cs` | `TransactionDB::Open` (use_seq_per_batch / use_batch_per_txn), `PessimisticTransactionDB::Initialize` | **ported.** |

Compat (native librocksdb 11.12): for both policies, native committed (with duplicate keys), rolled back and prepared
transactions (WriteUnprepared: a prepared transaction written as several unprepared batches) recover in the managed
DB, are committed there, and managed prepared transactions recover natively and vice versa; the WAL records of the same
sequence of operations (plain put, 2PC commit with a duplicate key, rollback, commit without prepare) are
byte-identical; a native WritePrepared WAL with a prepared section is refused by managed WriteCommitted / allow_2pc
opens, a managed WriteUnprepared WAL is refused by native WritePrepared / WriteCommitted, and WriteUnprepared reads
WritePrepared WALs, like upstream. All of these also pass with two_write_queues on both sides (the extra rollback
record and the WAL-less WriteInternal commit are byte-identical too).

Benchmarks (dbbench, warm, 2 threads, 30k transactions of 10 puts, shared 4-core container): txnwriteprepared managed
22.5k txn/s vs rocksdb-sharp 22.0k; txnwriteunprepared 17.9k vs 13.0k; txnpessimistic 21.8k vs 22.2k (measured before the allocation work; see the
allocation note in "Transactions" above: transactions no longer allocate beyond the memtable data).

Tests: `tests/RocksDb.Managed.Tests/Features/` (unit) and `tests/RocksDb.Managed.CompatTests/Api/{CustomComparator,
PropertiesStatistics,SetOptions,WideColumn,WriteBatchWithIndex}CompatTests.cs` (native librocksdb 11.12).

| Managed file | C++ source(s) | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `Utilities/WriteBatchWithIndex/*` (`WriteBatchWithIndex`, `WriteBatchEntryIndex`, `WriteBatchWithIndexIterator`, `BaseDeltaIterator`) | `utilities/write_batch_with_index/write_batch_with_index{,_internal}.{h,cc}` | **ported.** Skip list over batch offsets ordered by (cf, key by the CF comparator, offset descending) in reusable struct-of-arrays nodes; overwrite_key (Put/Delete/SingleDelete/PutEntity update the newest entry, merges always add); GetFromBatch (MergeInProgress when only merges), GetEntityFromBatch, GetFromBatchAndDB (MergeAcrossBatchAndDB with plain or entity base), WBWIIterator with iterate bounds (Seek/SeekForPrev/SeekToFirst/SeekToLast clamping, NextKey/PrevKey, FindLatestUpdate) and BaseDeltaIterator (direction changes, deletes, merges onto base / batch Put / PutEntity / Delete, entities, columns) line by line; savepoint rollback rebuilds the index. Compat: the batch rep is byte-identical to native WBWI `rocksdb_writebatch_wi_data` for random Put/Delete/SingleDelete/Merge sequences on two CFs (both overwrite modes, also after RollbackToSavePoint), and GetFromBatchAndDB / NewIteratorWithBase results equal native on the same DB. Transactions now use it. GetEntityFromBatchAndDB / MultiGetEntityFromBatchAndDB (one DB MultiGetEntity for the unsettled keys; batch merge operands applied to the DB entity's default column, a plain base when it has only the default column, kWideBaseValue keeping the other columns; timestamp argument checks with upstream's messages). Custom comparators work for open/create/recover/flush/compaction/iterators/read-only/secondary (MANIFEST file ordering uses the configured comparators). A MANIFEST comparator name different from the configured one fails with InvalidArgument "X: does not match existing comparator Y"; files on levels >= num_levels fail with "db has more levels than options.num_levels". Compat: native DBs with a custom comparator / a comparator named `rocksdb.ReverseBytewiseComparator` round-trip with the managed port (flush, compaction, WAL tail on both sides); mismatches fail on both sides. |
| `Db/DbImpl.Options.cs`, `ColumnFamily.Options.cs` | `DBImpl::SetOptions` / `SetDBOptions`, `cf_mutable_options_type_info`, `db_mutable_options_type_info` | **ported.** Empty input, unknown names ("Unrecognized option") and immutable options ("Option not changeable") fail with InvalidArgument and change nothing; values are validated on a copy (with the open-time sanitization), applied under the DB mutex, derived state refreshed (memtable size, compaction score, write stall, flush job limit, delayed write rate, stats dump timer), a new super version installed, flushes/compactions rescheduled and a new OPTIONS file written. bytes_per_sync = 0 is stored as 1 MiB like upstream. Typed overloads take `Action<ColumnFamilyOptions>` / `Action<DbOptions>`. Compat: the same changes give the same OPTIONS-file values as native, with the same accept/reject decisions. Every super version carries an immutable options snapshot (`SuperVersion.MutableOptions`, SuperVersion::mutable_cf_options; iterators take max_sequential_skip_in_iterations and prefix_extractor from it). Block-based table options are mutable like upstream's cloned table factory (`table_factory.NAME`, `block_based_table_factory.NAME`, `block_based_table_factory={a=b;...}`; a different table factory is refused): the builder-side options (block_size, block_size_deviation, block_restart_interval, index_block_restart_interval, metadata_block_size, format_version, checksum, data block index type / util ratio, block_align, super block alignment, separate_key_value_in_data_block, uniform_cv_threshold, enable_index_compression, use_delta_encoding, index_shortening, optimize_key_common_prefix, verify_compression, optimize_filters_for_memory, detect_filter_construct_corruption) replace the column family's table format for the next flush or compaction output and are persisted; compat-tested (same OPTIONS values and data block counts as native). SetDBOptions(max_open_files) resizes the shared table cache. Reader-side table options are accepted too since the 2026-11-03 pass (see "Small gaps closed (2026-11-03)"). Known deviations: background jobs read the live options object (changed under the DB mutex) instead of a copy taken at job start; the *_bytes_per_sync options apply to files created afterwards. Since the second review (see "Options layer (second correctness review, 2026-11-06)") the whole option set is validated before it is installed, struct options are updated field by field, and SetDBOptions switches the WAL for max_total_wal_size / wal_bytes_per_sync, skips no-ops and validates every column family. |
| `Db/DbImpl.Properties.cs`, `ColumnFamily.Properties.cs`, `Core/DbProperties.cs`, `Monitoring/InternalStats.cs` | `db/internal_stats.cc`, `DBImpl::KeyMayExist`, `GetApproximateSizes`, `GetApproximateMemTableStats`, `VersionSet::ApproximateSize/OffsetOf`, `BlockBasedTable::ApproximateOffsetOf/Size`, `Version::GetPropertiesOfAllTables`, `VersionStorageInfo::EstimateLiveDataSize`, `Version::DebugString`, `TableProperties::ToString/Add` | **ported.** KeyMayExist (memtables resolved fully, then only table filters without file I/O, see "Remaining gaps closed"); approximate sizes are identical to native on the same files (compat); GetIntProperty / GetMapProperty / GetAggregatedIntProperty; properties estimate-live-data-size, cur/size-all-mem-tables, block-cache-usage/capacity/pinned-usage, num-live-versions, oldest-snapshot-time/sequence, cfstats (upstream level table format and map keys), dbstats, sstables, aggregated-table-properties[-at-level N] (the aggregatable prefix of the native string, compat-checked), options-statistics; since the second review every other ppt_name_to_info name too (see "Second review: DB API, properties, EVENT_LOG, PerfContext, Raft"). Deviations: CPU time equals wall time. (Earlier notes here said cfstats lacks the file-read latency histograms and that size-all-mem-tables equals cur-size; both were fixed since: cfstats ends with DumpCFFileHistogram, and size-all-mem-tables counts the flushed memtables kept as history or pinned.) |
| `Monitoring/Statistics.cs`, `StatisticsNames.g.cs` | `include/rocksdb/statistics.h`, `monitoring/statistics.cc`, `monitoring/histogram.cc` | **ported.** All upstream tickers/histograms (names generated from TickersNameMap/HistogramsNameMap of the C++ tree), StatsLevel with upstream values, per-processor striped long counters and histogram buckets (HistogramBucketMapper, Percentile, Data, ToString), `ToString` in native format (compat: same line formats, common names in the same order). Recorded: block cache hit/miss/add per type, bloom useful/full positive/prefix (from the table readers), memtable hit/miss, keys/bytes read and written, write done by self/other, WAL bytes/syncs, iterator seek/next/prev (+found, bytes), iterators created/deleted, flush/compaction bytes, compaction key drops, stall micros, DB_GET/DB_WRITE/DB_SEEK/FLUSH_TIME/COMPACTION_TIME/WRITE_STALL/BYTES_PER_READ/WRITE histograms. Off by default (null). Not recorded: per-level GET_HIT_*, file I/O histograms, compression timers, async iterator calls. |

Open summary + options dump, flush/compaction EVENT_LOG_v1 records, shutdown messages, periodic stats dump (stats_dump_period_sec, also adjustable with SetDBOptions). `DbOptions.InfoLog` replaces the file with a user logger. Compat: native and managed rotate each other's LOG. Read-only/secondary instances only log to a user logger. |
| `Db/Wide/*`, `ColumnFamily.Entities.cs`, wide-column paths in `GetContext`, `DbIter`, `MergeHelper`, `MemTableInserter` | `db/wide/wide_column_serialization.cc`, `wide_columns.cc`, `DBImpl::PutEntity/GetEntity`, `GetContext::SaveValue`, `DBIter::SetValueAndColumnsFromEntity`, `MergeWithWideColumnBaseValue`, `MergeHelper` (TimedFullMergeWithEntity) | **ported.** V1 serialization (byte-identical to the C++ test vectors), V1/V2 deserialization with all corruption checks (V2 blob columns: see "Integrated BlobDB"), PutEntity (sorted columns, duplicate names rejected), GetEntity (plain values as a default column), Get of an entity returns the default column, iterator `IsEntity` / `Columns` (forward, reverse, seek-based paths), merges onto entities in Get, iterators and compaction (result stays an entity with the merged default column; a missing default column merges as an existing empty value, like FullMergeV3), WAL replay, flush and compaction. Compat: entities written by managed are read, merged and compacted by native (Get returns the default column) and native-stored entities (written through a WriteBatch rep, as the 11.12 C API has no PutEntity) are read by managed across WAL, L0 and compacted files. Compaction filters on entities and blob-backed columns: see "Integrated BlobDB". Not ported: TTL DBs. |
| `MultiGetBatch` entity mode, `ColumnFamily.MultiGetEntity`, `AttributeGroups.cs` (`AttributeGroup`, `PinnableAttributeGroup`, `PutAttributeGroups`, `GetAttributeGroups`, `MultiGetAttributeGroups`), `WriteBatch.PutAttributeGroups` | `DBImpl::MultiGetEntity` (single CF and `PinnableAttributeGroups`), `DBImpl::GetEntity(key, PinnableAttributeGroups*)`, `DBImpl::PutEntity(attribute_groups)` + `WriteBatch::PutEntity(key, attribute_groups)`, include/rocksdb/attribute_groups.h | **ported.** MultiGetEntity runs the batched MultiGet path and resolves each key as an entity (plain values as a default-column entity, merges applied); `GetValue` of an entity run returns the default column. Attribute-group writes are one write batch with one entity per group (rep byte-identical to per-CF `PutEntity`; empty groups and timestamped CFs rejected with upstream's messages). Attribute-group reads use one consistent view: the explicit snapshot, or an implicit snapshot when several CFs are read (upstream: MultiCFSnapshot), then one batched MultiGetEntity per distinct CF. Naming deviation: the attribute-group overloads are `PutAttributeGroups` / `GetAttributeGroups` / `MultiGetAttributeGroups` (a C# `PutEntity(key, [])` would be ambiguous). Compat: managed attribute-group batches are applied by native and native-written groups are read back by every managed API. |
| `Db/Iterators/MultiCfIterator.cs` (`CoalescingIterator`, `AttributeGroupIterator`) | db/multi_cf_iterator_impl.h, db/coalescing_iterator.{h,cc}, db/attribute_group_iterator_impl.{h,cc}, `DBImpl::NewCoalescingIterator` / `NewAttributeGroupIterator` / `NewMultiCfIterator` | **ported.** Same positioning, direction-switch (re-seek to the current key), grouping of children at the current key, column coalescing (by name, later CF wins) and argument checks (no CF, different comparators). Deviations: children are scanned linearly instead of a binary heap (a handful of CFs), values are coalesced on first access instead of eagerly (allow_unprepared_value semantics always), a single CF still goes through the coalescing iterator (upstream returns the plain iterator), consistent view via an implicit snapshot owned by the iterator. |
| `LazyWideColumns.cs` (`LazyWideColumns`, `LazyWideColumnsBatch`), `Db/DbImpl.LazyEntity.cs`, `GetContext.DeferEntityBlobColumns`, `BlobSource.ReadBlobRange` / `BlobFileReader.ReadValueRange` | include/rocksdb/lazy_wide_columns.h, db/wide/lazy_wide_columns.cc, db/wide/read_path_blob_resolver.{h,cc}, `DBImpl::GetEntityLazy` / `MultiGetEntityLazy` / `GetEntityLazyForBatch` | **ported** (experimental upstream too). The lookup leaves a found V2 entity's blob columns unresolved unless merge operands apply; the result pins the super version (standalone) or shares the batch's pin; inline values, logical sizes (null for compressed references), whole-column resolution cached once per result, partial byte-range reads of uncompressed references (no checksum, no blob cache fill) unless `forceVerify`, kBlockCacheTier -> Incomplete, lazy-read tickers. Deviations: `max_open_files == -1` is not required (the managed port has no SST-embedded blobs, and blob files stay alive through the pinned version); `MultiResolve` (`LazyWideColumns.MultiResolve.cs`, `LazyColumnReadRequest` with per-read Result / Error): the single-result form resolves read by read (as upstream); the batch form plans like upstream (pass 1 classification: individual / whole fetch deduplicated per (entry, column) / partial range; force_verify columns and the records they reference stay individual; passes 3-5 slice the cached columns) and dispatches the blob reads grouped by blob file in offset order, but issues one read per record (the managed BlobSource has no MultiGetBlobLazy / MultiRead coalescing of adjacent records); reads are synchronous (single columns also async via `ResolveColumnAsync`); requests address results and columns by index (InvalidArgument for a not-found entry or an out-of-range column instead of a foreign column pointer); range reads copy into a caller span instead of returning a pinned slice; not supported on WritePrepared/WriteUnprepared DBs (NotSupported). Compat: native-written V2 entities are read lazily (whole and partial) by managed. |

Fixed while doing this: `FlushColumnFamilyAsync(wait)` could wait forever when the DB closed after the memtable switch
(no flush is scheduled once shutdown starts); it now fails with ObjectDisposedException and close wakes waiters (the
same fix was made independently on the port branch).

## User-defined timestamps

Port of persisted user-defined timestamps (UDT) for timestamp-aware comparators (`Comparator.BytewiseWithU64Ts`,
`ReverseBytewiseWithU64Ts` or any custom comparator with `TimestampSize > 0`). Every user key of such a column family
is stored as `key | timestamp`; the comparator orders newer timestamps first. Tests: `tests/RocksDb.Managed.Tests/Db/DbTimestampTests.cs`
(ports of db_with_timestamp_basic_test.cc, db_with_timestamp_compaction_test.cc and the write_batch_test.cc timestamp
cases), `tests/RocksDb.Managed.CompatTests/Api/TimestampCompatTests.cs` and the RoundTrip scenario
`FeatureRoundTripTests.UserDefinedTimestamps_Alternating`.

| Managed file(s) | C++ source(s) | Result |
|---|---|---|
| `Db/WriteBatch.cs` (timestamp overloads, `UpdateTimestamps`) | `db/write_batch.cc` (Put/Delete/SingleDelete/Merge/DeleteRange with ts, `UpdateTimestamps` / `TimestampUpdater`) | **ported.** Records are `key \| ts` (both bounds for DeleteRange); `UpdateTimestamps` rewrites the trailing timestamps in place for the column families whose size callback is non-zero. Compat: the rep is byte-identical to native `rocksdb_writebatch_*_cf_with_ts`. Native batches built with a CF handle carry a placeholder timestamp; the managed batch API takes raw CF ids, so callers pass the timestamp (or use `UpdateTimestamps`). |
| `ColumnFamily.Timestamps.cs`, `RocksDatabase.Timestamps.cs`, `Db/DbImpl.Timestamps.cs` | `DBImpl::Put/Delete/SingleDelete/Merge(..., ts)`, `FailIfCfHasTs`, `FailIfTsMismatchCf`, `FailIfReadCollapsedHistory`, `IncreaseFullHistoryTsLow(Impl)`, `GetFullHistoryTsLow`, `GetNewestUserDefinedTimestamp` | **ported.** Writes without a timestamp to a UDT column family, timestamped writes to other column families and wrong timestamp sizes fail with InvalidArgument (same messages); reads must set `ReadOptions.Timestamp`. `IncreaseFullHistoryTsLow` rejects decreases and writes the MANIFEST edit (TryAgain when a concurrent caller raised it further). Deviation: the collapsed-history check reads the column family's current full_history_ts_low instead of the super version's copy (it only increases, so a read may be rejected slightly earlier, never accepted wrongly). `GetNewestUserDefinedTimestamp` is only defined for non-persisted timestamps (NotSupported for persisted ones; see "User-defined timestamps in memtable only"). |
| `Db/DbImpl.Write.cs` (MemTableInserter, WAL), `Wal/LogWriter.cs`, `Db/DbImpl.Open.cs` | `MemTableInserter::DeleteCF`, `DBImpl::WriteToWAL` (`MaybeAddUserDefinedTimestampSizeRecord`), `util/udt_util.cc` | **ported.** Deletes in UDT column families become `kTypeDeletionWithTimestamp`. Each WAL records the timestamp sizes of the live UDT column families before the first batch that needs them (allocation-free check per write). Recovery compares the recorded sizes with the running ones (AllRunningColumnFamiliesConsistent / CheckWriteBatchTimestampSizeConsistency): consistent WALs replay as is; batches of column families whose timestamp size changed (UDT toggled in memtable only) are reconciled, see "User-defined timestamps in memtable only". |
| `Db/DbImpl.Read.cs`, `Db/DbImpl.MultiGet.cs`, `Db/GetContext.cs`, `Db/MemTable.cs` | `DBImpl::GetImpl`, `Version::Get` / `FilePicker`, `GetContext::SaveValue` (timestamp_, ts_from_rangetombstone_, SetTimestampFromRangeTombstone), `MemTable::Get` / `SaveValue` | **ported.** The lookup key is `key \| read_ts`; entries of the same key are matched with `EqualWithoutTimestamp`, file ranges are checked with `CompareWithoutTimestamp`. The timestamp of the first matched entry (also a deletion) is returned by `TryGet(options, key, value, timestamp)`; a covering range tombstone reports its own timestamp unless a newer point entry is found. Timestamp validation happens after the super version is referenced (audit rule). Timestamped `MultiGet` looks keys up one at a time on one super version/sequence (the batched table lookup is not timestamp-aware); the async forms read table blocks asynchronously (see below). **Fixed:** entries newer than the read snapshot are skipped (older timestamps sort after the lookup key whatever their seqno); previously a point lookup with a snapshot returned keys written after it (native 11.12 does not, compat test `RangeDeletionWithTimestampAndSnapshotsMatchesNative`). |
| `Db/MemTableRangeTombstones.cs` (`FragmentedRangeTombstoneList`), `Db/RangeDelAggregator.cs`, `Db/FlushJob.cs`, `Db/Compaction/CompactionJob.cs` (AddRangeDels), `CompactionIterator.cs`, `Db/Iterators/DbIter.cs`, `DbIteratorFactory.cs` | `db/range_tombstone_fragmenter.cc` (FragmentTombstones with ts, SetMaxVisibleSeqAndTimestamp), `db/range_del_aggregator.{h,cc}` (ts_upper_bound_), `CompactionOutputs::AddRangeDels` (consider_drop with full_history_ts_low, SetMaxSeqAndTs), `CompactionIterator::NextFromInput` (range-del drop only below history_ts_low), `RangeTombstone(sk, ek, seq, ts)`, `MergingIterator` | **ported** (range deletions with timestamps). Tombstones are `start \| ts` -> `end \| ts`; fragments are cut on the keys without timestamps and carry the maximum timestamp at both boundaries (like upstream), so user-key comparisons against keys with timestamps behave like CompareWithoutTimestamp. The timestamps of a fragment are kept next to its seqnos, both sorted newest first independently (as upstream). Readers bound the visible tombstones by the read timestamp (Get, MultiGet, iterators); compactions by full_history_ts_low (keys are only dropped by a tombstone when both are below it; tombstones below it are dropped at the bottommost level); every tombstone is kept per snapshot stripe (no stripe dedup with timestamps). Flush and compaction write each tombstone with its own timestamp (the file boundary end keeps the maximum timestamp). Iterators drop a covered entry individually (like the MergingIterator) instead of skipping the rest of the key, since an older timestamp can have a higher seqno. New API: `DeleteRange(begin, end, ts)` (+ options/async) on `ColumnFamily` / `RocksDatabase`. Compat: native DBs with timestamped range tombstones (WAL, flushed, compacted with and without full_history_ts_low) read identically by managed (Get with timestamps, scans both ways, seeks, iter_start_ts) and after managed flush/compaction by native, and vice versa (`NativeRangeDeletionsWithTimestampsReadIdenticallyBothWays`, `ManagedRangeDeletionsWithTimestampsReadIdenticallyInNative`). Not ported: merge chains whose base value is covered stop like a deletion (upstream drops the covered entry and continues; only differs when timestamps and seqnos are ordered differently). |
| `Db/Iterators/DbIter.cs`, `Iterator.cs`, `LevelIterator.cs` | `db/db_iter.cc` (IsVisible, CompareKeyForSkip, timestamp_ub_/lb_, saved_timestamp_, AppendInternalKeyWithDifferentTimestamp seeks), `DBIter::key()/timestamp()` | **ported** (forward, reverse, reseek and seek-based paths compared line by line). Seek targets and bounds carry no timestamp; seeks use `target \| ts_ub`, SeekForPrev `target \| (ts_lb or min ts)`, reseeks min/max timestamps. `Iterator.Key` never includes the timestamp; `Iterator.Timestamp` returns it; with `IterStartTs` every version in [IterStartTs, Timestamp] is returned, deletions included, and `Iterator.InternalKey` holds upstream's `key()` (internal key). Compat: identical forward/reverse/seek/iter_start_ts results to native on native- and managed-written DBs. |
| `Table/BlockBasedTableBuilder.cs`, `FilterBlockBuilder.cs`, `BlockBasedTableReader.cs`, `Db/FlushJob.cs`, `Compaction/CompactionJob.cs`, `ExternalSstFileIngestionJob.cs` | `BlockBasedTableBuilder::Add` (ExtractUserKeyAndStripTimestamp), `PartitionedFilterBlockBuilder::CutAFilterBlock` (AppendKeyWithMinTimestamp), `TimestampTablePropertiesCollector`, `ExtractTimestampFromTableProperties`, `FullFilterKeyMayMatch` / `PrefixRangeMayMatch`, `UseCommonPrefixForDataBlock` | **ported.** Filters (full, partitioned, prefix) hold user keys without timestamps; decoupled filter partition keys carry the minimum timestamp; `rocksdb.timestamp_min` / `rocksdb.timestamp_max` are written (and land in FileMetaData / MANIFEST NewFile4 custom tags); fv8 common-prefix encoding is off with timestamps. Point lookups use a binary-search Seek instead of the data block hash index (keyed by the user key with its timestamp). Not ported: the table-level TimestampMayMatch skip (an optimization). |
| `Db/Compaction/CompactionIterator.cs`, `Db/MergeHelper.cs`, `Compaction.cs`, `VersionStorageInfo.Compaction.cs`, `LevelCompactionPicker.cs`, `CompactionPicker.cs`, `DbImpl.Compaction.cs`, `VersionSet.cs` | `CompactionIterator::NextFromInput` (UpdateTimestampAndCompareWithFullHistoryLow, cmp_with_history_ts_low_, SingleDelete is_timestamp_eligible_for_gc), `PrepareOutput` (BottommostSeqnoCanBeZeroed + timestamp zeroing), `MergeHelper::MergeUntil` (full_history_ts_low), `KeyNotExistsBeyondOutputLevel`, `sstableKeyCompare`, `GetOverlappingInputs`, `CompactRangeInternal` (CompactRangeOptions::full_history_ts_low) | **ported.** Without full_history_ts_low every timestamp is kept; versions below it are collapsed onto the newest one below it (rule A), deletions/single deletes below it may be dropped, and at the bottommost level the seqno and timestamp are zeroed together. Merge operands of different timestamps are only merged below it. Overlap / clean-cut checks treat all versions of a key as one key. full_history_ts_low is captured when the compaction job starts, persisted in the MANIFEST (also in new-MANIFEST snapshots) and recovered. Compat: native compactions with full_history_ts_low read identically by managed and vice versa. Flush drops nothing (known deviation of the managed flush). |
| `Db/SstFileWriter.cs`, `Db/ExternalSstFileIngestionJob.cs`, `Db/DbImpl.Ingest.cs` | `table/sst_file_writer.cc` (Rep::Add with timestamp, DeleteRangeImpl / DeleteRange(begin, end, ts), "Timestamp size mismatch"), `db/external_sst_file_ingestion_job.cc` (kTypeDeletionWithTimestamp / entity keys, overlapping files and memtable-overlap errors with timestamps, SerializeEndKey), `Version::OverlapWithLevelIterator` (AfterFile / BeforeFile without timestamps) | **ported.** `SstFileWriter.Put(key, ts, value)`, `Delete(key, ts)` (kTypeDeletionWithTimestamp), `DeleteRange(begin, end, ts)`; the untimestamped calls fail with InvalidArgument on a timestamp comparator (and vice versa). Ingestion compares file ranges, level files and memtables without timestamps (an overlap with any version of a key counts; seeks use the maximum timestamp), rejects overlapping files with NotSupported and a memtable overlap with InvalidArgument instead of flushing, as upstream. Compat: `TimestampedSstFilesIngestedBothWays` (managed SST ingested by native, native `rocksdb_sstfilewriter_put/delete_with_ts` SST ingested by managed, identical reads at every timestamp on both sides). Ingestion of files without persisted timestamps: see "User-defined timestamps in memtable only". |

Timestamped `GetAsync` / `MultiGetAsync` are truly asynchronous: memtables are searched synchronously and table files
through `BlockBasedTableReader.GetAsync(ikey, state, visitor, ...)`, the async counterpart of the visitor Get (every
version of the key is visited, data blocks read with async I/O); `TryGetAsync(options, key, value, timestamp)` reports
the timestamp found. **Fixed:** `GetContext.ResolveBlobAsync` looked up blob records of timestamped keys without the
version's timestamp (Corruption: header CRC mismatch). Test: `DbTimestampMemtableOnlyTests.AsyncTimestampedGetAndMultiGetMatchSync`
(no block cache, persisted and memtable-only timestamps, flushed/compacted files, range deletions).

**Not ported:** `TimestampMayMatch` table skipping (an optimization).

### User-defined timestamps in memtable only (persist_user_defined_timestamps = false)

Tests: `tests/RocksDb.Managed.Tests/Db/DbTimestampMemtableOnlyTests.cs` (ports of ColumnFamilyRetainUDTTest,
AutoFlushRetainUDTTest, ManualFlushSkipRetainUDTTest from db/column_family_test.cc, DBWALTestWithTimestamp
RecoverAndNoFlush / RecoverAndFlush / EnableDisableUDT from db/db_wal_test.cc, toggling cases of util/udt_util_test.cc),
compat `TimestampCompatTests.MemtableOnlyTimestampsNativeToManagedAndBack` / `MemtableOnlyTimestampsManagedFirstIdenticalTablesProperties`
(native side: `rocksdb_options_set_persist_user_defined_timestamps(0)` + `allow_concurrent_memtable_write = false`;
flushes, compactions, range deletions and WAL tails written by either side read identically by the other at every
read timestamp, full_history_ts_low equal, every table has `rocksdb.user.defined.timestamps.persisted` = 0).

| Managed file(s) | C++ source(s) | Result |
|---|---|---|
| `Db/ColumnFamilyData.cs` | `ColumnFamilyData::ValidateOptions` | **ported.** NotSupported with atomic_flush, allow_concurrent_memtable_write, or a comparator whose name does not end in ".u64ts" (messages verbatim). |
| `Table/BlockBuilder.cs`, `IndexBuilder.cs`, `FilterBlockBuilder.cs`, `BlockBasedTableBuilder.cs`, `TableFormatOptions.cs` | `BlockBuilder` (strip_ts_sz_, MaybeStripTimestampFromKey, EstimateSizeAfterKV), `ShortenedIndexBuilder` / `PartitionedIndexBuilder` (ShouldUseKeyPlusSeqAsSeparator with CompareWithoutTimestamp, GetFirstInternalKey), `PartitionedFilterBlockBuilder` index, `RangeDelBlockBuilder`, `BlockBasedTableBuilder::Add` (raw_key_size), `TimestampTablePropertiesCollector` gating, `props.user_defined_timestamps_persisted` | **ported.** Every block builder strips the timestamp from the keys it persists (the data block hash index still hashes the key with its timestamp, as upstream); range tombstone end keys are stored without it; no `rocksdb.timestamp_min/max`. The strip costs one predictable branch per `BlockBuilder.Add` on a readonly field. |
| `Table/BlockIter.cs`, `BlockBasedTableReader.cs`, `Db/TableCache.cs`, `RangeDelAggregator.cs` | `BlockIter` pad_min_timestamp_ (UpdateRawKeyAndMaybePadMinTimestamp, TrimAppendWithTimestamp), IndexBlockIter first_internal_key padding, `FragmentedRangeTombstoneList` (tombstone_end_include_ts = false) | **ported.** Padded blocks decode through the generic (slow) entry path into a separate raw-key buffer (delta base) and expose keys with the minimum timestamp; restart keys are padded before comparisons; the hash index and the Prev cache are not used. Blocks of tables with timestamps keep the existing fast path (the fast-path guard is the same single flag test as before). Deviation: whether a table is padded comes from its properties (persisted = 0, or written by "&lt;name&gt;" and read by "&lt;name&gt;.u64ts") instead of FileMetaData::user_defined_timestamps_persisted; both agree for every file the toggling rules accept. |
| `Db/MemTable.cs`, `MemTableSupport.cs` | `MemTable::MaybeUpdateNewestUDT` / `GetNewestUDT` | **ported** (tracked only in this mode; a lock instead of the CAS loop, since concurrent memtable writes are disallowed). |
| `Db/FlushJob.cs`, `Db/DbImpl.Flush.cs`, `Db/DbImpl.Open.cs` | `FlushJob::WriteLevel0Table` (TimestampStrippingIterator, NewTimestampStrippingRangeTombstoneIterator), `GetEffectiveCutoffUDTForPickedMemTables`, `MaybeIncreaseFullHistoryTsLowToAboveCutoffUDT`, `GetFullHistoryTsLowFromU64CutoffTs`, `DBImpl::ShouldRescheduleFlushRequestToRetainUDT`, `ColumnFamilyData::ShouldPostponeFlushToRetainUDT` / `SetFlushSkipReschedule`, `WriteLevel0TableForRecovery` cutoff | **ported.** Memtables are flushed through the min-timestamp view; versions collapsing onto one key are dropped like the flush-time CompactionIterator (rule A per snapshot stripe; merge operands and single deletes keep what follows them) and keys covered by a newer tombstone of the same stripe are dropped (FlushRemovesStaleEntries / RangeDeletionFlushRemovesStaleEntries give upstream's entry counts). full_history_ts_low moves to newest flushed timestamp + 1 (in the flush's own MANIFEST edit; also recovery flushes). Automatic flushes are retried every 100 ms while a picked memtable holds timestamps &gt;= full_history_ts_low, unless writes would stall; manual flushes are never postponed. |
| `Db/Manifest.cs`, `Db/VersionSet.cs`, `Db/DbImpl.Comparators.cs` | `VersionEdit::EncodeFileBoundaries`, `VersionEditHandler::MaybeHandleFileBoundariesForNewFiles` (min ts, max ts for range tombstone sentinels, mixed-flag Corruption), `ValidateUserDefinedTimestampsOptions` / `CompareComparator`, cfds_to_mark_no_udt_ | **ported.** **Fixed:** the MANIFEST writer always encoded boundaries with ts_sz 0, so files not persisting timestamps would have kept them in the MANIFEST; edits and new-MANIFEST snapshots now use the column family's timestamp size. Enabling UDT in memtable only on an existing column family marks and pads every file; disabling removes the padding added by the replay. Other comparator / persist-flag changes fail with upstream's InvalidArgument messages. |
| `Db/DbImpl.Timestamps.cs` | `HandleWriteBatchTimestampSizeDifference`, `TimestampRecoveryHandler` (kReconcileInconsistency) | **ported.** WAL batches of column families whose timestamp size changed are rebuilt with keys padded with the minimum timestamp or stripped; a different non-zero size is unrecoverable (InvalidArgument). Log data is dropped from rebuilt batches, as upstream. |
| `Db/SstFileWriter.cs`, `Db/ExternalSstFileIngestionJob.cs`, `Db/Compaction/CompactionJob.cs` | `SstFileWriter::Rep` strip_timestamp, `ExternalSstFileIngestionJob::GetIngestedFileInfo` (mark_sst_file_has_no_udt, padded boundaries), compaction output `user_defined_timestamps_persisted` | **ported.** SstFileWriter accepts only the minimum timestamp ("persist_user_defined_timestamps flag is set to false, only minimum timestamp is accepted."), writes stripped files and reports keys without timestamps; ingestion accepts stripped files and files written without timestamps into such column families and rejects them elsewhere. Compaction outputs strip as well. |
| `ColumnFamily.Timestamps.cs`, `Db/DbImpl.Timestamps.cs` | `DBImpl::GetNewestUserDefinedTimestamp` | **ported** (mutable memtable, immutable memtables newest first, then full_history_ts_low - 1 when only table files hold data). |

### User-defined timestamps in transactions and WriteBatchWithIndex

Tests: `tests/RocksDb.Managed.Tests/Transactions/TransactionTimestampTests.cs` (ports of key cases of
utilities/transactions/write_committed_transaction_ts_test.cc), compat
`TimestampCompatTests.TimestampedTransactionsRecoverBothWays` (native `rocksdb_transaction_put` +
`rocksdb_transaction_set_commit_timestamp`, plain and 2PC commits left in the WAL, recovered by managed with the
commit timestamps, and managed commits recovered by native).

| Managed file(s) | C++ source(s) | Result |
|---|---|---|
| `Utilities/WriteBatchWithIndex/WriteBatchWithIndex.cs`, `BaseDeltaIterator.cs` | `WriteBatch::Put/Delete/SingleDelete/Merge(cfh, ...)` dummy timestamps (needs_in_place_update_ts_, has_key_with_ts_), `WriteBatchWithIndex::Rep::AddNewEntry` / MakeEntry (index key without ts), `WBWIIteratorImpl::Entry`, `GetFromBatchAndDB` ("Must specify timestamp"), `BaseDeltaIterator::timestamp` | **ported.** Writes to a column family with timestamps append a zero placeholder timestamp to the batch key; the index, lookups and iterators use the key without it. Explicit-timestamp WBWI writes and PutEntity on such column families are NotSupported (upstream: NotSupported / TODO). |
| `Utilities/Transactions/Transaction.cs`, `PessimisticTransaction.cs`, `TransactionOptions.cs`, `TransactionIterator.cs` | `WriteCommittedTxn::SetReadTimestampForValidation`, `SetCommitTimestamp`, `SanityCheckReadTimestamp`, `GetForUpdateImpl`, `CommitWithoutPrepareInternal` / `CommitInternal` (UpdateTimestamps, MarkCommitWithTimestamp, "Must assign a commit timestamp"), `PessimisticTransaction::TryLock` / `ValidateSnapshot` with read_timestamp_, `TransactionDBOptions::enable_udt_validation` | **ported.** GetForUpdate / TryGetForUpdate(Async) with `doValidate` (TransactionBaseImpl::GetForUpdate "If do_validate is false then GetForUpdate with snapshot is not defined.", SanityCheckReadTimestamp "... with read_timestamp is not defined."). `TransactionOptions.WriteBatchTrackTimestampSize` (write_batch_track_timestamp_size, WriteBatch::SetTrackTimestampSize / MaybeTrackTimestampSize / GetColumnFamilyToTimestampSize): timestamp sizes tracked by timestamped writes to `Transaction.WriteBatch` are used first by the commit's UpdateTimestamps; deviation (safer): the fallback is the column family's own timestamp size instead of the WriteBatchWithIndex comparator, so writes bypassing the transaction APIs (WritesBypassTransactionAPIs) are timestamped correctly with the option off too. Not ported: the CommitBatch "timestamp assigned before locking" check. |
| `Utilities/Transactions/OptimisticTransactionDatabase.cs` (UDT) | `include/rocksdb/utilities/transaction.h` (Transaction::SetCommitTimestamp / SetReadTimestampForValidation default `Status::NotSupported("timestamp not supported")`, not overridden by OptimisticTransaction), `db/db_impl/db_impl_write.cc` DBImpl::WriteImpl (`WriteBatchInternal::TimestampsUpdateNeeded` -> `Status::InvalidArgument("write batch must have timestamp(s) set")`) | **upstream parity (no timestamp support).** utilities/transactions/optimistic_transaction*.cc has no timestamp handling, so an optimistic transaction accepts operations on a column family with timestamps (placeholder timestamps in its WriteBatchWithIndex), cannot set a commit timestamp, and a commit with such writes fails with InvalidArgument "write batch must have timestamp(s) set" (raised by the managed commit before the write, the same code and message). Reads with a read timestamp and writes to other column families commit normally. Test: `TransactionTimestampTests.OptimisticTransactionsCannotCommitTimestampedWrites`. |
| `Db/DbImpl.Transactions.cs`, `Db/DbImpl.Write.cs` | `TransactionUtil::CheckKey` (timestamp conflicts with enable_udt_validation), `DBImpl::GetLatestSequenceForKey` (max-ts lookup, timestamp out), `MemTableInserter::MarkCommitWithTimestamp` | **ported.** A newest version with a timestamp above the read timestamp is a conflict (Busy). Recovery assigns the commit timestamp of a `kTypeCommitXIDAndTimestamp` marker to the rebuilt prepared batch before inserting it (previously NotSupported). |

## Integrated BlobDB (blob files)

Ported in `src/RocksDb.Managed/Db/Blob/` with small, isolated hooks in the read paths (`GetContext`, `DbImpl.Read.cs`,
`DbImpl.MultiGet.cs`, `DbIter`, `Iterator.ScanAsync`), flush (`FlushJob`, `DbImpl.Flush.cs`, recovery flush in
`DbImpl.Open.cs`), compaction (`CompactionIterator.PrepareOutput` / `InvokeFilterIfNeeded`, `CompactionJob`,
`MergeHelper.MergeUntil`), the version set (`Version.cs`, `VersionSet.cs`) and obsolete-file deletion (`DbImpl.cs`).
Tests: `tests/RocksDb.Managed.Tests/Blob/` (ports of key cases of blob_file_builder_test.cc, blob_file_reader_test.cc,
db_blob_basic_test.cc, db_blob_compaction_test.cc, db_blob_index_test.cc; 28 cases),
`tests/RocksDb.Managed.CompatTests/Api/BlobCompatTests.cs` (native librocksdb 11.12, blob options through the C API) and
two round-trip scenarios (`FeatureRoundTripTests.BlobDb_BothWays`, native first and managed first).

| Managed file | C++ source(s) | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `Db/Blob/BlobLogFormat.cs` | `db/blob/blob_log_format.{h,cc}` | **ported.** Header (30 bytes), record header (32 bytes, masked crc32c of the first 24 bytes and of key + value), footer (32 bytes, masked crc32c), magic 0x00248f37, version 1, IsValidBlobOffset; every Corruption message. |
| `Db/Blob/BlobIndex.cs` | `db/blob/blob_index.h` | **ported.** kBlob / kBlobTTL / kInlinedTTL encode and decode with the upstream error messages. TTL blob references (stacked BlobDB) raise Corruption when read ("Unexpected TTL blob file"); inlined TTL values are returned as values. |
| `Db/Blob/BlobFileBuilder.cs` | `db/blob/blob_file_builder.cc`, `blob_log_writer.cc`, `util/compression.cc` (LegacyForceBuiltinCompression) | **ported.** Lazy open, min_blob_size (empty values never extracted), blob_file_size rollover, per-blob compression with format-2 framing kept even when larger, footer + fsync on close, BlobFileAddition (no file checksum: empty method/value, as without a checksum factory), prepopulate_blob_cache (flush only). Flushed blob files are **byte-identical** to native for none / snappy / lz4 / zstd (compat test, several files per flush). zlib blobs of any size are written (chunked above 16 KiB, see Compression; readable by native, not byte-identical). |
| `Db/Blob/BlobFileReader.cs` | `db/blob/blob_file_reader.cc` | **ported.** Header/footer validation (magic, version, TTL, column family id, footer CRC, malformed size), offset and compression checks, record + CRC verification when verify_checksums (key, sizes, blob CRC), decompression errors as Corruption, sync and async reads. Not ported: prefetch buffers, multi-blob batched reads (MultiGetBlob reads blob by blob). |
| `Db/Blob/BlobSource.cs` | `db/blob/blob_source.cc`, `blob_file_cache.cc`, `Version::GetBlob` | **ported.** Optional blob cache (`ColumnFamilyOptions.BlobCache`, any `BlockCache`, bottom priority, OffsetableCacheKey(db_id, session, file).WithOffset(offset)), fill_cache, kBlockCacheTier Incomplete, "Invalid blob file number" for files not in the reading version. Blob file readers are bounded by max_open_files like BlobFileCache (capacity `max_open_files - 10`, min 20; least recently used idle readers are closed and reopened on demand; readers in use are reference counted so an eviction never closes one under a read; obsolete files are evicted right away). Deviation: the bound applies to the blob readers of the column family alone, not to table and blob readers together (upstream shares the table cache; the managed table readers of all column families share one cache since the "Recovery, WAL tracking, space and I/O limits" work). Test: `DbBlobTests.BlobReadersBoundedByMaxOpenFiles`. |
| `Db/Blob/BlobFileMetaData.cs`, `Version.cs`, `VersionSet.cs`, `Manifest.cs` | `db/blob/blob_file_meta.{h,cc}`, `db/version_builder.cc` (ApplyBlobFileAddition/Garbage, LinkSst, GetMinOldestBlobFileNumber, SaveBlobFilesTo / AddBlobFileIfNeeded), `VersionStorageInfo::GetBlobStats` | **ported.** Blob files are part of every version (linked SSTs from oldest_blob_file_number, garbage counters); blob files older than the oldest one with linked SSTs, and unlinked fully-garbage files, are dropped exactly like upstream; duplicate additions, unknown files and garbage overflow raise Corruption. New MANIFESTs (WriteCurrentStateToManifest) carry the blob files. **Fixed (high):** previously a managed MANIFEST rewrite of a native blob DB dropped all blob file records. |
| Obsolete blob files (`VersionSet`, `DbImpl.PurgeObsoleteFiles`, full scan) | SharedBlobFileMetaData deleter, `VersionSet::AddObsoleteBlobFile`, `FindObsoleteFiles` | **ported.** Reference counted per version like table files: a blob file is deleted (and its reader evicted) only when no live version (super version, iterator, compaction input) references it; pending blob outputs of running jobs are protected from the open-time scan; failed jobs delete their blob files unless their MANIFEST write was attempted. |
| `Db/Blob/CompactionBlobProcessor.cs`, `CompactionIterator`, `CompactionJob` | `compaction_iterator.cc` (ExtractLargeValueIfNeeded, GarbageCollectBlobIfNeeded, ComputeBlobGarbageCollectionCutoffFileNumber, blob values for the compaction filter), `blob_fetcher.cc`, `compaction_job.cc` | **ported.** Extraction for outputs at levels >= blob_file_starting_level, relocation of blobs older than the age cutoff (CompactRangeOptions blob_garbage_collection_policy / age cutoff overrides), compaction filters on blob values (the blob is read; FilterBlobByKey is not ported), merges onto blob base values in compaction. |
| `Db/Blob/BlobGarbageMeter.cs` | `db/blob/blob_garbage_meter.cc`, `blob_counting_iterator.h` | **ported.** Inflow over every positioned input entry, outflow over outputs, BlobFileGarbage per preexisting file; the garbage recorded by managed and native for the same compaction feeds identical metadata (compat test: native GC on managed garbage and vice versa). |
| Forced GC (`VersionStorageInfo.Compaction.cs`, `LevelCompactionPicker`) | `VersionStorageInfo::ComputeFilesMarkedForForcedBlobGC`, `LevelCompactionBuilder` (kForcedBlobGC) | **ported** for leveled compaction. Universal and FIFO need nothing: upstream 11.12/11.14 only consumes the marks in the leveled picker (`compaction_picker_level.cc` is the only picker referencing FilesMarkedForForcedBlobGC / kForcedBlobGC), so this is parity, not a gap. |
| Read paths (`GetContext`, `DbImpl.Read`, `DbImpl.MultiGet`, `DbIter`) | `GetContext::SaveValue` / GetBlobValue, `DBIter::SetValueAndColumnsFromBlob` | **ported.** Blob indexes (from tables or the memtable) are resolved while the super version is referenced (the audit rule: super version before sequence still holds; blob files are pinned by the same version). Merges onto blob base values in Get, MultiGet, iterators (both directions, the seek-based reverse path) and compaction. Iterators read the blob lazily on the first `Value` access (allow_unprepared_value semantics; `PrepareValueAsync` and `ScanAsync` read it with async I/O). Known deviation: an iterator positioned on a blob whose read fails throws from `Value` instead of being invalidated during positioning. KeyMayExist does not read blobs (value_found = false). |
| WAL replay, WriteBatch, replication, checkpoint | `MemTableInserter::PutBlobIndexCF`, `DBImpl::GetLiveFiles`, `GetLiveFilesStorageInfo` | **ported.** kTypeBlobIndex WAL records (blob direct write / legacy) are replayed into the memtable and flushed as blob indexes; GetLiveFiles and checkpoints include the blob files of the current versions; GetUpdatesSince returns PutBlobIndex records unchanged. |
| Properties and statistics | `internal_stats.cc` (num-blob-files, blob-stats, total-blob-file-size, live-blob-file-size, live-blob-file-garbage-size, blob-cache-*) | **ported.** Flush and compaction write bytes include blob files (bytes_written_blob). Tickers: BLOB_DB_BLOB_FILE_BYTES_WRITTEN/READ, BLOB_DB_BLOB_FILE_SYNCED, BLOB_DB_CACHE_*. Histograms (later pass): BLOB_DB_BLOB_FILE_READ/WRITE/SYNC_MICROS, BLOB_DB_COMPRESSION/DECOMPRESSION_MICROS and the CF's blob file read latency histogram. Not ported: perf context, the blob fields of compaction/flush job info. |
| Options | `options/cf_options.cc` | **ported** (already persisted): enable_blob_files, min_blob_size, blob_file_size, blob_compression_type, enable_blob_garbage_collection, blob_garbage_collection_age_cutoff / force_threshold, blob_compaction_readahead_size (recorded only, no readahead), blob_file_starting_level, prepopulate_blob_cache; `BlobCache` (not serialized, like upstream). Also persisted now: blob_file_writable_file_max_buffer_size, enable_blob_direct_write, blob_direct_write_partitions (see "Blob direct write"). Not ported: blob_compression_opts (default level). |
| `Db/Wide/WideColumnSerialization.cs`, `EntityColumnList.cs` | `db/wide/wide_column_serialization.{h,cc}` (SerializeV2Impl, DeserializeV2Impl, Deserialize with/without blob_columns, HasBlobColumns, ForEachBlobFileNumber, ResolveEntityBlobColumns, ResolveEntityForMerge) | **ported.** Version 2 layout (header, skip info, column types kTypeValue / kTypeBlobIndex, name sizes, value sizes, names, values) byte-identical; version 2 only when a column is blob-backed. Every Corruption / InvalidArgument message copied (recursive entity types, empty / undecodable blob indexes, out-of-order names, section size mismatch, trailing data). Consumers of resolved entities (`WideColumns`, `GetValueOfDefaultColumn`) report a blob reference as Corruption ("Wide column blob reference in a context where it is not expected."). |
| Entity blob columns on the read paths (`GetContext`, `DbIter`, `MultiGetBatch`, `MergeHelper`) | `GetContext::SaveValue` (ResolveEntityBlobColumns), `DBIter::SetValueAndColumnsFromEntity`, `MergeHelper::TimedFullMergeWithEntity` (ResolveEntityForMerge) | **ported.** Get / GetEntity / MultiGet / iterators (both directions, async Get) resolve blob-backed columns while the super version is referenced; merges onto a V2 base resolve it first. Deviations: the whole entity is resolved (upstream's GetValueOfDefaultColumn fast path reads only the default column's blob for a plain Get), async reads resolve entity blob columns with synchronous I/O, the lazy entity API and FilterV4 are ported separately (see the lazy wide columns and compaction filter rows). **Fixed:** MultiGet returned the serialized entity instead of its default column. |
| `Db/Blob/EntityBlobRewriter.cs`, `CompactionIterator.PrepareOutput`, `FlushJob` | `CompactionIterator::ExtractLargeColumnValuesIfNeeded`, `GarbageCollectEntityBlobsIfNeeded` (FetchBlobsNeedingGC, RelocateBlobValues, SerializeEntityAfterGC) | **ported.** Flush and compaction outputs at levels >= blob_file_starting_level move non-empty columns of at least min_blob_size bytes to blob files (entities that already have blob columns are left alone, upstream's simplification); blob GC relocates the columns below the cutoff (inlined when the output gets no blob files; version 1 when no blob column is left). |
| `BlobGarbageMeter`, `FlushJob.UpdateOldestBlobFileNumberForEntity` | `BlobGarbageMeter::ProcessEntityBlobReferences`, `FileMetaData::UpdateBoundaries` (ForEachBlobFileNumber) | **ported.** Entity blob references count as inflow/outflow and set oldest_blob_file_number; native GC of managed entity garbage and vice versa is compat-tested. |
| `CompactionFilter.FilterV3` / `FilterV4` / `FilterBlobByKey`, `Db/Blob/CompactionBlobResolver.cs`, `CompactionIterator.InvokeFilterIfNeeded`, `MergeHelper.FilterMerge` | `CompactionFilter::FilterV3` (default: kKeep for entities) / `FilterV4` / `SupportsFilterV4` / `FilterBlobByKey`, `CompactionBlobResolver`, `CompactionIterator::InvokeFilterIfNeeded`, `MergeHelper::FilterMerge` | **ported.** Plain values (blob values read first) and entities (blob columns resolved eagerly: the FilterV3 compatibility path of FilterV4) reach `FilterV3`; kChangeWideColumnEntity (sorted, serialized V1, large columns separated again by PrepareOutput), kChangeValue on entities, kRemove / kPurge / kRemoveAndSkipUntil; kChangeBlobIndex / kIOError / kUndetermined fail with upstream's NotSupported messages. **FilterV4** (default: FilterV3) is the entry point for every value type, including merge operands (MergeHelper::FilterMerge); with `SupportsFilterV4` an entity with blob columns gets a `WideColumnBlobResolver` (`CompactionBlobResolver`: blob columns carry their blob index, resolved on demand and cached per entity in separate pooled buffers so earlier spans stay valid, sticky first error fails the compaction even if the filter returned kKeep). **FilterBlobByKey** runs first for blob values; kUndetermined reads the blob and continues with FilterV4 as kValue; a kChangeWideColumnEntity from it yields an entity without columns like upstream. Compat: a FilterV4 filter rewrites/removes native-written V2 entities and native reads the result. |

Compat (native librocksdb 11.12): a native blob DB (two CFs, merges, deletes, several blob files per flush, compaction
with forced GC, a WAL-only tail) is read by managed (Get, async Get, MultiGet, both iteration directions), extended,
compacted with GC and handed back to native, which verifies and compacts again; a managed blob DB (garbage recorded
without GC) is read and garbage-collected by native and read back; flushed blob files are byte-identical for none,
snappy, lz4 and zstd. Round trip: `BlobDb_BothWays` (generated op streams, blob CFs with and without compression/GC,
compactions, checkpoint, reopen) passes with either engine first.

Entity blob columns: `tests/RocksDb.Managed.Tests/Blob/EntityBlobTests.cs` (ports of wide_column_serialization_test.cc
V2LayoutStructureVerification / V2BlobColumnRejectsDeserialize / DeserializeV2RejectsRecursiveType / SerializeV2Errors /
RandomizedSerializeDeserializeRoundTrip and db_wide_basic_test.cc EntityBlobAfterFlush / EntityBlobAfterCompaction /
EntityBlobReverseIteration / MergeEntityWithBlobColumns(NoDefault) / AllBlobColumnsEntity / EmptyEntityWithBlobOptions /
CompactionFilter / CompactionFilterWithBlobGC) and `tests/RocksDb.Managed.CompatTests/Api/EntityBlobCompatTests.cs`:
librocksdb 11.12 separates entity columns at flush, so native-written V2 entities are read, merged, garbage collected and
extended by managed and handed back to native (which GCs again), and managed-written V2 entities (with managed garbage
metadata) are read, merged onto and garbage collected by native.

Remaining risks: blob reads in the async Get/MultiGet/scan paths complete synchronously on a blob-cache hit and use
async file reads otherwise, but iterators resolving blobs through `Value` do synchronous I/O.

### Blob direct write

`enable_blob_direct_write` exists in librocksdb 11.12 (the C API has `rocksdb_options_set_enable_blob_direct_write` /
`_blob_direct_write_partitions`; HISTORY.md first mentions blob direct write fixes in 11.2), so the feature is
compat-tested against native. It is off by default; a DB using it needs a RocksDB release with blob direct write (blob
indexes in the WAL / memtable, V2 entities written at write time and the OPTIONS entries).

| Managed file | C++ source(s) | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `Db/Blob/BlobDirectWriteManager.cs` | `db/blob/blob_file_partition_manager.{h,cc}` (WriteBlob, OpenNewBlobFile, SealActiveBlobFile / FinalizeBlobFile, RotateCurrentGeneration, PrepareFlushAdditions, SyncAllOpenFiles, ResolveBlobDirectWriteIndex, RoundRobinBlobFilePartitionStrategy) | **ported.** Partition per blob from the strategy (`% partitions`, entity partition chosen once per PutEntity), rollover at blob_file_size and when the compression changes, records flushed to the OS one by one (kDoFlushEachRecord), footer + sync when sealed, generations per memtable. Reads of files the reading version does not know fall back to a footer-less reader whose visible size follows the writes (BlobFileCache footer-skip retry). Deviations: one writer at a time (the write group leader writes the blobs; upstream serializes on a manager mutex too); blob cache prepopulation is not implemented (file checksums of direct-write files: see "Atomic replace range, blob checksums and histograms, backup checksums, scans" below); failed transformed writes are not tracked with MarkBlobWriteAsGarbage: the flush computes the garbage of each registered file as the blobs its output does not reference, which also covers upstream's "flush-time filtering and overwrite elision" garbage (11.2 fix). |
| `Db/Blob/BlobWriteBatchTransformer.cs`, `DbImpl.Write.cs` (leader) | `db/blob/blob_write_batch_transformer.cc`, `DBImpl::MaybeTransformBatchForBlobDirectWrite`, `SyncBlobDirectWriteManagers` | **ported.** Done by the write group leader after PreprocessWrite (so the blobs belong to the memtable the batch goes to): Put values >= min_blob_size become PutBlobIndex records, PutEntity columns >= min_blob_size become blob-backed V2 columns, everything else (TimedPut, deletes, merges, PutBlobIndex, log data) is copied; batches with merge operands are not transformed (upstream rule); pre-serialized entities with blob references fail ("Blob direct write does not support pre-serialized wide entities with blob references"); a failing writer is excluded from the group; open blob files are synced before a sync WAL write. Deviation: transactional writes (TransactionWriteContext: 2PC prepare/commit, optimistic/pessimistic commits) are not transformed; their values are separated by the flush as without direct write. |
| `DbImpl.Write.cs` (SwitchMemtable), `DbImpl.Flush.cs`, `DbImpl.BlobDirectWrite.cs`, `DbImpl.cs` | `DBImpl::SwitchMemtable` (RotateCurrentGeneration), `FlushJob` (PrepareFlushAdditions + additions/garbage in the flush edit), `CommitFlushedGenerations`, `Protect/UnprotectSealedBlobFileNumbers`, `CancelAllBackgroundWork` (force_flush_for_blob_direct_write) | **ported.** Files stay pending outputs from creation until the memtable referencing them is disposed (then files no version references are deleted), so reads of old super versions keep working; the shutdown flush runs even with avoid_flush_during_shutdown. After a crash, the blob indexes recovered from the WAL are read from the blob files no flush registered (ResolveBlobDirectWriteIndex opens any file the version does not know), and footer-less blob files survive obsolete-file purges (ShouldKeepFooterlessBlobFile), like upstream; see "WAL recycling and the WAL matrix". |
| `ColumnFamilyData`, `DbImpl.Open.cs` (SanitizeCfOptions), `Options/ColumnFamilyOptions.cs`, `Options/BlobDirectWritePartitionStrategy.cs` | `ColumnFamilyData::ValidateOptions`, `SanitizeOptions`, `cf_options.cc` (enable_blob_direct_write, blob_direct_write_partitions, blob_direct_write_partition_strategy kDontSerialize, mutable blob_file_writable_file_max_buffer_size), `include/rocksdb/blob_file_partition_strategy.h` | **ported.** NotSupported (messages verbatim) with pipelined writes, allow_concurrent_memtable_write (default true, as upstream), unordered writes, two write queues, MemPurge and user-defined timestamps; without enable_blob_files the option is sanitized to false; 0 partitions = 1. The options are typed and persisted (native parses managed OPTIONS files with them). blob_file_writable_file_max_buffer_size also applies to the flush/compaction blob builders. |

Tests: `tests/RocksDb.Managed.Tests/Blob/BlobDirectWriteTests.cs` (ports of db_blob_direct_write_test.cc
DirectWriteDefaultRoundRobinSpreadsWriteBatchAcrossPartitionsBeforeAndAfterFlush, DirectWriteCustomPartitionStrategy*,
DirectWriteCloseFlushesWhenShutdownFlushIsDisabled, DirectWriteAutoFlushPreservesBlobGenerationOrder,
DirectWriteRejectsUnsupportedWriteModes / MemPurge, DirectWriteIteratorMixedValuesBeforeAndAfterFlush,
DirectWriteBlobFileRotationSinglePartition, DirectWriteBoundaryValues, DirectWriteWriteBatchNoQualifyingValues,
DirectWriteDeleteAndReput, DirectWriteSnapshotIsolation and db_wide_blob_direct_write_test.cc
DirectWriteWideEntityBeforeAndAfterFlush; plus flush garbage accounting with GC, sync writes with compression, async
reads and OPTIONS round trip) and `tests/RocksDb.Managed.CompatTests/Api/BlobDirectWriteCompatTests.cs` (native
direct-write DB with 3 partitions and an entity read and extended by managed with direct write, reread and compacted by
native; managed direct-write DB with flushes, compaction and sync writes read and extended by native; native
rocksdb_load_latest_options parses the managed OPTIONS file).

## Audit #3: remaining files

Audit #3 closes the coverage gaps of audits #1 and #2. Every managed source file is now listed in
[validation-coverage.md](validation-coverage.md) with its C++ source(s) and the audit that covered it. Files with no
C++ counterpart were design-reviewed instead. These include the I/O layer, pooling and the replication layer ported
from rocksdb-sharp. Base revision: `origin/claude/rocksdb-csharp-dotnet-port-w9bqfg` at 57698f8. Regression tests are
in `tests/RocksDb.Managed.Tests/Validation/Audit3RegressionTests.cs` unless noted.

### Fixed

| Area | C++ source | Finding |
|---|---|---|
| FIFO TTL compaction (`FifoCompactionPicker`, `FlushJob`, `CompactionJob`, `Compaction`, `TableCache`, `FileMetaData`) | `FIFOCompactionPicker::PickTTLCompaction` (`FileMetaData::TryGetNewestKeyTime`), `FlushJob::WriteLevel0Table` (`newest_key_time = current_time`), `CompactionJob::OpenCompactionOutputFile` (`MaxInputFileNewestKeyTime`) | **fixed (medium: data dropped early).** The picker expired a file by its oldest ancestor time. Upstream expires it by its newest key time. An intra-L0 compaction output (FIFO `allow_compaction`) carries the oldest input's ancestor time, so it was dropped as soon as its oldest input expired, together with keys younger than the TTL. Native-flushed files were also expired up to one memtable lifetime early. The port also wrote `rocksdb.newest.key.time` = 0 in every table. Flush now writes the flush time, compaction writes the maximum over its inputs, and the table cache records the property when it opens a file. DB open opens the L0 readers of FIFO column families with a TTL, as upstream pins every reader with `max_open_files = -1`. Tests: `FlushAndCompactionRecordNewestKeyTime`, `CompactionPickerTests.FifoTtlUsesNewestKeyTime`. |
| WAL recovery (`DbImpl.Open`, `DbImpl.ReadOnly`) | `DBImpl::InitializeLogReader` ("We intentionally make log::Reader do checksumming even if paranoid_checks==false") | **fixed (medium: corruption propagated).** The WAL reader verified checksums only with `paranoid_checks`. With `paranoid_checks = false` a corrupted record was replayed into the memtable. A flipped byte inside a value was returned by Get after reopen. Recovery now always verifies record checksums, in the primary and in read-only opens. Test: `WalRecoveryVerifiesChecksumsWithoutParanoidChecks`. |
| `compaction_options_fifo` / `compaction_options_universal` (`CompactionStyleOptions`, `ColumnFamilyOptions`, `DbImpl.Options`) | `options/cf_options.cc` (`fifo_compaction_options_type_info`, `universal_compaction_options_type_info`, both mutable) | **fixed (low/medium: missing behavior).** Neither struct was written to the OPTIONS file, and SetOptions rejected both as unrecognized. Typed settings were lost when options were loaded from an OPTIONS file. They are now struct options: typed members `max_table_files_size` and `allow_compaction`; `size_ratio`, `min_merge_width`, `max_merge_width`, `max_size_amplification_percent`, `compression_size_percent`, `stop_style` and `allow_trivial_move`. Other members are preserved verbatim. A single scalar sets `max_table_files_size`, as upstream. Both are mutable. Test: `CompactionStyleOptionsArePersistedAndMutable`. `OptionsFileTests` now expects the typed struct. |
| Blob GC option validation (`DbImpl.Open`, `DbImpl.Options`) | `ColumnFamilyData::ValidateOptions` | **fixed (low).** At open and CreateColumnFamily, an age cutoff or force threshold outside [0, 1] was accepted. SetOptions rejected it even with garbage collection disabled. Both now follow upstream: InvalidArgument only when `enable_blob_garbage_collection` is set. Test: `OpenValidatesBlobGarbageCollectionRanges`. |
| TTL database (`DbWithTtl.cs`) | `DBWithTTLImpl::IsStale` (`int64_t timestamp_value = DecodeFixed32(...)`) | **fixed (low, year 2038).** The 4-byte write-time suffix was decoded as a signed int32. Values written after January 2038 (suffix >= 2^31) counted as stale and compactions dropped them. The suffix is now zero-extended like upstream. `SanityCheckTimestamp` keeps upstream's signed comparison. Test: `TtlIsStaleZeroExtendsTheTimestampSuffix`. |

### Verified (line by line in this audit)

| Managed file | C++ source(s) | Result |
|---|---|---|
| `Db/Compaction/CompactionIterator.cs` | `compaction_iterator.cc` (NextFromInput, NextImpl, InvokeFilterIfNeeded, PrepareOutput, findEarliestVisibleSnapshot, `BottommostSeqnoCanBeZeroed`) | verified: rule (A), obsolete deletion drop with KeyNotExistsBeyondOutputLevel, bottommost delete skipping per snapshot stripe, SingleDelete (all five sub-cases, `enforce_single_del_contracts` Corruption, the "clear and output next key" optimization), TimedPut preferred-seqno swap with both range-tombstone checks, merge output and `MergeInProgress` reset, filter decisions (RemoveAndSkipUntil before the current key degrades to Keep; Remove/Purge/ChangeValue type changes), UDT `cmp_with_history_ts_low` gating and seqno+timestamp zeroing. Deviations (existing, harmless): no range-deletion sentinel keys, no snapshot checker (WritePrepared work stream), entities are not passed to compaction filters (upstream's default FilterV3 keeps them too), `ChangeWideColumnEntity` is NotSupported. |
| `Db/Compaction/CompactionJob.cs` (ShouldStopBefore, UpdateGrandparentBoundaryInfo), `Compaction.cs` (max output size) | `compaction_outputs.cc`, `compaction.cc` | verified, including the 2x max output size above the bottommost level. The cuts that were not ported (TTL, SST partitioner, round-robin split key, `target_file_size_is_upper_bound`) were added afterwards, see "Compaction features". |
| `Db/Compaction/FifoCompactionPicker.cs` | `compaction_picker_fifo.cc` | verified after the fix above (TTL, size, cost-based intra-L0, CompactRange). Deviation: a file whose newest key time and ancestor time are both unknown is kept, where upstream drops a file whose reader is not pinned. `max_data_files_size`, kv-ratio compaction and temperature change were added afterwards, see "Compaction features". |
| `Db/Compaction/UniversalCompactionPicker.cs` | `compaction_picker_universal.cc` | verified: sorted runs, size amplification (the newest runs up to one being compacted), size-ratio and sorted-run-count picking (min/max merge width, stop styles), output level choice, overlap refusal, trivial move. The deviations found then (compression_size_percent, periodic / delete-triggered compaction, incremental mode, max_read_amp, MightExcludeNewL0sToReduceWriteStop, marked files) were fixed afterwards, see "Compaction features". |
| `Cache/LruCache.cs` | `lru_cache.{h,cc}` | verified: Lookup/Release/Erase/InsertItem ref-count and usage transitions, MemoryLimit and evict-on-insert without handle, the three-pool LRU (LRU_Insert, MaintainPoolSize, LRU_Remove), table resize, capacity changes. The secondary cache hooks (eviction callback, standalone handles) were added later, see "Cache layers". The metadata charge now follows CalcuMetaCharge, see "Compaction features". |
| `Cache/HyperClockCache.cs` | `clock_cache.{h,cc}` (FixedHyperClockTable, BaseClockTable) | verified: meta word (30-bit acquire/release counters, hit bit, state bits renumbered consistently), optimistic Lookup, TryInsert with "already matches" boosting, displacement rollback, ClockUpdate countdown and ownership CAS, Evict (step 4, `kMaxCountdown << length_bits` bound), strict and non-strict charging with the occupancy limit (0.84), standalone entries, Release with erase-if-last-ref, Erase, EraseUnRefEntries, CalcHashBits. Deviations: Erase keeps probing after a match (no effect); a non-strict insert that only needs a free slot requests one freed entry instead of one probe step. The eviction effort cap and the CalcHashBits clamp were added afterwards, see "Compaction features". |
| `Db/TransactionLogIterator.cs` | `db/wal_iterator_impl.cc` (renamed from `transaction_log_impl.cc`) | verified. Existing managed extensions: it follows WAL rotation instead of returning TryAgain, and stays on the start batch instead of skipping it. The gap handling was corrected afterwards (this row first assumed 11.12 still re-seeks; it does not), see "Compaction features". |
| `Db/DbImpl.ReadOnly.cs` | `db_impl_readonly.cc`, `db_impl_secondary.cc` | verified after the checksum fix. The secondary uses a different but equivalent design: a full MANIFEST re-read, and memtables rebuilt when a log number advances, filtered by per-CF log number. Upstream skips batches below the oldest L0 file's largest seqno and seals memtables. Re-inserting an entry that is already in a table file is harmless, since key, seqno and value are identical. |
| `Db/ErrorHandler.cs` (severity classification) | `error_handler.cc` (ErrorSeverityMap, DefaultErrorSeverityMap, DefaultReasonMap) | verified entry by entry. |
| `Db/Blob/BlobIndex.cs`, `BlobLogFormat.cs` | `blob_index.h`, `blob_log_format.cc` | re-verified constants, field order, CRC coverage and error messages. |
| `Db/DbWithTtl.cs` | `utilities/ttl/db_ttl_impl.{h,cc}` | verified after the fix (kTSLength 4, kMinTimestamp, SanityCheckTimestamp, StripTS, filter re-appending the timestamp). TtlMergeOperator, Merge and column families: see "TTL merges, merge failure scope, FullMergeV3, transaction APIs". |
| `Options/*` defaults | `options.h`, `advanced_options.h`, `table.h`, `universal_compaction.h`, `transaction_db.h`, `optimistic_transaction_db.h`, `backup_engine.h` | verified: DB, CF, table, compression, read/write/flush/compact-range, FIFO/universal, transaction DB / transaction / optimistic, and backup engine defaults all match upstream. |
| `Env/*` | `env/io_posix.cc`, `env/fs_posix.cc`, `file/file_prefetch_buffer.cc` | design review: rename is rename(2) with overwrite (atomic). Sync is flush plus fdatasync; directory fsync on new files and renames is done by the DB layer. Preallocated or direct-I/O padded files are truncated to their logical size on close. The LOCK uses OFD locks that conflict with native fcntl locks. FilePrefetchBuffer is a lite port: one buffer, doubling readahead. `LocalRandomAccessFile.Prefetch` is PosixRandomAccessFile::Prefetch (readahead(2) on Linux, not supported with direct I/O); `IRandomAccessFile.Prefetch` defaults to not supported. `AsyncPrefetchBuffer` ports its async_io two-buffer mode (PrefetchAsync of the next chunk while the current one serves reads), used for compaction inputs (`CompactionJob.NoFillTableCache`, compaction_readahead_size, BlockPrefetcher) only when the file does not support Prefetch; deviation: the chunk boundaries follow the previous read's end and a block straddling two chunks is stitched through a reserved gap instead of upstream's overlap buffer. |
| `Env/FileSyncWorkers.cs`, `Env/LocalFileSystem.cs` (`LocalWritableFile.SyncAsync`, `LocalFileSystem.OffloadAsyncSync`) | `env/io_posix.cc` (PosixWritableFile::Sync / Fsync: fdatasync on the calling thread) | **managed-only design.** `SyncAsync` queues the fdatasync to dedicated sync threads (created on demand, at most min(16, cores); a worker polls the queue ~50 us before it blocks) and returns a pooled IValueTaskSource completed with asynchronous continuations, so an async synced write never blocks a thread-pool thread and user continuations never run on a sync thread. Semantics are the blocking call's: the request is queued after the data reached the OS (Flush/FlushAsync first when buffered), the task completes after fdatasync returned and carries its error; sync order is the caller's (each caller awaits). `Sync()` stays inline; `OffloadAsyncSync = false` restores the inline async path. Cost: two thread wake-ups per sync (docs/perf.md). Tests: `FileSystemTests.LocalSyncAsyncOnSyncThreads` (12 files synced concurrently, buffered and flushed tails, closed-file error, both modes), plus every WAL/MANIFEST sync of the unit/compat suites. |
| `src/RocksDb.Managed.Replication*` | rocksdb-sharp `Replication/`, `Switchover/`, `Cluster/` | design review only (no C++ counterpart): WAL tailing goes through `TransactionLogIterator` / `GetUpdatesSince` (audited) and the batches are applied with the regular write path. |

### Remaining deviations found by this audit (not fixed)

The compaction and cache deviations listed here originally (memtable first-insert time, the FIFO and universal picker
features, output cutting, the HyperClock eviction effort cap, TransactionLogIterator gaps) were fixed afterwards; see
"Compaction features (audit #3 remaining deviations)" below for what changed and what still differs.

- Several items are under active change by other work streams and were only reported, not changed:
  `CompactionIterator` snapshot checker (WritePrepared); entity blob columns (filtering, extraction, GC: upstream
  PrepareOutput now handles `kTypeWideColumnEntity` with blob columns).

## Compaction features (audit #3 remaining deviations)

Ports of the compaction features audit #3 left as deviations. The C++ reference is `main`; every function was also
diffed against the 11.12 sources the native package is built from (`compaction_picker_universal.cc`,
`compaction_picker_fifo.cc`, `wal_iterator_impl.cc` and `ComputeFilesMarkedForPeriodicCompaction` are identical up to
declaration style; `compaction_outputs.cc` only moved the partitioner prefix code into `PrepareNewOutputForFirstKey`).
Unit tests are ports from `compaction_picker_test.cc` in `tests/RocksDb.Managed.Tests/Db/CompactionPickerTests.cs` and
DB-level tests in `Db/CompactionFeatureTests.cs`; native comparisons are in
`tests/RocksDb.Managed.CompatTests/Compaction/CompactionPickerParityTests.cs` (lock step) and
`CompactionStyleCompatTests.cs`.

| Area | C++ source | Change | Verified against native |
|---|---|---|---|
| Universal picker | `compaction_picker_universal.cc` (whole file) | Rewritten as a port of UniversalCompactionBuilder: periodic compaction (PickPeriodicCompaction, full compaction from the oldest run, marked-file check), delete-triggered compaction of files marked for compaction (single level: from the marked file to the oldest run; multi level: PickFilesMarkedForCompaction + BuildCompactionToNextLevel), `max_read_amp` (-1, 0 = auto-tuned from max_run_size / size_ratio / write_buffer_size, N), MightExcludeNewL0sToReduceWriteStop, incremental size amplification (PickIncrementalForReduceSizeAmp, GetMaxOverlappingBytes, grandparents for cut alignment), `compression_size_percent` (outputs of newer runs are written uncompressed; GetCompressionFlush disables flush compression), trivial moves decided on user keys like IsInputFilesNonOverlapping. `incremental`, `max_read_amp` and `reduce_file_locking` are typed options now. | Lock-step parity (`LockStepUniversalMatchesNative`, `LockStepUniversalMaxReadAmpMatchesNative` with size_ratio 20, max_read_amp 0, allow_trivial_move): every LSM shape and every compaction identical to native 11.12 over 40 flushes (write-amp 3.201 vs 3.202, 4.408 vs 4.408). `NativeMarkedFilesAreCompactedLikeNative`: an L0 file marked by native's CompactOnDeletionCollector is compacted to the same level by both engines and `compaction-pending` clears. |
| Periodic compaction defaults | `SanitizeCfOptions` (`column_family.cc`), `ComputeFilesMarkedForPeriodicCompaction` | `periodic_compaction_seconds` defaults to 0xfffffffffffffffe and resolves like upstream (30 days for universal and for leveled with a compaction filter; universal takes min(ttl, periodic)); the OPTIONS file records the sanitized value as native does. **Fixed** the file age: upstream uses `file_creation_time` first, then `oldest_ancester_time`; the port used the reverse order (compacted files carry their oldest input's ancestor time, so they were marked again right after a periodic compaction). | unit tests; the OPTIONS value matches native's sanitized one. |
| FIFO picker | `compaction_picker_fifo.cc` (whole file), `ComputeCompactionScore` (FIFO), `ShouldChangeFileTemperature` | `max_data_files_size`: blob-aware score, size-based drop (proportional data per file) and TTL remaining-size estimate. `use_kv_ratio_compaction`: PickRatioBasedIntraL0Compaction (tiered boundaries from the capacity-derived target; SanitizeCfOptions keeps max_compaction_bytes 0 for it). `file_temperature_age_thresholds`: PickTemperatureChangeCompaction rewrites one file at a time with the target temperature, recorded in the MANIFEST; `age_for_warm`, `allow_trivial_copy_when_change_temperature` and `trivial_copy_buffer_size` are persisted (upstream ignores `age_for_warm`; the managed file system has one tier, so the copy options have no effect). Compaction outputs use Compaction::GetOutputTemperature (override, last_level_temperature, default_write_temperature) and flushes `default_write_temperature`. | `LockStepFifoMatchesNative` (size-based deletion plus cost-based intra-L0): identical shapes and compactions. `FifoTemperatureChangeIsVisibleToNative`: managed reads native's `default_write_temperature`, and native's GetLiveFilesStorageInfo reports the temperature a managed temperature-change compaction recorded. kv-ratio / blob-aware sizing: unit-test ports only (the C API cannot attach blob files to a FIFO DB in a way the harness compares). |
| Output cutting | `CompactionOutputs::ShouldStopBefore`, `FillFilesToCutForTtl`, `UpdateFilesToCutForTTLStates`, `PrepareNewOutputForFirstKey`, `Compaction::Compaction` (output_split_key_), `BlockBasedTableBuilder::EstimatedTailSize` | Complete ShouldStopBefore: TTL cuts at old output-level files (leveled kMinOverlappingRatio), SST partitioner (prefix fast path and ShouldPartition fallback; also for level-0 outputs, as upstream), max output size with the estimated tail under `target_file_size_is_upper_bound`, round-robin split at the output level's compact cursor.
