New public API: `SstPartitioner`, `SstPartitionerFactory`, `SstPartitionerFixedPrefixFactory` (`ColumnFamilyOptions.SstPartitionerFactory`, persisted as `{id=SstPartitionerFixedPrefixFactory;length=N;}`), `ColumnFamilyOptions.TargetFileSizeIsUpperBound`. IsTrivialMove consults CanDoTrivialMove. | unit tests (partitioner cuts and OPTIONS form, per-key partitioner, upper bound keeps outputs below the target, TTL cut candidates, split key); the leveled lock-step parity test is unchanged (none of these cuts fire in it). The C API has no partitioner setter, so the partitioner is not compared with native. |
| Memtable first-insert time | `MemTable::UpdateOldestKeyTime`, `FlushJob::WriteLevel0Table`, `BuildTable` (SetSeqnoTimeTableProperties) | The first insert stores the time with one compare-exchange on a long that is 0 (afterwards one relaxed load per insert). Flush writes it as `rocksdb.oldest.key.time`, uses min(now, it) as oldest_ancester_time and as `rocksdb.creation.time` (FIFO: the file creation time), as upstream. | unit test `FlushRecordsMemtableFirstInsertTime`; the dbbench comparison below shows no write-path change. |
| HyperClock | `BaseClockTable::IsEvictionEffortExceeded`, `FixedHyperClockTable::Evict` / `CalcHashBits` / constructor | `HyperClockCacheOptions.EvictionEffortCap` (default 30, sanitized to >= 1; a strict capacity limit means no cap), `EvictionEffortExceededCount`. CalcHashBits shrinks the table until its metadata fits the capacity. With full metadata charging the slot table (64 bytes per slot, sizeof(HandleImpl)) is charged once at creation and entries are charged their own charge, as upstream; the port charged a per-entry constant. | unit tests (`HyperClockEvictionEffortCapLetsInsertsExceedCapacityWhenAllPinned`, `HyperClockTableIsClampedForTinyCapacities`). |
| LRU metadata charge | `LRUHandle::CalcuMetaCharge` | `malloc_usable_size` of the handle: sizeof(LRUHandle) - 1 + 16-byte key = 87 bytes, served by glibc malloc with 88 usable bytes (the constant was 112). This is an approximation: jemalloc builds report 96 and builds without malloc_usable_size 87; the managed entry object's real size is not measured. | existing tests (usage arithmetic). |
| TransactionLogIterator | `WalIteratorImpl::UpdateCurrentWriteBatch`, `StopBeforeRecoveryGap` | A discontinuity in sequence numbers (writes with disableWAL, ingestion) now ends the run with NotFound "Gap in sequence numbers"; the iterator is spent. Audit #3 kept the older re-seek on the assumption that 11.12 differs from `main`, but the 11.12 sources and the native binary already have this behavior (and the recovery-gap tracker). The recovery-gap tracker itself is not ported: the port never resumes writes after a failed WAL write (the DB stays stopped until reopened), so no indeterminate sequence range can exist. | `WalIteratorStopsAtSequenceGapLikeNative`: same batch count and NotFound status as native. |
| `rocksdb.compaction-pending` | `InternalStats::HandleCompactionPending` | **Fixed**: reported the picker's NeedsCompaction only with auto compactions enabled; upstream reports it regardless of `disable_auto_compactions`. | `NativeMarkedFilesAreCompactedLikeNative` (native reports 1 for a marked file with auto compactions disabled). |

Still different from upstream after this pass:

- Standalone range tombstone files (FileIsStandAloneRangeTombstone, ShouldSkipMarkedFile) are treated like other
  marked files: the port does not keep per-file entry counts. They only come from atomic range replacement ingestion.
- Native SetOptions of `compaction_options_universal` / `compaction_options_fifo` is typed now; unknown future members are
  still preserved verbatim.

Read-triggered compaction, `reduce_file_locking`, `preclude_last_level_data_seconds` and the CompactRange partition
preselection, listed here before, are ported now: see the next section.

## Compaction triggers and tiering (read-triggered compaction, bottom-priority pool, per-key placement)

The last compaction features audit #3 listed as accepted-but-inert options. C++ reference: `main`; the native
comparisons run against the 11.12 package, which has all four features (the C API exports
`read_triggered_compaction_threshold`, `max_compaction_trigger_wakeup_seconds`, `reduce_file_locking`,
`preclude_last_level_data_seconds` and the fixed-prefix partitioner). Unit tests: `Db/CompactionPickerTests.cs`
(ReadTriggered* ports from `compaction_picker_test.cc`), `Db/CompactionFeatureTests.cs`, `Db/SeqnoToTimeMappingTests.cs`
(ports from `seqno_time_test.cc`); native comparisons: `tests/RocksDb.Managed.CompatTests/Compaction/CompactionTriggerCompatTests.cs`.

| Area | C++ source | Managed | Verified against native |
|---|---|---|---|
| Read sampling | `monitoring/file_read_sample.h`, `Version::Get` / `MultiGetFromSST` (`version_set_sync_and_async.h`), `LevelIterator::SampleRead`, `Version::AddIteratorsForLevel` (L0) | `Monitoring/FileReadSample.cs`; `FileMetaData.NumReadsSampled` / `NumCollapsibleEntryReadsSampled` (FileSampledStats, reset by trivial moves like upstream's re-added FileMetaData); `LiveFileMetadata.NumReadsSampled`. A Get or seek is sampled 1/1024 with a thread-local Lehmer generator (`Random::GetTLSInstance`), Next/Prev 1/65536 with a thread-local counter; a sampled lookup that ends NotFound / Merge / Deleted in a file counts a collapsible read, a sampled iterator position on a deletion / single deletion / merge operand likewise. Allocation-free; MultiGet draws per key and file (upstream once per key: same rate). | `ReadTriggeredCompactionMatchesNative` (random sampling on both sides, 20000 lookups of deleted keys). |
| Read-triggered picking | `VersionStorageInfo::ComputeFilesMarkedForReadTriggeredCompaction`, `LevelCompactionBuilder::SetupInitialFiles`, `UniversalCompactionBuilder::PickReadTriggeredCompaction`, `NeedsCompaction` of both pickers, `ColumnFamilyData::ValidateOptions` | `VersionStorageInfo.ReadTriggeredCompactionFiles` (hottest first, last non-empty level skipped except L0, FIFO never), leveled pick after forced blob GC (`kReadTriggered`, compact to next level), universal pick after delete-triggered (clean cut, intra-L0 for one level, BuildCompactionToNextLevel). `ColumnFamilyOptions.ReadTriggeredCompactionThreshold` is typed, validated (>= 0, finite) and mutable. | `ReadTriggeredCompactionMatchesNative`: same LSM shape after enabling auto compactions, with the threshold and with 0; each engine reads the other's OPTIONS value. |
| Periodic compaction trigger | `DBImpl::TriggerPeriodicCompaction`, `ComputeTriggerCompactionPeriod`, `GetMinTimeBasedCompactionInterval` | `DbOptions.MaxCompactionTriggerWakeupSeconds` (typed, persisted, mutable); a timer re-evaluates the scores of column families with ttl / periodic / FIFO temperature / read-triggered settings, so a DB that only serves reads picks up TTL, periodic and read-triggered compactions (before, scores were only recomputed when a version was installed). `bottommost_file_compaction_delay` shortens the period too (see the next section). | OPTIONS value both ways in `ReadTriggeredCompactionMatchesNative`. |
| Bottom-priority pool, `reduce_file_locking` | `DBImpl::BackgroundCompaction` (forwarding branch), `CreateIntendedCompactionForwardedToBottomPriorityPool`, `ResetBottomPriCompactionIntent`, `UniversalCompactionBuilder::MeetsOutputLevelRequirements` | `DbOptions.BottomPriorityBackgroundThreads` (managed-only, not persisted: the equivalent of `Env::SetBackgroundThreads(n, BOTTOM)`, per DB instead of per Env; default 0 like upstream). Automatic compactions to the non-zero max output level that are not trivial moves are queued for the bottom pool; with `reduce_file_locking` only the newest input run (the oldest L0 file or the input level) stays reserved and the bottom thread repicks with `require_max_output_level`. | unit tests (`BottomPriorityPoolReservesFilesPerReduceFileLocking`, `UniversalRequireMaxOutputLevelPicksOnlyLastLevelCompactions`). No native comparison: the native bottom pool belongs to the process-wide default Env, which other compat tests share, and its scheduling is timing dependent. |
| CompactRange first level | `DBImpl::CompactRangeInternal`, `Version::OverlapWithLevelIterator`, `OverlapWithIterator` | With both bounds the managed port now checks keys inside files (seek per file, range tombstones through `RangeDelAggregator.IsRangeOverlapped`), as upstream; it used only file metadata before, so it compacted files whose key range spanned the request without any key in it. With an SST partitioner that has a boundary inside the range (`CanDoTrivialMove(begin, end)` false), file-level overlap preselects the files straddling it. With user-defined timestamps the port keeps the file-level check. | `CompactRangePartitionStraddleMatchesNative` (no partitioner / key in range / boundary in range / no boundary in range). |
| Seqno-to-time mapping | `db/seqno_to_time_mapping.{h,cc}`, `DBImpl::{GetSeqnoToTimeSample, EnsureSeqnoToTimeMapping, PrepopulateSeqnoToTimeMapping, RegisterRecordSeqnoTimeWorker, RecordSeqnoToTimeMapping}`, `BuildTable` (TimedPut conversion, SetSeqnoTimeTableProperties) | `Db/SeqnoToTimeMapping.cs` (encoding, Append/merge, time span, capacity with the min-gap removal, PrePopulate, cutoffs), `Db/DbImpl.SeqnoTime.cs` (DB-wide mapping under the mutex, recording timer at min_preserve_seconds / 100, 100 pre-allocated seqnos on a new DB with the options). Flushes and compactions write `rocksdb.seqno.time.map`. **Fixed**: flush wrote TimedPut entries with their packed write time; it now converts them like BuildTable (preferred seqno from the mapping, else kTypeValue). Upstream copies the mapping into each super version; the port copies it under the mutex when a flush / compaction starts. | `PrecludeLastLevelPlacementMatchesNative`: both engines pre-allocate seqnos to 100, and each engine places the other's data using the mapping in its tables. |
| Per-key placement | `Compaction::{EvaluateProximalLevel, PopulateProximalLevelOutputRange, OverlapProximalLevelOutputRange, GetOutputTemperature, IsTrivialMove}`, `CompactionJob::PrepareTimes`, `ProcessKeyValueCompaction` / `SubcompactionState::AddToOutput`, `FinishCompactionOutputFile` (range tombstone split), `CompactionPicker::{RangeOverlapWithCompaction, FilesRangeOverlapWithCompaction}`, `ShouldSkipLastSortedRunForSizeAmpCompaction` | `CompactionJob` keeps one output group per level (CompactionOutputs); entries above proximal_after_seqno_ (max of the preclude cutoff, the earliest snapshot when lower, and keep_in_last_level_through_seqno) go to the proximal level, range tombstones are split at the same seqno, outputs carry the merged mapping; `preserve_seqno_after_` stops seqno zeroing inside the preserve window (`preserve_internal_time_seconds`). The pickers check overlap with running compactions' proximal output ranges; universal size amplification skips the last sorted run. | `PrecludeLastLevelPlacementMatchesNative` (leveled CompactRange: L5 with the option, L6 without, cross-engine), `UniversalPrecludeLockStepMatchesNative` (12 lock-step flushes, identical shapes). |

Not ported with the tiering features (CompactForTieringCollector was ported later): BlobDB outputs on the proximal level (one
blob builder per compaction, as upstream today), remote compaction inputs for the mapping, and the per-level proximal
output statistics of the compaction LOG / EVENT_LOG lines (the output file counts include both levels).

## Remaining gaps closed (ingestion, TTL iterator, WAL, statistics, backup, WBWI)

| Managed file(s) | C++ reference | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `Db/ExternalSstFileIngestionJob.cs`, `Db/DbImpl.Ingest.cs`, `Options/IngestExternalFileOptions.cs` | `db/external_sst_file_ingestion_job.cc` (Prepare, ComputeFilesOverlap, DivideInputFilesIntoBatches, Run, AssignLevelsForOneBatch, AssignLevelAndSeqnoForIngestedFile, CheckLevelForIngestedBehindFile, SanityCheckTableProperties, GetSeqnoBoundaryForFile), `DBImpl::IngestExternalFiles` | **ported.** Overlapping files in one call: files keep the argument order, a file overlapping the current batch starts a new batch, each batch is placed above the previous one (`must_assign_to_l0` once a batch went to L0, FIFO always L0), every file that needs a seqno gets its own (last_seqno + 1 per file, also for non-overlapping files with snapshots) and the DB's last sequence becomes max_assigned_seqno; levels between L0 and the base level are skipped; a range overlapping a running compaction's output counts as an overlap with the DB. Compat (`IngestionCompatTests.OverlappingFilesMatchNative`): identical levels and per-file seqnos to native 11.12 on the same files, contents identical, DBs cross-open. ingest_behind: requires cf_allow_ingest_behind or the deprecated DB-wide allow_ingest_behind (folded into the CF options used by compaction, `ColumnFamilyOptions.AllowIngestBehind`), last level, seqno 0, epoch kReservedEpochNumberForFileIngestedBehind, "doesn't fit at the last level" / "0 seqno at upper levels" errors, not with timestamps or overlapping files; compat with native (same placement, compaction keeps the last level reserved, cross-open + native compaction). allow_db_generated_files: version 0 without the external-file properties, cf id not checked, keys with any seqno and type, seqno bounds from key_smallest/largest_seqno or a scan, InvalidArgument when such a file overlaps the DB; compat: managed-flushed SSTs ingested by native and native-flushed SSTs ingested by managed keep their seqnos. Also ported: link_files, "External file column family id don't match", "File contain no entries", key_largest_seqno > 0 rejected, V1 files with allow_global_seqno/allow_blocking_flush rejected, unique id from db_id/session/orig file number, marked_for_compaction for single-range-deletion files, upstream's UDT rule that an overlap with the DB is InvalidArgument (the previous port assigned a seqno). write_global_seqno was removed upstream by #15287 (11.12); the port keeps it as the legacy option: the assigned seqno overwrites the fixed64 value of `rocksdb.external_sst_file.global_seqno` in place (no checksum update, file synced; skipped on file systems without random writes, `IFileSystem.WriteAt`). The table reader now verifies the properties block like ReadTablePropertiesHelper: on a checksum mismatch it retries with that value restored to the zero placeholder (`BlockBasedTableReader.VerifyPropertiesBlockChecksum`), so such files (also those written by native < 11.12) open; other corruption is still detected. Compat: native 11.12 reads and compacts DBs with rewritten files. File checksums and temperatures of ingested files were ported later ("Small gaps closed (2026-11-03)"), atomic_replace_range later still (see "Atomic replace range, blob checksums and histograms, backup checksums, scans"). Not ported: PreparedFileInfo, MergeForSameColumnFamily (multi-arg ingestion). |
| `Db/DbWithTtl.cs`, `Db/Iterators/Iterator.cs` | `utilities/ttl/db_ttl_impl.h` (TtlIterator), `DBWithTTLImpl::NewIterator` | **ported.** `TtlDatabase.NewIterator` strips the 4-byte write time from `Value` (and `Range` / `ScanAsync` / `Columns`); like upstream, expired entries stay visible until a compaction drops them and the TTL is not checked. A value shorter than the suffix throws Corruption (upstream asserts in debug). Compat: same scan as native DBWithTTL's iterator. |
| `Db/WriteBatch.cs`, `Db/DbImpl.Write.cs` | `WriteBatch::MarkWalTerminationPoint`, `WriteBatchInternal::Append(wal_only)`, `DBImpl::MergeBatch` | **ported.** The write group logs a single batch as is only without a termination point; otherwise the WAL parts are flattened (records after the point go to the memtables only and consume sequence numbers). `Clear` (and the public `SetContents`) drop the point. Compat: native recovery of a port DB sees only the logged part. Also: WriteBatch overloads taking a `ColumnFamily` (rep identical to native `*_cf` batches) and `WriteOptions.SyncWrites` (shared, read-only). |
| `Db/DbImpl.ColumnFamilies.cs`, `Db/DbImpl.cs` | `DBImpl::DropColumnFamily`, `~ColumnFamilyHandleImpl`, column_family_test ReadDroppedColumnFamily | **fixed (behavior).** Reads and iterators through a dropped column family's handle keep working (upstream contract); the port's handles live as long as the DB, so the CF's memtables / version are released and its files deleted at close (upstream: when the handle is destroyed). Writes to it still fail. |
| `Db/DbImpl.Write.cs` (PreprocessWrite, SwitchWalAsync, SwitchMemtableAsync), `Db/DbImpl.cs`, `Db/DbImpl.Open.cs`, `Db/DbImpl.Replication.cs` | `DBImpl::SwitchWAL`, `GetMaxTotalWalSize`, `wals_total_size_`, `DBImpl::SwitchMemtable` (WriteBuffer of the last log), `WriteToWAL` / `SyncWAL` (sync of every logs_ entry), `FlushMemTableToOutputFile` (SyncClosedWals) | **ported.** WAL bytes are accounted per alive WAL (recovered WALs by file size); with more than one CF, a write that finds the total above max_total_wal_size (0: 4 x the sum of write_buffer_size x max_write_buffer_number, kept up to date on CF add/drop/SetOptions) switches the memtables of the CFs whose data is in the oldest alive WAL (once per WAL, kWalFull flushes). Lazy WAL sync: with a single CF and no 2PC the closed WAL is only written to the OS at the switch and synced by the next sync write / SyncWAL / FlushWAL(sync) (oldest first), WALs whose data was flushed are closed unsynced, like upstream; with several CFs or 2PC the closed WAL is synced at the switch (upstream syncs closed WALs before every flush; the port does it earlier, at least as durable). Deviations: no 2PC prep-log check in SwitchWAL, no atomic_flush selection. |
| `Monitoring/FileReadStatistics.cs`, `Db/TableCache.cs`, `Db/DbImpl.Read.cs`, `Db/GetContext.cs`, `Monitoring/InternalStats.cs`, `Db/DbImpl.Properties.cs` | `Version::Get` (GET_HIT_L0/L1/L2_AND_UP), `RandomAccessFileReader::Read` (SST_READ_MICROS + GetFileReadHistograms), `InternalStats::DumpCFStatsNoFileHistogram` (cf_stats_snapshot_, "Int" row, is_periodic), `DumpDBStats` (db_stats_snapshot_), `DBImpl::KeyMayExist` (kBlockCacheTier) | **ported.** GET_HIT tickers for sync and async Get (MultiGet since the 2026-11-03 pass). With statistics, table files are read through a timing decorator recording SST_READ_MICROS and FILE_READ_GET / MULTIGET / DB_ITERATOR / COMPACTION_MICROS by the thread's activity (set around sync Get, MultiGet, iterator positioning and compaction jobs; async continuations record SST_READ_MICROS only, like kUnknown); no wrapper without statistics. rocksdb.cfstats / rocksdb.stats / the periodic dump start a new interval, cfstats-no-file-histogram does not; dbstats always does; interval lines and the "Int" row follow upstream. KeyMayExist does no file I/O: a table that is not open or a filter that is neither pinned nor cached answers "may exist" (TableReadFlags.BlockCacheTier, TableCache.TryGetOpenReader); flushed tables are opened right after the flush (BuildTable verification). Per-level file-read latency histograms and FILE_READ_FLUSH/DB_OPEN were added in the 2026-11-03 pass. Deviation: values in cached data blocks are not returned by KeyMayExist. |
| `Utilities/Backup/BackupEngine.cs`, `BackupEngineReadOnly.cs`, `BackupEngineOptions.cs`, `BackupMeta.cs` | `BackupEngineImpl::CreateNewBackupWithMetadata` (excludable_items, exclude_files_callback, copy_engine_), `RestoreDBFromBackup` (alternate_dirs, restore_file_infos), max_background_operations | **ported.** `CreateBackupOptions.ExcludeFilesCallback` (requires schema_version >= 2, exceptions -> Aborted) sees the shared_checksum files that would be copied; excluded ones are listed in the meta file as `<file> ni::excluded true` and not copied; `RestoreOptions.AlternateDirs` supplies them (InvalidArgument "Excluded file ... not found" otherwise); `BackupInfo.ExcludedFiles`. Copies of a backup and of a restore run on max_background_operations workers (decisions stay sequential, results recorded in dispatch order). Compat: native lists and verifies backups with excluded files, parallel backups restore natively. Deviation: kKeepLatestDbSessionIdFiles does not accept an excluded file missing from every alternate dir when the DB already has it. |
| `Utilities/WriteBatchWithIndex/WriteBatchWithIndex.cs`, `MultiGetBatch.cs` | `WriteBatchWithIndex::MultiGetFromBatchAndDB` | **ported.** Keys settled by the batch (put, delete, merges over a put/delete) are answered from it; the others (absent, merge in progress) are read with one DB MultiGet (one consistent view) and the batch operands merged on top; per-key status / value / error. Compat: same results as native GetFromBatchAndDB for every key of a random workload. |

## Parallel compression and per key-value protection

| Managed file(s) | C++ source | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `Table/BlockBasedTableBuilder.Parallel.cs`, `BlockBasedTableBuilder.cs` (CompressBlock, WriteMaybeCompressedBlock with a precomputed checksum), `IndexBuilder.cs` (PrepareIndexEntry / FinishIndexEntry, PreparedIndexEntry) | `table/block_based/block_based_table_builder.cc` (ParallelCompressionRep, EmitBlockForParallel, BGWorker, MaybeStartParallelCompression, StopParallelCompression, EstimatedFileSize), `index_builder.{h,cc}` (PrepareIndexEntry / FinishIndexEntry of the shortened, partitioned and hash index builders) | **ported (design deviation, same output).** `CompressionOptions.ParallelThreads` &gt; 1 with compression enabled and without coupled partitioned filters (Rep::Rep's sanitization) emits finished data blocks into a ring of 4/8/16/32 slots (ComputeRingBufferNbits); thread-pool work items (at most min(parallel_threads, ring - 1), no dedicated threads per file) compress them and checksum the compressed payload; the emit thread writes completed blocks in order (trailer with the context modifier added, padding, data size properties, FinishIndexEntry) and compresses a still pending head block itself when it needs a slot, so it never waits for unstarted work. Upstream's lock-free state machine and dedicated writer thread are replaced by per-slot CAS states; writing stays on the emit thread because output is staged in memory anyway. Output is byte-identical to the serial path (unit test over binary search / first key / partitioned (+ decoupled partitioned filters) / hash indexes, LZ4/LZ4HC/ZSTD/Snappy/zlib, raw and trained dictionaries, format_version 2..8, super block alignment, XXH3/xxHash/crc32c, range deletions; parallel_threads 2, 4, 9). Deviation: upstream's PartitionedIndexBuilder::FinishIndexEntry may cut a partition before the last entry (AddIndexEntry does not), so upstream parallel and serial outputs can differ; the port replays AddIndexEntry's rules so they never do. Compaction cuts output files on `EstimatedFileSize` (FileSize plus the in-flight uncompressed blocks, like CompactionOutputs::UpdateFileSize). The table property collectors see the written file size, which lags behind with parallel compression (as upstream). |
| `Db/KvChecksum.cs` | `db/kv_checksum.h` (ProtectionInfo, ProtectionInfoKVO / KVOC / KVOS / KV) | **ported.** Same seeds (kSeedK/V/O/S/C) and hash (NPHash64 = Hash64, the XXH3 preview); the value is never persisted. The C++ wrapper types are not separate C# types (callers track the covered fields). |
| `Db/WriteBatch.Protection.cs`, `Db/WriteBatch.cs` (record hook, save points, Append, UpdateTimestamps), `Db/DbImpl.Protection.cs`, `Db/DbImpl.Write.cs` (MemTableInserter, WAL write) | `db/write_batch.cc` (WriteBatch::ProtectionInfo, ProtectionInfoUpdater, VerifyChecksum, Append, TimestampUpdater), `db/db_impl/db_impl_write.cc` (Write / WriteImpl / WriteToWAL) | **ported.** `new WriteBatch(reserved, maxBytes, protectionBytesPerKey: 8)` protects each record as it is added (computed from the record just written, i.e. the ReadRecordFromWriteBatch view VerifyChecksum uses, which equals upstream's per-op computation); `WriteOptions.ProtectionBytesPerKey` 8 computes it in DB::Write (UpdateProtectionInfo), other non-zero values fail with InvalidArgument "`WriteOptions::protection_bytes_per_key` must be zero or eight". The WAL write verifies the logged batch (VerifyChecksum: Corruption "ProtectionInfo mismatch", nothing written, background error); the memtable insert hands each entry's KVOC -&gt; KVOS (StripC, ProtectS with the entry's sequence; UpdateO for DeletionWithTimestamp) to MemTable.Add, which verifies the encoded entry (VerifyEncodedEntry, upstream messages). Deviations: entries applied through in-place updates (inplace_update_support) do not carry the batch protection into the memtable (the memtable computes its own checksum). Parallel memtable inserts (concurrent_memtable_writes: each writer inserts its own batch through MemTable.AddConcurrently) carry and verify the protection exactly like the serial path (test ParallelMemtableInsertsWithProtection / ParallelMemtableInsertDetectsCorruptedEntry). |
| `Db/MemTable.cs` (entry checksum, Get, MemTableIterator, Update / UpdateCallback), `Db/MemTableSupport.cs` (option + validation) | `db/memtable.cc` (UpdateEntryChecksum, VerifyEncodedEntry, VerifyEntryChecksum, SaveValue, MemTableIterator::VerifyEntryChecksum), `db/column_family.cc` (ValidateOptions) | **ported.** memtable_protection_bytes_per_key (0, 1, 2, 4, 8; else NotSupported "Memtable per key-value checksum protection only supports 0, 1, 2, 4 or 8 bytes per key.") appends the checksum to every entry; point lookups and every memtable iterator position (so DB iterators and flush) verify it: Corruption "Corrupted memtable entry, per key-value checksum verification failed." (iterators throw instead of returning an invalid iterator with a status; a failed flush sets the background error). In-place updates re-encode the checksum. |
| `Table/BlockKvProtection.cs`, `Table/Block.cs`, `Table/BlockIter.cs` (VerifyKvChecksum), `Table/BlockBasedTableReader.cs` (Protect), `Db/TableFactory.cs` | `table/block_based/block.{h,cc}` (Initialize{Data,Index,MetaIndex}BlockProtectionInfo, GenerateKVChecksum, BlockIter::UpdateKey, PerKVChecksumCorruptionError) | **ported (lazy).** block_protection_bytes_per_key (0, 1, 2, 4, 8) protects data, index, metaindex and range deletion blocks loaded by the reader (not filter blocks); every positioning of a block iterator verifies ProtectKV(key, value): Corruption "Corrupted block entry: per key-value checksum verification failed. Offset: N. Entry index: M.". Deviations: the checksums are computed when the block is first verified (upstream: when it is loaded), from the keys as the iterator exposes them (global seqno / padded timestamp applied; upstream uses the raw key), and are found by entry offset (binary search) instead of a tracked entry index, keeping the parse loops untouched. |
| Options | `options/cf_options.cc`, `include/rocksdb/{options,advanced_options}.h` | memtable_protection_bytes_per_key / block_protection_bytes_per_key / compression_opts parallel_threads round-trip through OPTIONS with native (compat tests). Cost when disabled: one predictable branch per record / memtable entry / iterator step on a per-instance field (the options are per column family, so a static readonly gate is not possible). |

Tests: `Table/ParallelCompressionTests` (byte-identical SSTs, abandon with blocks in flight, EstimatedFileSize), `Db/KvProtectionTests` (ports of DbKvChecksumTest NoCorruptionCase / WriteToWALCorrupted / MemTableAddCorrupted[WithColumnFamily] for Put, Delete, SingleDelete, DeleteRange, Merge, PutEntity, TimedPut; DbMemtableKVChecksumTest Get / Iterator / Flush with corruption for 2/4/8 bytes; block entry corruption for 1/2/4/8 bytes; option validation; protected round trip incl. in-place updates), compat `ParallelCompressionCompatTests` and `KvProtectionCompatTests` (native reads parallel-compressed and protected managed DBs, options parsed both ways).

## Compaction completeness (compensated sizes, bottommost files, cursors, CompactFiles, subcompactions, stalls)

The compaction items of the 2026-11-02 triage. C++ reference: `main`, diffed against the 11.12 behaviour of the native
package where the compat tests compare. Unit tests: `tests/RocksDb.Managed.Tests/Db/CompactionPickerTests.cs`
(bottommost marking, compensated sizes, cursors, `WriteStallTests`), `Db/DbCompactionTests.cs` (`DbCompactFilesTests`,
`DbSubcompactionTests`); native comparisons: `CompatTests/Compaction/CompactionPickerParityTests.cs` (lock step) and
`CompactionApiCompatTests.cs`.

| Area | C++ source | Managed | Verified against native |
|---|---|---|---|
| Compensated file size | `Version::UpdateAccumulatedStats` / `MaybeInitializeFileMetaData` (kMaxInitCount = 20, unlimited with max_open_files = -1, the fallback for all-deletion samples), `VersionStorageInfo::UpdateAccumulatedStats`, `GetAverageValueSize`, `ComputeCompensatedSizes` (kDeletionWeightOnCompaction) | `Compaction/VersionStorageInfo.Inherited.cs`. `FileMetaData` keeps num_entries / num_deletions / num_range_deletions / raw sizes, init_stats_from_file and compensated_file_size (in memory). The stats of new files are loaded before the version is published (in LogAndApply under the MANIFEST lock, outside the DB mutex; at open unless skip_stats_update_on_db_open); flush and compaction outputs take them from their table builder instead of reading the properties back (same values). Accumulated stats are inherited by every new version; the compensated size is computed once, when a file first appears, and used by the level scores, kByCompensatedSize / kMinOverlappingRatio ordering, size-based intra-L0 and universal sorted runs. Trivially moved files are re-initialized like upstream's re-added FileMetaData. | `LockStepDeletionHeavyMatchesNative` (70% point deletes, kByCompensatedSize and kMinOverlappingRatio): identical shapes and compactions over 50 flushes; without the boost the shapes diverge at step 23 (checked by disabling it). |
| Bottommost files marked for compaction | `GenerateBottommostFiles`, `UpdateOldestSnapshot`, `ComputeBottommostFilesMarkedForCompaction` (`BottommostSeqnoCanBeZeroed`: preserve-time window, UDT full_history_ts_low, `bottommost_file_compaction_delay`), `DBImpl::ReleaseSnapshot` (bottommost_files_mark_threshold_), `InstallSuperVersionAndScheduleWork` (threshold), `MaybeUpdatePreserveTimeMinSeqno`, `LevelCompactionBuilder` (kBottommostFiles, CompactToNextLevel::kNo), `GetMinTimeBasedCompactionInterval` | Oldest snapshot and preserve-time seqno are inherited per version; releasing a snapshot past the DB-wide threshold re-marks the current versions and schedules the column families with marks (11.12 ReleaseSnapshot behaviour; main's lazier reconciliation gives the same marks). New option `ColumnFamilyOptions.BottommostFileCompactionDelay` (persisted, mutable). **Managed safeguard**: with a snapshot checker (WritePrepared / WriteUnprepared) the oldest snapshot used for marking is capped at SmallestUnCommittedSeq (DisableGC checker: no marking), because prepared-but-uncommitted entries cannot be zeroed and the file would be rewritten forever (found by `PreparedSectionWalSurvivesFlushesAndSwitches`). | `BottommostFilesCompactionAfterSnapshotReleaseMatchesNative` (no delay: both drop the deleted data after the release, same files; 3600 s delay: neither compacts), `BottommostFileCompactionDelayOptionRoundTrips` (OPTIONS both ways). |
| Round-robin compact cursors | `VersionStorageInfo::compact_cursor_` / `GetNextCompactCursor`, `CompactionJob::InstallCompactionResults` (AddCompactCursor for kLevelMaxLevelSize / kRoundRobinTtl from L1+), `VersionBuilder::ApplyCompactCursors`, `WriteCurrentStateToManifest` (SetCompactCursors), `Compaction::Compaction` (output split key from the input version) | Cursors moved from the picker into `VersionStorageInfo` (inherited, applied from the edit, levels >= num_levels dropped); the compaction's edit carries the next cursor; so does a trivially moved kLevelMaxLevelSize compaction (`DBImpl::BackgroundCompaction` trivial-move branch; corrected 2026-11-06, the earlier claim that trivial moves don't was wrong); new MANIFESTs write them and recovery loads them. The cursor no longer advances at pick time, so a second round-robin compaction of a level waits like upstream. | `LockStepRoundRobinMatchesNative` (50 flushes identical); `RoundRobinTrivialMovesAdvanceCursorLikeNative` (ascending keys, mostly trivial moves: shapes, moves and persisted cursors identical); `RoundRobinCursorsResumeAcrossEngines`: the first half written by one engine, copies continued by both: identical shapes and compactions, and the cursors both engines then write are byte-identical. |
| CompactFiles | `DBImpl::CompactFiles` / `CompactFilesImpl`, `CompactionPicker::SanitizeAndConvertCompactionInputFiles`, `SanitizeCompactionInputFilesForAllLevels`, `GetCompactionInputsFromFileNumbers`, `PickCompactionForCompactFiles`, `CompactionOptions` | `Compaction/CompactionPicker.CompactFiles.cs`; `ColumnFamily` / `RocksDatabase` `CompactFiles[Async]` by file name (`/000012.sst`, path or bare name) or number, with `CompactFilesOptions` (output_file_size_limit, compression, max_subcompactions, output temperature, allow_trivial_move, output path id). Runs on the caller's thread (sync) or a long-running thread (async); returns the output file paths. | `CompactFilesMatchesNative`: same added files (older L0 files, overlapping files down to the output level), same LSM after L0 -> L1, L0 -> L2 and a trivial move, same InvalidArgument errors (unknown file, upward compaction, level beyond the last), identical contents, managed outputs read by native. EnoughRoomForCompaction and the CompactionJobInfo output were added in the 2026-11-03 pass. Not ported: cancellation flag. |
| Subcompactions | `Compaction::ShouldFormSubcompactions`, `CompactionJob::GenSubcompactionBoundaries` (+ Acquire/Shrink/ReleaseSubcompactionResources), `BlockBasedTable::ApproximateKeyAnchors`, `ClippingIterator`, `SubcompactionState`, `CompactionOutputs::AddRangeDels` (comp_start / comp_end bounds), `SetOutputSlitKey`, `AggregateCompactionStats` | `CompactionJob` builds one sub-job per range (first on the calling thread, the rest on long-running threads), each with its own outputs, compaction filter, merge helper, blob builder and stats; results merged in key order (blob garbage per file summed). Round-robin leveled compactions may use more subcompactions than max_subcompactions by reserving free compaction slots. NUM_SUBCOMPACTIONS_SCHEDULED is recorded. Column families with user-defined timestamps form subcompactions since the 2026-11-03 pass. | `SubcompactionsMatchNative` (max_subcompactions 4, puts / deletes / a range tombstone across boundaries): identical contents, managed outputs read by native. File boundaries are not required to match: native deduplicates anchors with equal (shortened separator) keys after an unstable std::sort, so which anchor size it keeps is unspecified; in the test both write one L6 run (19 vs 21 files). Bench (`compact` of 1M x 100 B with 16 MiB memtables, `--max_subcompactions 4`, loaded VM): managed 1.57 s -> 0.51 s, c-native 1.62 s -> 0.96 s. |
| Write controller | `ColumnFamilyData::RecalculateWriteStallConditions`, `SetupDelay`, `GetPendingCompactionBytesForCompactionSpeedup`, `GetMarkedFileCountForCompactionSpeedup`, `WriteController::set_delayed_write_rate`, `DBImpl::DelayWrite` | **Fixed**: leaving a delay raises the delayed rate by kDelayRecoverSlowdownRatio (1.4) instead of resetting it at the next delay; compaction pressure also for a soft limit of 0, for debt above min(max(bottommost / 8, 2 x level base), soft / 4) (was soft / 4) and for >= 2 marked files, without the disable_auto_compactions gate; DelayWrite sleeps in ~1 ms steps and ends early when the delay condition clears. **Fixed (2026-11-03)**: compactions, RefitLevel and flushes install the super version (and so recalculate the stall) in the DB-mutex section that installs the version (`VersionSet.LogAndApplyAsync` `installedUnderMutex`; upstream's LogAndApply returns with the mutex held), so no thread sees the new version with the old stall (a manual compaction could return with L0 empty while writes were still delayed); the stall is recalculated only when the version or the memtables changed (`ColumnFamilyData::InstallSuperVersion`; the extra recalculations at open, after every compaction and after bottom-priority jobs each cut the delayed rate by 0.8); a continued delay acquires its new token before releasing the old one, so the controller keeps its credit and refill time. Remaining: the delay is computed from the current write group's size (upstream: the previous group's); SetDBOptions / SetOptions recalculate the stall at once (upstream: at the next version or memtable change). | `WriteStallSingleColumnFamily` port, `ContinuedDelayKeepsWriteControllerDebt`, `CompactionInstallsVersionAndWriteStallTogether`, `WriteSlowdownDelaysWrites`. L0 pile-up under the free-running compaction-heavy config (`docs/perf.md`): with warm code the peak L0 count is 7 / 7 / 9 / 8 / 7 (managed) vs 6 / 7 / 8 / 7 / 7 (native); neither engine reaches a stall trigger, so the write controller is not involved; cold runs reach 10 (JIT). |

## Findings in out-of-scope files (for the coordinator)

1. `Db/ColumnFamilyData.cs` passes `ColumnFamilyOptions.ArenaBlockSize` straight into `MemTableOptions`. With the new
   default of 0, the derivation happens in `MemTableOptions.GetEffectiveArenaBlockSize`, so no change is required.
   The todo item "small WriteBufferSize with default ArenaBlockSize trips Debug.Assert" can be closed.
2. `Db/DbImpl.Read.cs` `GetFromFile`: the file's range tombstones are applied without truncating them to the file
   boundaries. This matches upstream TableCache::Get, so no change; it is noted for when range-tombstone truncation
   in iterators is revisited.
3. Callers of `DbFormat.TryParseInternalKey` (ExternalSstFileIngestionJob, TableCache range-del loading) now reject
   WAL-only value types as corruption, as upstream does. No call sites needed changes.

Since a primary deletes a file only after the MANIFEST edit that dropped it (LogAndApply, then PurgeObsoleteFiles), a missing file means a newer edit was appended after the read: the same catch-up re-reads the MANIFEST (up to 3 times) and installs the newer state, else the install is retried by the next catch-up. Before, the column family kept its old version while the WALs with the newer data were already deleted, leaving the secondary several flushes behind until a later catch-up (the `ManifestRolloverSwitchesToTheNewManifest` flake). User iterators: ReadOptions.readahead_size gives a fixed readahead; otherwise, after num_file_reads_for_auto_readahead sequential blocks, a readahead buffer starts at initial_auto_readahead_size and doubles up to max_auto_readahead_size, reset by a non-sequential block. Like upstream (readahead_limit_, readahead_size_), the auto readahead and the compaction input readahead are an OS prefetch (`IRandomAccessFile.Prefetch`, readahead(2)) of the block plus the readahead size whenever a block lies past the prefetched range, with no buffer; the readahead buffer is the fallback for files without Prefetch (direct I/O, other platforms, in-memory files), and ReadOptions.readahead_size always uses it. Deviation: only the sync positioning paths use readahead. | `BlockBasedTableTests.AsyncPathsReadIndexPartitionsAsynchronously`, `IteratorAutoReadahead` (buffer fallback), `IteratorAutoReadaheadUsesFilePrefetch`. |

## Atomic replace range, blob checksums and histograms, backup checksums, scans

| Managed file(s) | C++ source | Status | Tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Db/ExternalSstFileIngestionJob.cs`, `Db/DbImpl.Ingest.cs`, `Options/IngestExternalFileOptions.cs` (`IngestExternalFileArg.AtomicReplaceRange`, `IngestReplaceRange`), `Db/Compaction/CompactionPicker.cs` (RangeOverlapWithCompaction range_limit_exclusive, IsCompactionInProgress, UnregisterCompaction), `Db/Compaction/Compaction.cs` | `db/external_sst_file_ingestion_job.cc` (Prepare's range checks, SupportsAtomicReplaceRangeTombstone / CanUse..., HasPartialOverlap, PrepareAtomicReplaceRangeTombstone, ActivateAtomicReplaceRangeTombstone, NeedsFlush, Run, AddFileToReplace, CreateEquivalentFileIngestingCompactions, RegisterRange / UnregisterRange, Cleanup / DeleteInternalFiles), `DBImpl::IngestExternalFiles` / `PrepareFileIngestion` / `CommitFileIngestionHandles` (argument checks, Run under the mutex, RegisterRange before LogAndApply, UnregisterRange(restore on failure), EnqueuePendingCompaction on failure), `ColumnFamilyData::RangesOverlapWithMemtables(range_limit_exclusive)`, `CompactionPicker::RangeOverlapWithCompaction`, `Compaction::OverlapProximalLevelOutputRange` | **ported.** Argument checks (FIFO NotSupported, ingest_behind InvalidArgument, snapshot_consistency NotSupported, one bound NotSupported, limit <= start, files outside [start, limit)); the memtable overlap / blocking flush applies to the half-open range (whole CF: any memtable data); Run selects from the current version under the DB mutex: files inside the range are deleted by the ingestion's edit, a running compaction over the range (any, for the whole CF) or a replaced file being compacted is InvalidArgument; ingested files go to the deepest level without overlap checks; a partial overlap with universal compaction (+ allow_global_seqno, no DB-generated files, no UDT) ingests the prepared range tombstone file ([start, limit), written into the DB by SstFileWriter at Prepare, default_write_temperature, the factory's checksum) as the oldest batch, consuming one extra seqno; fail_if_not_bottommost_level -> TryAgain; a partial overlap appearing after Prepare -> TryAgain; other configurations InvalidArgument. RegisterRange registers the file-ingesting compactions (now also for plain ingestions) and marks the replaced files being compacted while LogAndApply releases the mutex; failed commits release them and reschedule compactions; replaced files are purged after a successful ingestion; an unused tombstone file is deleted. Deviations: the whole-CF flush check also looks at unflushed immutable memtables (upstream checks the mutable one only, so immutable data could survive the replacement); PreparedFileInfo / multi-handle commits are not ported (single IngestExternalFileArg). The C API of the native 11.12 package cannot request atomic_replace_range, so compat checks the resulting DB directory. | `AtomicReplaceRangeTests` (port of AtomicReplaceRangeGeneratesTombstoneForPartialOverlap: TryAgain / InvalidArgument cases, seqno + 2, levels 0,0,0,0,1,1,2, snapshot reads, compaction and reopen; contained files deleted and purged, leveled partial overlap rejected; whole-CF replacement incl. memtable data; memtable overlap against the exclusive limit; argument validation; port of AtomicReplaceRangeNotSupportedWithFIFOCompaction; running-compaction conflicts; port of AtomicReplaceRangeRacesWithIntraL0UniversalCompaction (a compaction attempted during the MANIFEST write cannot take the replaced file); a failed MANIFEST write releases the files and deletes the tombstone; power loss at every file system operation recovers either the old or the new range), compat `IngestionCompatTests.AtomicReplaceRangeResultIsReadByNative` (partial overlap with tombstone + whole-CF replacement read and compacted by native). |

| `Monitoring/FileReadStatistics.cs` (hist_type_), `Db/Blob/BlobFileReader.cs`, `BlobSource.cs`, `BlobFileBuilder.cs`, `BlobDirectWriteManager.cs`, `Monitoring/InternalStats.cs` (BlobFileReadHist), `Db/ColumnFamilyData.cs` | `BlobFileReader::OpenFile` (RandomAccessFileReader with BLOB_DB_BLOB_FILE_READ_MICROS and blob_file_read_hist), `UncompressBlobIfNeeded` (BLOB_DB_DECOMPRESSION_MICROS), `BlobFileBuilder::CompressBlobIfNeeded` (BLOB_DB_COMPRESSION_MICROS), `WritableFileWriter::Append` (BLOB_DB_BLOB_FILE_WRITE_MICROS), `BlobLogWriter::Sync` (BLOB_DB_BLOB_FILE_SYNC_MICROS), `InternalStats::DumpCFFileHistogram` ("Blob file read latency histogram") | **ported.** With statistics, blob file reads (header, footer, blobs, sync and async) record BLOB_DB_BLOB_FILE_READ_MICROS, the activity histogram and the CF's blob file read latency histogram shown by rocksdb.cfstats / cf-file-histogram; decompression, compression, appends and syncs of flush/compaction and direct-write blob files record their histograms. Checkpoints do not request checksums (upstream passes get_live_table_checksum = false). Layout unchanged: names and crc32 lines identical to native. | `BackupEngineTests.DbFileChecksumsAreUsedAndVerifiedByBackups` (legacy names = MANIFEST crc32c; mismatch detected only with the factory), compat `BackupCompatTests.BackupsOfAChecksummedDbMatchNative` (native and managed backups of the same checksummed DB with blob files have identical shared_checksum lines; native verifies and restores the managed backup). |
| `Db/Iterators/DbIter.cs` (PushOlderOperand, TempPinDataForMerge), `Env/AsyncPrefetchBuffer.cs` | `DBIter::MergeValuesNewToOld` (TempPinData, PushOperand(value, IsValuePinned())), `FindValueForCurrentKeyUsingSeek`, `FilePrefetchBuffer` async reads | **ported.** Forward merges (and the reseek path of reverse merges) reference their operands in the temp-pinned blocks / memtable, newest first, reversed in place for the merge; the first unpinnable operand turns the list into copies. The prefetch of AsyncPrefetchBuffer keeps the file's ValueTask (no `AsTask` / `Task.FromResult` per prefetch); a read still pending gets a cached continuation and a reusable event, so pooled IValueTaskSource reads are never blocked on directly. BenchmarkDotNet `ScanBenchmarks` (short job): cold full scan with auto readahead 6.4 KB -> "-" allocated per scan; merge scans allocate nothing either way and run within noise (24-byte operands 7.8 -> 8.4 ms, 1 KiB operands 40.0 -> 35.9 ms). | `DbBasicTests.ReverseIterationMergesPinnedOperands` (forward pinned merges, async forward equal), `DbIterRandomTests`, `FileSystemTests.AsyncPrefetchBufferWaitsForPooledValueTaskSources`. |

## Cache layers (2026-11-03)

Secondary cache API, compressed and tiered secondary caches, persistent cache tiers, SimCache, CacheReservationManager
and WriteBufferManager. C++ reference: `main` (11.14); native comparisons against the 11.12 package through its C API
(`rocksdb_write_buffer_manager_*`, `rocksdb_options_set_db_write_buffer_size`, `rocksdb_options_set_lowest_used_cache_tier`,
`rocksdb_load_latest_options`). The C API has no secondary, tiered, simulated or persistent cache constructors, so those
layers are covered by unit tests only.

| Managed file(s) | C++ source | Status | Tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Cache/SecondaryCache.cs`, `Cache/CacheItemHelper.cs`, `Cache/CacheSupport.cs`, `Cache/CacheTypes.cs`, `Cache/BlockCache.cs` (TryLookup with helper, StartAsyncLookup / WaitAll / WaitAllAsync, TryCreateStandalone, Release, eviction handler) | `include/rocksdb/secondary_cache.h`, `include/rocksdb/advanced_cache.h` (CacheItemHelper, AsyncLookupHandle, CreateStandalone, SetEvictionCallback), `cache/cache_entry_roles.cc`, `CacheTier` / `TieredAdmissionPolicy` / `PrimaryCacheType` | **ported.** Status results map to bool (NotSupported = false); `del_cb` is the value's IDisposable / pooled buffer. Deviation (.NET platform): the helper's create callback has no memory allocator argument. | `SecondaryCacheTests` |
| `Cache/LruCache.cs`, `Cache/HyperClockCache.cs` | `LRUCacheShard::NotifyEvicted`, the clock cache eviction callback and hit bit | **ported.** The callback runs outside the shard lock (LRU) or while the evicting thread owns the slot (HCC), for capacity evictions, insert-and-evict and replaced entries, not for Erase / EraseUnRefEntries. HCC sets the hit bit only while a callback is installed. Deviation: standalone handles created through the base class are charged to a cache-wide counter included in Usage / PinnedUsage, not to a shard, so they never cause evictions. | `SecondaryCacheTests.StandaloneHandles*`, `Adapter*` |
| `Cache/CacheWithSecondaryAdapter.cs` | `cache/secondary_cache_adapter.cc` (EvictionHandler, ProcessDummyResult, Promote, Insert / Release placeholder distribution, Lookup, StartAsyncLookup, WaitAll, SetCapacity, UpdateCacheReservationRatio, UpdateAdmissionPolicy, NewTieredCache, UpdateTieredCache) | **ported** line by line, including the 1 MiB reservation chunks and the shrink/grow orderings. Placeholder releases must go through `BlockCache.Release(ref handle, erase)` (Cache::Release), which `CacheReservationManagerImpl` does. `WaitAllAsync` is a managed addition that awaits pending handles. | `SecondaryCacheTests.Tiered*`, `CacheLayerTests.TieredCacheAsDbBlockCache*` |
| `Cache/CompressedSecondaryCache.cs` | `cache/compressed_secondary_cache.{h,cc}` | **ported.** Dummy-first admission, advise_erase, the tag bytes (source tier, compression type), do_not_compress_roles, the InsertSaved rules, split/merge into malloc-bin chunks with upstream's charges, Deflate / Inflate through a reservation manager, and capacity 0 disables the cache. Deviations: the format is in-memory only and never persisted, so compressed data uses format_version 2 block framing instead of the BuiltinV2 compression manager's; non-split values are charged their tagged length (no malloc_usable_size). | `SecondaryCacheTests.CompressedSecondaryCache*` |
| `Cache/TieredSecondaryCache.cs` | `cache/tiered_secondary_cache.{h,cc}` | **ported** (MaybeInsertAndCreate promotion into the compressed tier, async result handles, WaitAll). | `SecondaryCacheTests.ThreeQueueTier*` (fake non-volatile tier) |
| `Table/BlockBasedTableReader.CacheTiers.cs`, `Table/BlockBasedTableReader.cs`, `Table/BlockFetcher.cs` (ProcessSerializedCopy), `Table/BlockCacheStatistics.cs` (Owner), `Monitoring/Statistics.cs` | `table/block_based/block_cache.h` (BlockCreateContext, FullTypedCacheHelper), `BlockBasedTable::RetrieveBlock` / `MaybeReadBlockAndLoadToCache` with helper and `lowest_used_cache_tier`, `table/persistent_cache_helper.cc`, the persistent cache paths of BlockFetcher | **ported.** Blocks are saved as their uncompressed contents and re-created with the reader as create context (data a tier kept compressed is decompressed); filters are saved as their raw payload. Only caches with `ExtendedLookup` (a secondary tier or SimCache) get lookups with a helper, so plain caches keep their hit path. A secondary hit counts as a block cache hit plus the SECONDARY_CACHE_* tickers; the persistent cache records PERSISTENT_CACHE_HIT/MISS. MultiGet batches the secondary lookups of its data blocks (one WaitAll per batch), blocks read from files are offered compressed to three-queue tiers, and async reads use async persistent cache lookups: see "Cache dump / load and cache-tier refinements". | `Cache/SimCache.cs` | `utilities/simulator_cache/sim_cache.cc` (SimCacheImpl, CacheActivityLogger) | **ported.** Activity log lines are byte-identical (upper-case hex keys). Deviation: the log is written with a FileStream instead of an Env file. | `CacheLayerTests.SimCache*` |
| `Cache/PersistentCache/*` | `include/rocksdb/persistent_cache.h`, `utilities/persistent_cache/persistent_cache_tier.{h,cc}`, `volatile_tier_impl.{h,cc}`, `block_cache_tier.{h,cc}`, `block_cache_tier_file.{h,cc}`, `block_cache_tier_file_buffer.h`, `block_cache_tier_metadata.cc` | **ported** (upstream still ships this legacy API). PersistentCacheConfig with ValidateSettings, PersistentTieredCache, VolatileCacheTier (LRU, eviction into the next tier), and BlockCacheTier: `<path>/cache/<id>.rc` files, the 16-byte record header (magic 0xfefa, crc32c, key size, value size), write buffers padded to 4 KiB, reads from the buffers until a file is closed, LRU eviction of whole files down to 90%, pipelined inserts, and threaded writers that reserve space. Deviations: no direct I/O; the metadata index is a lock-protected dictionary instead of the evictable hash tables; a writer that cannot reserve space retries every 10 ms (upstream: every second) and writes anyway after 30 s; a closing tier stops waiting for free write buffers after 30 s (upstream waits forever); latency histograms are not collected. As upstream, the cache does not survive a restart. | `CacheLayerTests.VolatileTier*`, `BlockCacheTier*`, `DbReadsThroughPersistentCache` |
| `Memtable/WriteBufferManager.cs`, `Memory/AllocTracker.cs`, `Memory/Arena.cs`, `Memory/ConcurrentArena.cs`, `Db/MemTable.cs` (MarkImmutable, tracker), `Db/DbImpl.WriteBufferManager.cs`, `Db/DbImpl.Write.cs` (PreprocessWrite), `Options/DbOptions.cs` | `include/rocksdb/write_buffer_manager.h`, `memtable/write_buffer_manager.cc`, `memtable/alloc_tracker.cc`, the `Arena::AllocateNewBlock` tracker, `MemTable::MarkImmutable`, `DBImpl::PreprocessWrite` (ShouldFlush / TryAcquireLocalFlush / ShouldStall), `HandleWriteBufferManagerFlush`, `CollectFlushableCFs`, `WriteBufferManagerStallWrites`, SanitizeOptions (db_write_buffer_size) | **ported.** ShouldFlush / ShouldStall / ReserveMem / ScheduleFreeMem / FreeMem with cache charging (delayed decrease), BeginWriteStall / MaybeEndWriteStall / RemoveDBFromQueue, SetBufferSize / SetAllowStall / SetFlushPolicy, the oldest and largest flush policies (with atomic_flush: every non-empty CF), FlushReason kWriteBufferManager, and no_slowdown -> Incomplete "Write stall". Deviations: kFlushLargestAcrossDBs uses a simpler coordinator: while ShouldFlush holds, a task asks the registered DB with the most flushable memory to switch its largest CF, one DB at a time. It replaces upstream's sorter thread, flush hand-off leases and fences. The hard-limit local flush grant (one per 20 ms work cycle, or the front of the stall queue) is ported. The stall blocks the write leader, and later writers queue behind it, instead of WriteThread::BeginWriteStall. | `WriteBufferManagerTests` (ShouldFlush and ChargeWriteBuffer ports, stalls, policies, managers shared across CFs and DBs), compat `CacheTierCompatTests` |
| `Options/DbOptions.cs`, `Options/OptionTypeInfo.cs` (CacheTier map), `Options/BlockBasedTableOptions.cs` | `db_options.cc` (`lowest_used_cache_tier`, `db_write_buffer_size`), `cache_tier_string_map` | **ported.** `lowest_used_cache_tier` is persisted (kVolatileTier / kVolatileCompressedTier / kNonVolatileBlockTier); `write_buffer_manager` and `persistent_cache` are not persisted, as upstream. | compat `DbWriteBufferSizeAndLowestUsedCacheTierRoundTripWithNative` (native rocksdb_load_latest_options reads managed's file; managed parses native's) |

Compat (`CacheTierCompatTests`): a 2 MiB `rocksdb_write_buffer_manager_create` manager shared by two native DBs with
64 MiB memtables flushes like the managed manager shared by two managed DBs, and each side reads the other's DBs.
`rocksdb_write_buffer_manager_create_with_cache` charges 256 KiB placeholders that cover the memtable usage, like the
managed cost-to-cache, with memtable usage within 25% of native. The setters (buffer size, allow stall, flush policy)
behave alike. Benchmarks: see docs/perf.md ("Cache layers").

## Cache dump / load and cache-tier refinements (2026-11-06)

C++ reference: `main` (11.14). The C API has no cache dump / load functions (nor secondary or persistent cache
constructors), so there is no native compat test. The dump format is checked against an encoder written in the test
from the upstream definitions (`CacheDumpLoadTests.DumpFileMatchesTheUpstreamFormat`), and the upstream DB tests are
ported.

| Managed file(s) | C++ source | Status | Tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Cache/CacheDumpLoad.cs` | `include/rocksdb/utilities/cache_dump_load.h`, `utilities/cache_dump_load.cc`, `utilities/cache_dump_load_impl.{h,cc}` | **ported.** CacheDumpOptions (clock, deadline, max_size_bytes), CacheDumpWriter / CacheDumpReader with the file implementations (fixed32 length prefix per record), CacheDumperImpl (SetDumpFilter by stable base cache key prefix, header / blocks / footer, roles data / filter / filter meta / index, the max-size and deadline checks), CacheDumpedLoaderImpl (header and unit crc32c checks, InsertSaved until the footer). The dump bytes match upstream on little-endian hosts: DumpUnitMeta (fixed32 seq, fixed32 crc32c, fixed64 size), DumpUnit (fixed64 timestamp, type byte, length-prefixed key, fixed32 value size, fixed32 value crc32c, length-prefixed value), the header text with kTraceMagic, format 0.1 and RocksDB 11.14, the footer "cache dump completed", crc32c unmasked. Deviations: (1) SetDumpFilter takes the table properties of every column family of each DB, upstream only those of the default column family (`DB::GetPropertiesOfAllTables(TablePropertiesCollection*)`); (2) upstream ignores the status of each block write and only reports the footer write; the port stops at the first failed write and throws it; (3) the file reader rejects a length prefix that runs past the end of the file before reading (upstream fails on the short read); (4) Status results are exceptions. The clock is a `TimeProvider`. | `CacheDumpLoadTests` (format vector, max size / deadline, corruption and argument errors, writer errors, DumpLoadBasic / DumpLoadWithFilter ports for LRU and HyperClock with and without cached index and filter, dump while readers evict) |
| `Cache/BlockCache.cs`, `Cache/LruCache.cs`, `Cache/HyperClockCache.cs`, `Cache/CacheWithSecondaryAdapter.cs`, `Cache/SimCache.cs` | `ShardedCache::ApplyToAllEntries`, `LRUCacheShard::ApplyToSomeEntries`, `LRUHandleTable::ApplyToEntriesRange`, `ClockCacheShard::ApplyToSomeEntries`, `ConstApplyToEntriesRange`, CacheWrapper | **ported.** Shards are visited in rotation, average_entries_per_lock buckets / slots at a time; LRU reports the charge without the metadata charge, the clock cache its total charge; clock slots are pinned with an optimistic acquire during the callback. Deviation: the clock cache releases that reference like a handle (an entry erased meanwhile is freed by it); upstream undoes the acquire with a plain decrement. | `CacheDumpLoadTests.ApplyToAllEntries*` (incl. concurrent inserts / erases / lookups) |
| `Table/BlockBasedTableReader.MultiGet.cs` | `BlockBasedTable::MultiGet` (`block_based_table_reader_sync_and_async.h`: StartAsyncLookupFull per data block, one WaitAll, then RetrieveMultipleBlocks for the misses) | **ported** for caches with secondary tiers: the first data block of every key is looked up up front (consecutive keys sharing a block share the lookup), one WaitAll (sync MultiGet) or WaitAllAsync (MultiGetAsync) completes them, hits count as block cache hits, misses are read without a second lookup. Deviations: the index is sought twice for those keys (once for the lookups, once while visiting the blocks); misses are read one by one, not coalesced (RetrieveMultipleBlocks is not ported); plain caches keep the previous path (a primary lookup cannot be pending). | `CacheTierReadPathTests.MultiGet*`, `ConcurrentMultiGetsThroughAPendingSecondaryTier` |
| `Table/BlockBasedTableReader.cs`, `Table/BlockFetcher.cs`, `Cache/CacheWithSecondaryAdapter.cs` | `MaybeReadBlockAndLoadToCache` / `PutDataBlockToCache` (compressed_block_contents passed to InsertFull), `BlockFetcher::GetCompressedBlock`, `CacheWithSecondaryAdapter::Insert` (kAdmPolicyThreeQueue InsertSaved) | **ported.** With a three-queue tiered cache, a block read from the file and stored compressed is decompressed for the primary and its compressed payload (no trailer) and type are passed to Cache::Insert; the adapter hands them to the NVM tier. Uncompressed blocks are not offered (empty compressed_value, as upstream). Other caches do not keep the compressed block (`AdmitsCompressedBlocks` is checked on a miss only). Deviation: reads through a compaction readahead buffer or a persistent cache are not offered. | `CacheTierReadPathTests.ThreeQueueTierReceivesCompressedBlocksReadFromFiles`, `NonThreeQueueCachesAreNotOfferedCompressedBlocks` |
| `Table/BlockBasedTableReader.cs` (open) | `PrefetchIndexAndFilterBlocks` / `IndexReaderCommon::ReadIndexBlock` / `RetrieveBlock` with prefetch | **fixed.** With cache_index_and_filter_blocks, the index (unless pinned as the top level of a partitioned index) and the full filter are looked up in the block cache, with the secondary tiers, before being read at open, and the lookup is counted as a hit or miss as upstream. Before, they were always read from the file and re-inserted, so a reopened DB never used index / filter blocks restored by a cache load. | `CacheDumpLoadTests.DumpLoadBasic(False,True)`, `TableBlockCacheTests.IndexAndFilterBlocksInCache` |
| `Cache/PersistentCache/PersistentCache.cs`, `PersistentCacheTier.cs`, `BlockCacheTier.cs`, `Table/BlockBasedTableReader.CacheTiers.cs` | `table/persistent_cache_helper.cc`, `BlockCacheTier::Lookup`, `RandomAccessCacheFile::Read` | **managed addition** (upstream has no async persistent cache reads): `PersistentCache.LookupAsync` (default: the synchronous lookup); BlockCacheTier reads records of closed cache files with async I/O while holding a file reference (records still in write buffers are copied synchronously); async table reads use it and read the file with async I/O on a miss. | `CacheTierReadPathTests.AsyncReadsUseAsyncPersistentCacheLookups`, `BlockCacheTierLookupAsyncReadsBuffersFilesAndNextTier` |
| `Cache/PersistentCache/BlockCacheTier.cs` | `CacheWriteBufferAllocator::WaitUntilUsable`, `WriteableCacheFile::CloseAndOpenForReading` | **fixed** (flaky test, see below). WaitUntilUsable waited at most 100 ms once, so a pipelined insert was dropped after about 400 ms of slow writer threads; it now waits until a buffer is free, as upstream. A file that cannot be reopened for reading no longer throws on the writer thread (which crashed the process): its buffers are returned and it becomes evictable. | `CacheLayerTests.BlockCacheTier*` |

### BlockCacheTierInsertLookupAcrossFilesAndEviction(False): test assumption, plus two product fixes

Under CPU hogs (8 x `yes`, 4 cores) the test failed 25 of 40 runs. Its non-pipelined variant asserted that
`Insert` never returns TryAgain, but it does whenever every write buffer is held by files whose writes the writer
threads have not finished, as upstream (whose `PersistentCacheTierTest::InsertImpl` retries TryAgain). Two runs also
aborted the test host: after the assertion failed, the test deleted the cache directory while writer threads were
still closing files, and the unhandled IOException on the writer thread killed the process. Fixes: the test retries a
TryAgain after waiting for the dispatched writes (`Flush`, no sleep) and asserts that the retry succeeds and that no
insert is dropped, and it closes the tier before deleting the directory; the product changes are the two above. After
the fix: 100 of 100 runs under the same hogs (all three BlockCacheTier tests).

## Other SST formats (PlainTable, CuckooTable, adaptive reading, user-defined index)

Ported 2026-11-03 against `main` (the plain, cuckoo and footer code is identical in 11.12). Tests: unit
`tests/RocksDb.Managed.Tests/Table/PlainCuckooTableTests.cs` (randomized table-level model tests of plain option
combinations and of cuckoo tables against sorted oracles, encoding byte vectors, upstream error contracts, DB-level
flush / compaction / reopen with dictionary oracles, adaptive reading of mixed formats, OPTIONS sections) and
`UserDefinedIndexTests.cs`; compat `tests/RocksDb.Managed.CompatTests/Table/PlainCuckooCompatTests.cs` (native DBs read
by the managed port and vice versa for six plain and three cuckoo configurations, native OPTIONS files loaded and the
table section re-serialized byte-identically, SstFileWriter output with byte-identical plain data / index / bloom
regions and an identical cuckoo hash table and properties) and `UserDefinedIndexCompatTests.cs` (native `trie_index`
UDI files read through the standard index, managed custom-index files read by native, and the trie index below).

| Managed file(s) | C++ source(s) | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `Table/TableReader.cs`, `TableBuilder.cs`, `TableReaderFactory.cs`; `Db/TableCache.cs`, `ITableCache.cs`, `Iterators/TableInternalIterator.cs` (`Create`, `LeasedTableIterator`), `DbImpl.Read.cs`, `DbImpl.MultiGet.cs`, `FlushJob.cs`, `Compaction/CompactionJob.cs`, `SstFileWriter.cs` | `table/table_reader.h`, `table/table_builder.h`, `TableFactory::NewTableReader/NewTableBuilder`, `table/adaptive/adaptive_table_factory.cc` | **ported.** The table cache, reads, iterators, flush, compaction and SstFileWriter go through the format-neutral `TableReader` / `TableBuilder`; the hot read paths call the sealed `BlockBasedTableReader` directly (type test), so block-based reads keep their non-virtual dispatch. The column family's table factory picks the reader: a file of another format fails with Corruption "Bad table magic number" like upstream; `TableFactoryType.AdaptiveTable` ("AdaptiveTableFactory") reads all three formats by magic number and writes `AdaptiveTableWriteFactory` (not persisted: upstream registers no options for it). |
| `Table/Footer.cs` (`TableMagicNumbers`, `Decode(..., enforceTableMagicNumber)`, `BuildForMagic`) | `table/format.cc` (Footer::DecodeFrom, FooterBuilder::Build, IsLegacyFooterFormat / UpconvertLegacyFooterFormat / DownconvertToLegacyFooterFormat, BlockTrailerSizeForMagicNumber), `table/format.h` (IsSupportedFormatVersionForRead) | **ported.** Plain tables write the 48-byte format_version 0 footer with the legacy magic (read back as kPlainTableMagicNumber, checksum type crc32c), cuckoo tables a format_version 1 footer; non-block-based formats have no block trailers. |
| `Table/Plain/*` | `table/plain/plain_table_{builder,reader,key_coding,index,bloom,factory}.{h,cc}` | **ported** (kPlain / kPrefix encodings incl. the seq-0 0xFF marker and the 0x3F inline-size escape, fixed / variable key length, prefix hash index with sub-indexes, PlainTableBloomV1 with and without locality, store_index_in_file, full_scan_mode, the open-time prefix extractor checks and the "requires a prefix extractor" / total_order_seek / non-prefix seek errors). Deviations: (1) the file is always read fully into memory (upstream's mmap mode; the non-mmap buffered reads are not ported) regardless of allow_mmap_reads. (2) Errors upstream reports through the iterator status (`total_order_seek not implemented for PlainTable.`, NotSupported SeekToLast / SeekForPrev) throw `RocksDbException` from the move; `Prev()`, a debug assert and release no-op upstream, throws NotSupported. (3) Get in full_scan_mode throws InvalidArgument (upstream stores it in a member status nobody reads and then indexes an empty hash table). (4) Keys outside the prefix extractor's domain use the whole key as prefix (undefined behaviour upstream). (5) Range deletions throw NotSupported from Add (upstream records a status that a later Add can overwrite). (6) The seqno-to-time mapping property is not written (upstream TODO). (7) huge_page_tlb_size is accepted and persisted but ignored. CACHE_LINE_SIZE is 64 (x86-64 / aarch64 builds). |
| `Table/Cuckoo/*`, `Util/Hash.cs` (`MurmurHash64A`) | `table/cuckoo/cuckoo_table_{builder,reader,factory}.{h,cc}`, `util/murmurhash.cc` | **ported** (MakeHashTable / MakeSpaceForKey BFS incl. the upstream `hash_vals[hash_cnt]` indexing, unused-key search, last-level files storing user keys, module / power-of-two hashing, identity first hash, cuckoo blocks, at most 64 hash functions, the FileSize estimate, the property encodings). Like upstream: only Put / Delete entries, one entry per user key, fixed key and value sizes, no range deletions, reads require allow_mmap_reads (InvalidArgument "File is not mmaped"; DB::Open fails when it loads the table handlers), lookups ignore snapshots and last-level hits report kMaxSequenceNumber. Deviations: SeekForPrev throws NotSupported (assert upstream); lookups of user keys of another length miss instead of asserting; the occupied-bucket sort of the iterator is shared by all iterators of a reader. |
| `Options/PlainAndCuckooTableOptions.cs`, `ColumnFamilyOptions.cs`, `OptionsFile.cs`, `OptionEnums.cs` | `include/rocksdb/table.h`, `plain_table_type_info`, `cuckoo_table_type_info`, `encoding_type_string_map` | **ported.** `[TableOptions/PlainTable "cf"]` and `[TableOptions/CuckooTable "cf"]` are parsed into typed options and written in native 11.12's order (compat-tested byte-identically). |
| `Table/UserDefinedIndex.cs`, `BlockBasedTableBuilder.cs`, `BlockBasedTableReader.cs`, `BlockBasedTableIterator.cs` (IndexCursor), `Footer.cs`, `BlockCacheStatistics.cs` (read-index flags), `Options/BlockBasedTableOptions.cs`, `OperationOptions.cs` (`ReadIndex`) | `include/rocksdb/index_factory.h`, `user_defined_index.h`, `table/block_based/user_defined_index_wrapper.h`, the index_mode parts of `block_based_table_builder.cc` / `block_based_table_reader.cc`, `kFooterFeatureRequireUserDefinedIndex` | **ported** (builder forwarding of OnKeyAdded / AddIndexEntry with user keys and packed tags, the uncompressed `rocksdb.user_defined_index.<name>` meta block, kCustomOnly stub index + footer feature + udi / stub properties, the open-time mode checks and fallbacks, IndexFactoryReaderWrapper routing by index_mode and ReadOptions.ReadIndex). Deviations: (1) one factory per table (upstream's builder keeps a list but configures one). (2) Tables with a custom index are built without parallel compression (upstream falls back the same way unless the builder opts into SupportsParallelAddEntry, which is not ported). (3) The custom iterator receives no ReadOptions (Prepare / scan options and upper-bound results are not ported); a false return means "no position". (4) `IndexMode` and the factory are not persisted: the OPTIONS keys (index_mode, use_udi_as_primary_index, fail_if_no_udi_on_open, skip_standard_index, user_defined_index_factory) are preserved verbatim but not interpreted. (5) ReadOptions::table_index_factory is not ported. The built-in `trie_index` factory is ported (next row). |
| `Table/TrieIndex/Bitvector.cs`, `LoudsTrieBuilder.cs`, `LoudsTrie.cs`, `LoudsTrieIterator.cs`, `TrieIndexFactory.cs` (public `TrieIndexFactory`) | `utilities/trie_index/bitvector.{h,cc}`, `louds_trie.{h,cc}`, `trie_index_factory.{h,cc}` (main; the serialized format and the builder's seqno decision are unchanged since 11.12, where `must_use_separator_with_seq_` was always set) | **ported** (rank LUT / select hints, Elias-Fano, the streaming per-level LOUDS-Dense / LOUDS-Sparse build with retroactive internal marking and prefix-key handle migration, ComputeCutoffLevel, child position tables, path-compression chains with the candidate / 10 % budget filter, BFS-ordered handles and the seqno side-table with overflow runs, every InitFromData validation with upstream's Corruption messages; the iterator's Select-free sparse seek, chain skip, prefix keys, Next / Prev / SeekToLast; TrieIndexBuilder's separators (bytewise FindShortestSeparator, last block unshortened), same-user-key run detection and tags, the EstimatedSize bound, the 32-bit handle NotSupported and the double-Finish error; TrieIndexIterator's post-seek tag correction and overflow walking; the bytewise-comparator check). Verified against native 11.12 (the `RocksDB` NuGet package registers the factory as "trie_index"): trie meta blocks written by SstFileWriter for random key sets, and by a DB flush with snapshot-pinned versions (overflow runs with real tags), are **byte-identical**; native trie files are read through the managed trie and managed trie files by native with `use_udi_as_primary_index`. Unit tests port trie_index_test.cc (bitvector / Elias-Fano oracles and corruption, the 25 topology variants, randomized stress, chains, progressive truncation, seqno side-table cases) and compare every Get / Seek through the trie with the standard index on tables whose user keys span blocks. Deviations: (1) the block's arrays are decoded into typed managed arrays (endian-neutral, bounds-checked) instead of aliased; `ApproximateMemoryUsage` reports them all (upstream counts only the child position tables). (2) Alignment checks are relative to the start of the block (upstream re-aligns a misaligned buffer first, then checks addresses). (3) In the over-budget chain filter, chains of equal length are kept in label order (upstream's std::sort is unstable), so a trie whose chain metadata exceeds the budget with tied lengths at the cut can differ from native (both valid). (4) Prepare / scan bounds (CheckBounds) are not ported (the managed UDI iterator takes no ReadOptions), so every position is in bound. (5) The parallel AddIndexEntry protocol (PrepareAddEntry / FinishAddEntry) is not ported: tables with a trie are built single-threaded (deviation (2) of the row above). (6) An empty separator (a block whose last user key is empty and shortens to empty) throws InvalidArgument from Finish (upstream asserts in debug and writes an inconsistent trie in release). (7) The OPTIONS value `user_defined_index_factory=trie_index` is still preserved verbatim, not instantiated; set `BlockBasedTableOptions.UserDefinedIndexFactory = TrieIndexFactory.Instance`. Iterators are recycled through a per-thread slot, so trie-index Gets allocate nothing (unit test). |
| `Db/ExternalSstFileIngestionJob.cs` (GetIngestedFileInfo, SanityCheckTableProperties, GetSeqnoBoundaryForFile) | `db/external_sst_file_ingestion_job.cc` (GetIngestedFileInfo / ResetTableReader / SanityCheckTableProperties), `util/udt_util.cc` (ValidateUserDefinedTimestampsOptions, CompareComparator) | **ported.** External files are opened with the column family's table factory through `TableReaderFactory` (another format: Corruption "Bad table magic number"; the adaptive factory opens all three), iterated through the format-neutral internal iterator; a plain table's largest key comes from a full scan (no SeekToLast), and verify_checksums_before_ingest is NotSupported "VerifyChecksum() not supported" for non-block-based readers. Upstream applies no plain / cuckoo-specific rule, but its generic checks reject them: neither builder records a comparator name, so the comparator check fails with InvalidArgument "leveldb.BytewiseComparator: does not match existing comparator " (the managed check used to skip files without a comparator name; it now matches upstream, with upstream's message), and SstFileWriter cuckoo files fail earlier with Corruption "External file version not found" because the cuckoo builder runs no property collectors (allow_db_generated_files skips that check). Compat-tested: native 11.12 rejects its own and managed plain / cuckoo SstFileWriter files with the same messages, and the managed port rejects both the same way (`PlainCuckooCompatTests.PlainAndCuckoo_Ingestion_SameValidationAsNative`). |
