C++ reference: `main` (11.14); native comparisons against the 11.12 package through its C API
(`rocksdb_checkpoint_export_column_family`, `rocksdb_create_column_family_with_import`, `rocksdb_compactionservice_create`,
`rocksdb_open_and_compact`, `rocksdb_open_and_trim_history`; bound in `CompatTests/Infrastructure/NativeRemoteCompaction.cs`).

| Managed file(s) | C++ source | Status | Tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Db/DbImpl.ExportImport.cs`, `Db/ImportColumnFamilyJob.cs`, `Utilities/Checkpoint/ExportImportFilesMetadata.cs`, `ColumnFamily.ExportImport.cs`, `PooledValue.cs` (LiveFileMetadata internal keys) | `CheckpointImpl::ExportColumnFamily` / `ExportFilesInMetaData`, `DBImpl::CreateColumnFamilyWithImport`, `ImportColumnFamilyJob::Prepare` / `Run` / `Cleanup` / `GetIngestedFileInfo`, `VersionStorageInfo::RecoverEpochNumbers` | **ported.** Export: flush, `<dir>.tmp` with deletions disabled, hard links falling back to copies on the first NotSupported, rename + fsync, metadata with internal-key bounds (now also in `GetLiveFilesMetadata`); blob files are not exported (upstream neither). Import: comparator-name check, CF created first and dropped again on failure, file numbers reserved and persisted with a dummy edit, bounds read from the file when the metadata has none (point keys + range tombstone SerializeKey / SerializeEndKey), "File contain no entries", "CFs have overlapping ranges" across metadata sets, move_files (links, originals deleted on success) or copies, files keep their levels and seqnos (no global seqno), epoch numbers reassigned like RecoverEpochNumbers(force, restarted for the first set only), last sequence raised to the largest imported seqno, the edit installed with writes stopped. Additional check: overlapping files inside one sorted level are Corruption before the edit (upstream's VersionBuilder consistency check fails the LogAndApply instead). | `DbApiWaveTests.ExportAndImportColumnFamily`, `ImportMultipleExportsAndMoveFiles`, `ImportWithoutInternalKeysReadsTheFiles`; compat `ExportImportCompatTests` (native export -> managed import with the same levels and latest sequence; managed export -> native import; deletions and range deletions). |
| `Db/Compaction/CompactionService.cs`, `Db/Compaction/CompactionServiceFormat.cs`, `Db/DbImpl.RemoteCompaction.cs`, `RocksDatabase.RemoteCompaction.cs`, `Db/Compaction/CompactionJob.cs` (OutputDirectory, ForRange), `Db/DbImpl.ReadOnly.cs` (recoverWal), `Db/DbImpl.Compaction.cs` (RunCompaction, CancelAwaitingJobs), `Options/DbOptions.cs` (CompactionService) | `ProcessKeyValueCompactionWithCompactionService`, `CompactionServiceCompactionJob`, `CompactionServiceInput/Result::Read/Write` with `cs_input_type_info` / `cs_output_file_type_info` / `compaction_job_stats_type_info` / `compaction_stats_type_info` / `status_adapter_type_info` / `table_properties_type_info`, `OptionTypeInfo::SerializeType` / `SerializeVector` / `StringMap`, `StringToMap` / `NextToken` / `StripOuterBraces`, `DB::OpenAndCompact`, `DBImplSecondary::CompactWithoutInstallation`, `GetCompactionInputsFromFileNumbers`, `RemoteCompactionJobDir` | **ported.** Wire format: fixed32 version 1 + options string; hex strings, ':' vectors with upstream's brace rules (one output file is `{{{...}}}`), structs, hex string maps; unknown fields ignored and absent ones defaulted, so native 11.12 (which lacks the blob / placement / preserve fields) and the port read each other. Field order is declaration order (native: unordered_map order; irrelevant to every parser). Primary: Schedule / Wait / OnInstallation with upstream's status handling (kUseLocal falls back, kAborted -> Aborted, kFailure -> the worker's status or Incomplete, a malformed result is declined with OnInstallation(kUseLocal)); outputs renamed into the DB under new file numbers with the worker's metadata (tail size from tail_start_offset, entry stats from the table properties); REMOTE_COMPACT_READ/WRITE_BYTES; CancelAwaitingJobs at shutdown. A failed remote compaction is a background error like upstream's (kCompaction + paranoid_checks: fatal). Worker: OPTIONS file by number, override objects and options map, secondary open in `<db>/<output_directory>` without WAL replay (retried on TryAgain / NotFound), MANIFEST floor check, inputs by number without expansion, CompactFiles picker with MaxFileSizeForLevel, begin/end subrange, the primary's db_id for unique ids; like upstream the input-side statistics are left to the primary (native's VerifyInputRecordCount adds the worker's output-level stats to its own: sending them made native fail with "Expected 1600 but processed 800"). Deviations: one remote job per compaction (upstream schedules each subcompaction); compactions with blob files, per-key placement, a snapshot checker or a trim timestamp run locally (11.14 workers support blob files; the snapshot checker is an upstream TODO; trim_ts is not carried by the input); the managed worker declines blob jobs with Incomplete; the MANIFEST floor compares only the file number (the secondary always reads to EOF); paranoid_hash is 0 (not computed); `OpenAndCompact` throws `OpenAndCompactException` carrying the serialized failure result (upstream returns the status and fills the output). | `RemoteCompactionTests` (native input vector, round trips, rejection rules, manual and automatic compactions with snapshots and merges through an in-process worker, UseLocal / malformed / Failure / Aborted, worker cancellation and missing inputs); compat `RemoteCompactionCompatTests` (managed reader parses native jobs and results; native primary installs managed worker results, manual and automatic; managed primary installs native worker results, snapshot honoured, native reopens the DB). |
| `Db/DbImpl.MergeOperands.cs`, `Db/GetContext.cs` (ContinueCallback), `Db/DbImpl.Read.cs` (GetFromMemTables), `Db/MemTableSupport.cs` (MergeContext.Truncate), `ColumnFamily.DbApis.cs` | `DBImpl::GetImpl` with get_merge_operands_options, `FinalizeGetMergeOperands`, `GetContext::SaveValue` (do_merge_ = false, continue_cb), `MemTable` SaveValue (continue_cb) | **ported.** Operands oldest first with the base value (entity: default column) first; NotFound; expected_max_number_of_operands -> `ExceededExpectedMax` with the count (upstream: Incomplete(kMergeOperandsInsufficientCapacity); a result flag instead of an exception, as an expected outcome); continue_cb newest first, ending the lookup at an operand. Deviation: for memtable entries the callback runs after the memtable lookup collected that memtable's operands (same calls in the same order, same result). | `DbApiWaveTests.GetMergeOperandsAcrossMemtableAndFiles`, `GetMergeOperandsLimitAndContinueCallback` (sync and async). No C API in the 11.12 package. |
| `Db/DbImpl.Experimental.cs` | `DBImpl::SuggestCompactRange`, `PromoteL0`, `DeleteFilesInRanges`, `ClipColumnFamily`, `GetCreationTimeOfOldestFile`, `GetPropertiesOfTablesInRange` / `ByLevel`, `Version::GetSstFilesBoundaryKeys`, `MaybeAddTimestampsToRange` | **ported.** Same validation messages and file selection. Deviations: PromoteL0 holds the exclusive-manual barrier instead of the DB mutex across the MANIFEST write (no compaction can pick the L0 files meanwhile); ClipColumnFamily on a column family with user-defined timestamps is InvalidArgument up front (upstream's DeleteRange without timestamp fails later). | `DbApiWaveTests.PromoteL0MovesNonOverlappingFiles`, `SuggestCompactRangeTriggersCompaction`, `DeleteFilesInRangeAndClipColumnFamily`, `PropertiesOfTablesInRangeAndOldestFileTime`, `GetCreationTimeOfOldestFileNeedsUnlimitedOpenFiles`. |
| `Db/DbImpl.LockWal.cs`, `Db/DbImpl.Replication.cs` (FlushWalAsync), `Db/DbImpl.Checkpoint.cs` | `DBImpl::LockWAL` / `UnlockWAL`, `GetLiveFilesStorageInfo` (wal_locked skips the flush) | **ported.** The first lock enters the write thread(s), takes a write-stop token, then FlushWAL; checkpoints skip the memtable flush while locked. Deviations: locks are counted per `WalLock` handle (disposed = UnlockWAL) instead of per thread id (async code has no thread affinity); FlushWAL while locked does not enter the write thread (a stalled leader holds it until unlock; no write can be in its WAL stage). | `DbApiWaveTests.LockWalStopsWritesUntilUnlocked`. |
| `Db/DbImpl.ManualCompaction.cs`, `Db/DbImpl.Compaction.cs` | `DisableManualCompaction` / `EnableManualCompaction`, `manual_compaction_paused_` checks in CompactRange / RunManualCompaction / CompactFiles | **ported.** Incomplete(kManualCompactionPaused) for new and waiting manual compactions; running ones are canceled through their cancellation token (upstream: the canceled flag polled by the compaction iterator); Disable waits until none runs. | `DbApiWaveTests.DisableManualCompactionFailsManualCompactions`. |
| `RocksDatabase.Timestamps.cs` (OpenAndTrimHistory), `Db/Compaction/HistoryTrimmingIterator.cs`, `Db/Compaction/Compaction.cs` (TrimTs), `Db/Compaction/CompactionJob.cs`, `Options/OperationOptions.cs` (internal TrimTs) | `DB::OpenAndTrimHistory`, `CompactRangeInternal(trim_ts)`, `HistoryTrimmingIterator`, `CompactionRangeDelAggregator` (ts upper bound = min(full_history_ts_low, trim_ts)), trivial moves disallowed | **ported.** avoid_flush_during_recovery rejected; each timestamped CF compacted with kForceOptimized; point entries and range tombstones above the bound dropped. Additional check: a trim timestamp of the wrong size is InvalidArgument. | `DbApiWaveTests.OpenAndTrimHistoryDropsNewerVersions`; compat `TimestampCompatTests.OpenAndTrimHistoryMatchesNative` (the same native history incl. range tombstones and WAL tail trimmed by both engines reads identically). |
| `MultiScan.cs`, `Table/BlockBasedTableReader.cs` (TryGetDataBlockSpan) | `MultiScan` / `MultiScanIterator` (db/multi_scan.cc), `ValidateScanOptionsForMultiScan`, `MultiScanArgs`, `DBIter::Prepare` / `BlockBasedTableIterator::Prepare` | **ported (simplified prefetch).** Same validation; forward and reverse (SeekForPrev(limit), limit excluded) scans; the mixed-bounds slow path skips Prepare. Prepare locates each overlapping file's data block span per range through the index, coalesces spans closer than io_coalesce_threshold, caps at max_prefetch_size per file and issues OS readahead (no pinned block buffers, no async MultiRead / rolling prefetch); exceeding max_prefetch_size stops prefetching instead of failing with PrefetchLimitReached. Range bounds are checked by the scan instead of updating the iterator's upper bound in place. | `DbApiWaveTests.MultiScanVisitsEveryRange`. No C API in the 11.12 package. |

## Tracing, RepairDB and tools

C++ reference: `main` (11.14). Native comparisons against the 11.12 package, whose C API exports `rocksdb_start_trace`,
`rocksdb_new_default_replayer`, `rocksdb_start_block_cache_trace`, `rocksdb_start_io_trace`, `rocksdb_trace_reader_*`
and `rocksdb_repair_db`.

| Managed file(s) | C++ source | Status | Tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Tracing/TraceFormat.cs`, `Tracing/QueryTracer.cs`, `Tracing/TraceReaderWriter.cs`, `Tracing/TraceRecord.cs` | `trace_replay/trace_replay.{h,cc}` (kTraceMagic, header "Trace Version: 0.2", EncodeTrace / DecodeTrace, TracePayloadType bit order, TracedReadOptions / TracedWriteOptions incl. the kReadOptionsFlags bit list, DecodeTraceRecord for file versions 0.1 and 0.2, ShouldSkipTrace: size limit, filter, sampling), `utilities/trace/file_trace_reader_writer.cc` | **ported.** Byte-compatible records (header, footer, Write, Get, Seek / SeekForPrev with optional bounds, MultiGet, optional read/write options). Unknown payload bits from newer versions stop the decoding of that record, as upstream. Deviations: WriteOptions has no rate_limiter_priority here (recorded as IO_TOTAL); a trace writer error stops recording silently (upstream returns Incomplete from the traced call's trace step, which DBImpl ignores anyway). | `TracingTests` (all record types, filter, sampling, max size, options round trip, header bytes), compat `TraceCompatTests` (native trace replayed by the managed Replayer; managed traces framed by the native FileTraceReader and replayed by the native replayer with 1 and 3 threads). |
| `Db/DbImpl.Tracing.cs`, hooks in `DbImpl.Write.cs` (WriteAsync, LeadWriteGroupAsync), `DbImpl.Read.cs` (Get, GetAsync), `DbImpl.MultiGet.cs`, `Db/Iterators/Iterator.cs` (Seek / SeekForPrev, async), `ColumnFamily.cs` (SetTraceState) | `DBImpl::StartTrace` / `EndTrace` ("No trace file to close"), `WriteImpl` (trace before the sync + disableWAL check; preserve_write_order traces the group's valid writers in the leader), `GetImpl` / `MultiGet` tracing, `DBIter::Seek` / `SeekForPrev` (`TraceIteratorSeek*` with the iterate bounds) | **ported.** Each hook is one null check of the tracer field when no trace runs. Deviations: with preserve_write_order, unordered and two_write_queues WAL-only writes (no group leader in this port) are traced at submission; a plain write into a WritePrepared / WriteUnprepared DB is traced once, with its transformed batch; StartTrace while a trace runs closes the old one with a footer (upstream drops it without one). | as above |
| `Tracing/Replayer.cs` | `utilities/trace/replayer_impl.cc` (Prepare, Next, Execute, Replay: single- and multi-threaded, fast_forward sleeps, the first failing record by trace timestamp, end without footer = OK), `trace_replay/trace_record_handler.cc` (TraceExecutionHandler incl. IsOutcomeOf / IsIteratorOutcomeOf, "Invalid Column Family ID.", result types) | **ported.** Multi-threaded replay uses dedicated threads and a queue (ThreadPoolImpl). Deviations: `NewDefaultReplayer(reader)` without handles maps every open column family (convenience; the overload with handles maps those plus default, as upstream); MultiGet records spanning column families run one MultiGetBatch per run of keys of the same column family; RefusesTailingIterators is not ported (tailing iterators are not ported). | `TracingTests.TraceRecordsAllQueryTypesAndReplaysThemIntoAnotherDb`, `ReplayStopsAtFirstFailingRecord`, compat `NativeQueryTraceReplaysOnManagedDb`. |
| `Tracing/BlockCacheTracer.cs`, hooks in `Table/BlockBasedTableReader.cs` (GetBlockRef*, FilterBlockMayMatch, TraceAccess), `Db/TableCache.cs` (level), `Db/ColumnFamilyData.cs`, `Db/Compaction/CompactionJob.cs` | `trace_replay/block_cache_tracer.cc` (BlockCacheTraceWriterImpl::WriteBlockAccess / WriteHeader, BlockCacheTraceReader::ReadHeader / ReadAccess, ShouldTrace spatial sampling with GetSliceRangedNPHash, NextGetId), `BlockBasedTable::SaveLookupContextOrTraceRecord` / `FinishTraceRecord` (cache key as block key, nkeys estimate, cf id / name / level / file number for tracing) | **ported.** Same record layout; readers check one flag when no trace runs. The caller (Get, MultiGet, compaction, iterator otherwise) flows through an AsyncLocal set only while tracing (upstream: BlockCacheLookupContext). Deviations: accesses are recorded at lookup time, so the Get-only fields referenced_data_size and referenced_key_exist_in_block are 0 / false, and the referenced key is the user key (upstream: the lookup's internal key); uncompression-dictionary and range-deletion blocks (pinned by the reader here) and the legacy IBlockCache seam are not traced; blocks are traced only with a native BlockCache (upstream: with any block cache). | `TracingTests.BlockCacheTraceRecordsLookups`, `BlockCacheTraceSamplingKeepsWholeBlockHistories`, compat `NativeBlockCacheAndIoTracesParseWithManagedReaders`, `ManagedBlockCacheAndIoTracesFrameForNativeReader`. |
| `Tracing/IoTracer.cs` (IoTracer, IoTraceReader, IoTracingFileSystem) | `trace_replay/io_tracer.cc` (IOTraceWriter::WriteIOOp, IOTraceReader, header), `env/file_system_tracer.cc` (FileSystemTracingWrapper and the file wrappers: operation names, io_op_data bits, NowNanos timestamps, latency) | **ported.** The DB's file system is always wrapped (DBImpl::fs_ with io_tracer_), so files opened before StartIOTrace are traced too; when no trace runs each file operation costs one extra interface call and one flag check. `RocksDatabase.FileSystem` still returns the configured file system. Deviations: IODebugContext request ids are never recorded (trace_data = 0); only the operations of the managed IFileSystem are traced (no Fsync / RangeSync / InvalidateCache / PositionedAppend: the managed files have no such calls). | `TracingTests.IoTraceRecordsFileOperations`, compat tests above. |
| `Db/Repair.cs`, `RocksDatabase.Repair.cs` | `db/repair.cc` (FindFiles, ArchiveFile to lost/, NewDB, ScanTable: properties, unique id, key / seqno boundaries incl. range tombstones, unknown CF -> default or Corruption, AddTables with RecoverEpochNumbers order, ConvertLogFilesToTables) | **ported (structure deviation).** The new MANIFEST-000001 (NewDB edit, column family additions, per-CF level-0 files with epoch numbers, last sequence) is written directly; WALs are then converted by opening the repaired DB once (SkipAnyCorruptedRecords recovery + flush) instead of a hand-built memtable + BuildTable, and are deleted by that recovery instead of archived to lost/. Same result: every readable table in level 0, WAL data flushed, the DB opens natively. | `RepairTests` (lost MANIFEST with tables, WAL data, deletes and two CFs; corrupt table archived; unknown CF without options), compat `RepairCompatTests` (managed repair of a native DB opened natively; native `rocksdb_repair_db` of a managed DB opened by the managed port). |
| `tools/RocksDb.Managed.Tools/LdbTool.cs`, `SstDumpTool.cs`, `SstFileDumper.cs`, `ToolText.cs` | `tools/ldb_cmd.cc`, `tools/ldb_tool.cc`, `tools/sst_dump_tool.cc`, `table/sst_file_dumper.cc`, `table/table_properties.cc` (ToString), `db/dbformat.cc` (DebugString), `db/version_edit.cc` (DebugString), `db/version_set.cc` (DumpManifest, Version::DebugString), `db/version_edit_handler.cc` (DumpManifestHandler), `table/block_based/block_based_table_reader.cc` (DumpTable), `table/unique_id.cc` | **ported** (output compared with the C++ formatting code; the native tools were not built). Command-line parsing (`--opt=value`, `--flag`, positional), "Failed: ..." on stderr with exit code 1, " ==> " lines, 0x-prefixed upper-case hex, `'key' seq:N, type:T` internal keys, TableProperties::ToString keys and order, the WAL dump row format and the manifest dump layout match. Deviations: manifest_dump's `version#` numbers are best-effort (one per column family creation, then one per final version); `dump --count_only` prints a summary instead of HistogramImpl::ToString; `raw` omits the per-block checksum lines; `recompress` only names the input compression (the codec benchmark is not ported); `checkconsistency` checks the live file sizes after a read-only open; TTL, timestamps, blob file dumps, transactions-related commands and the remaining ldb commands are not ported. | `ToolsTests` (every command's output on MemoryFileSystem), compat `ToolsCompatTests` (the tools on a native DB: scan, idump, manifest_dump, dump_wal, sst_dump scan / verify, dump --path; native opens a DB written by the tools). |


## Built-in merge operators (utilities/merge_operators, utilities/agg_merge)

C++ reference: `main` (11.14). Native comparisons against the 11.12 package: its object registry creates the
built-ins by name (`rocksdb_get_options_from_string` with `merge_operator=<id>`, and `rocksdb_load_latest_options` of
an OPTIONS file), so no C++ helper is needed.

| Managed file(s) | C++ source | Status | Tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Db/MergeHelper.BuiltinOperators.cs` (`MaxOperator`) | `utilities/merge_operators/max.cc`, `max_operator.h` | **ported.** FullMergeV2 starts from the existing value (or an empty slice) and keeps the operand that compares greater with Slice::compare (memcmp, then length); PartialMergeMulti starts from an empty slice. "MaxOperator" / "max". The result is copied into the output buffer (upstream sets existing_operand; the port has no reference-result path). | `BuiltinMergeOperatorTests.MaxMergeOperator` (port of `util_merge_operators_test.cc` MaxMergeOperator); compat below. |
| `Db/MergeHelper.BuiltinOperators.cs` (`PutOperator`, `PutOperatorV2`) | `utilities/merge_operators/put.cc`, `put_operator.h` | **ported.** Both yield the newest operand and ignore the existing value (V1 through FullMerge, V2 through FullMergeV2's existing_operand); PartialMergeMulti = operand_list.back(). Both are named "PutOperator"; nick names "put_v1" / "put". As upstream registers the class name for V2 only, an OPTIONS file written with V1 reloads as V2 (same results). | `PutOperatorsKeepTheNewestOperand`; compat below (put, put_v1, PutOperator). |
| `Db/MergeHelper.BuiltinOperators.cs` (`SortListOperator`) | `utilities/merge_operators/sortlist.cc`, `sortlist.h` | **ported with deviations.** FullMergeV2 ignores the existing value and folds the two-way merge (ties from the right, unsorted operands not re-sorted) over the operands; pieces parsed like std::stoi (whitespace, sign, trailing garbage, int range) and printed with std::to_string. "MergeSortOperator" / "sortlist". **Deviations:** (1) MakeVector scans to a NUL terminator upstream, reading past the operand when the slice points into memtable or block memory; the port stops at the slice end (a NUL byte also ends the list). (2) A piece std::stoi rejects (empty, "1,", out of range) throws upstream (terminating the process); here the merge fails (Corruption / MergeOperatorFailed). (3) Upstream's PartialMergeMulti returns true without writing a value, replacing the operands by an empty operand that later fails to parse; the port declines the partial merge (operands kept). Because the existing value is ignored (as upstream), a value produced by a full merge at the bottommost level, or written by Put, does not take part in later merges. | `SortListMergesSortedLists`, `SortListStoiMatchesStrtol`, `BuiltinMergeOperatorDbTests.SortListInDb`; compat `SortListNamesAgreeWithNative` (names and OPTIONS text only: native's reads of merged values are not reliable, see (1) and (3)). |
| `Db/MergeHelper.BuiltinOperators.cs` (`BytesXorOperator`) | `utilities/merge_operators/bytesxor.{h,cc}`, `db/merge_operator.cc` (AssociativeMergeOperator) | **ported.** XOR of the common prefix, tail of the longer input; folded over existing + operands (FullMergeV2) and operands (PartialMergeMulti), computed in one pass as the XOR of zero-padded inputs (vectorised). "BytesXOR" / "bytesxor". | `BytesXorLengthRules` (randomized against a byte-by-byte port of BytesXOROperator::XOR); compat below. |
| `Db/MergeHelper.BuiltinOperators.cs` (`StringAppendTestOperator`, `StringAppendOperator`) | `utilities/merge_operators/string_append/stringappend2.{h,cc}`, `stringappend.{h,cc}` | **ported.** StringAppendTESTOperator: FullMergeV2 with the delimiter (single operand without existing value returned as is), PartialMergeMulti returns false; default ','. Both string append operators register a "delimiter" string option, so native persists them as `{id=StringAppendOperator;delimiter=,;}`; the port now writes the same struct form (it wrote the bare name before, which native reloads with ',' whatever the delimiter) and parses it with or without braces ("id=stringappend; delimiter=\|\|"). "StringAppendTESTOperator" / "stringappendtest". | `StringAppendTestOperatorMatchesStringAppend`; `BuiltinMergeOperatorDbTests` ports `stringappend_test.cc` (IteratorTest, Simple/Empty/MultiChar/Null delimiters, DelimiterIsDefensivelyCopied, VariousKeys, Persistent*, RandomMixGetAppend, BIGRandomMixGetAppend) for both operators (the TTL-DB variant runs as `TtlDatabaseTests` since TtlMergeOperator was ported); compat below. |
| `Db/MergeOperator.cs` (CreateBuiltin, NickName, OptionsId), `Options/ColumnFamilyOptions.cs` | `utilities/merge_operators.cc` (RegisterBuiltinMergeOperators, CreateFromString, MergeOperators::CreateFromStringId) | **ported.** Every class and nick name of the registry: put / PutOperator (V2), put_v1, uint64add / UInt64AddOperator, max / MaxOperator, sortlist / MergeSortOperator, bytesxor / BytesXOR, stringappend / StringAppendOperator, stringappendtest / StringAppendTESTOperator; struct forms with "delimiter" for the string append operators; unknown ids and unknown properties stay unresolved (the OPTIONS text is preserved). Extension: "AggMergeOperator.v1" resolves to the aggregation operator (upstream does not register it; native leaves merge_operator unset for it). | `MergeOperatorFromString` (options_test.cc MergeOperatorFromString, customizable_test.cc LoadMergeOperatorTest), `OptionsTextRoundTrips`; compat `UnregisteredIdsLeaveNativeWithoutOperator`. |
| `Utilities/AggMerge/AggMergeOperator.cs` (`AggMergeOperator`, `Aggregator`, `AggregatorValues`, `AggMerge`) | `include/rocksdb/utilities/agg_merge.h`, `utilities/agg_merge/agg_merge.cc`, `agg_merge_impl.h` | **ported.** Value format `varint32 len \| func \| payload` (PutLengthPrefixedSlice), kUnnamedFuncName "" and kErrorFuncName "kErrorFuncName", error values packing the existing value and all operands length-prefixed; EncodeAggFuncAndPayload's checks; ExtractAggFuncAndValue / ExtractList with upstream's GetLengthPrefixedSlice quirks; the Accumulator line by line (function switches aggregate the older values first and only in full merges, a malformed operand ignores the rest, partial aggregation only for registered aggregators with DoPartialAggregate()), AllowSingleOperand, name "AggMergeOperator.v1", a thread-local accumulator. Aggregator values are passed oldest first, as agg_merge.cc does (the header comment says the reverse). Upstream ships no aggregators (sum / mul / last3 are test utilities, ported in the tests). Deviations: AddAggregator returns false for an already registered name (upstream: OK, the first one is kept) and the registry is copy-on-write (upstream: an unsynchronized static map); the accumulator keeps (operand, offset) pairs instead of Slices. No native counterpart (not registered by name, no C API). | `AggMergeTests` (port of `agg_merge_test.cc` TestUsingMergeOperator with `test_agg_merge.cc`'s aggregators, encoding vectors, partial / full aggregation, failure packing, compaction above a snapshot, concurrent reads). |

Compat (`MergeOperatorCompatTests.NativeAndManagedMergeIdentically`, for put, put_v1, PutOperator, max, MaxOperator,
uint64add, bytesxor, BytesXOR, stringappend, `{id=stringappend;delimiter=<>;}`, stringappendtest and
`{id=StringAppendTESTOperator;delimiter=|;}`): native writes random merges, puts and deletes (flushed SSTs with native
partial merges, and a WAL tail); managed recreates the operator from native's OPTIONS file, reads identical values,
adds operands, flushes and compacts; native reopens managed's DB, reads identical values and compacts; both OPTIONS
files name the operator with the same text, and native loads and opens managed's OPTIONS file.

## Utilities: debug, migration, memory, info logs, mutexes, EnvMirror, UDI options

C++ reference: `main` (11.14); native comparisons against the 11.12 package through the C API where it exposes the
feature (memory usage, OPTIONS loading, the trie index); OptionChangeMigration, GetAllKeyVersions, GetInfoLogList,
TransactionDBMutexFactory and EnvMirror have no C API, so their compat tests check the on-disk side (native opens the
migrated database with the new options, native's LOG files are found).

| Managed file(s) | C++ source | Status | Tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Db/DbImpl.Debug.cs`, `Utilities/Debug/KeyVersions.cs`, `ColumnFamily.Debug.cs` | `utilities/debug.cc`, `include/rocksdb/utilities/debug.h`, `util/udt_util.cc` (MaybeAddTimestampsToRange) | **ported.** The internal iterator of the super version (all memtables and files, total order) from InternalKey::SetMinPossibleForUserKey(begin), stopping after user key `end` (inclusive), adding until `++num_keys >= max_num_ikeys` (so 0 returns one entry, as upstream); a malformed internal key is Corruption. With user-defined timestamps both bounds get the maximum timestamp (exclusive_end default), so versions of `end` itself are excluded, as upstream. `null` = no bound, an empty array = the empty key (upstream's OptSlice change). value_type_string_map copied as a name table (`KeyVersion.GetTypeName`). Results go to a reusable pooled `KeyVersionCollection` (struct enumerator) instead of `std::vector<KeyVersion>`. **Deviation:** keys covered by a range tombstone are skipped through a RangeDelAggregator (upstream's MergingIterator with range tombstones skips them the same way; its file-boundary sentinel keys never surface there either). | `DebugUtilitiesTests` (ports of DBBasicTest.GetAllKeyVersions / ValueTypeString; DBMergeOperatorTest.MaxSuccessiveMergesBaseValues adapted: the port does not implement max_successive_merges, so operands stay merges; bounds, limit, SST + memtable versions, range tombstones, UDT). |
| `Utilities/OptionChangeMigration.cs` | `utilities/option_change_migration/option_change_migration.cc`, `include/rocksdb/utilities/option_change_migration.h` | **ported.** ValidateCFDescriptors (messages kept), DetermineBaseOptions, ApplySpecialSingleLevelSettings, GetNoCompactionOptions, the open with no-compaction options, MigrateToUniversal (ColumnFamilyMetaData::levels.back().level is num_levels - 1 of the open column family), MigrateToLevelBase (L1, or the last level with dynamic level bytes), MigrateToFIFO (L0 with kForce), CompactToLevel (change_level + target_level), and the reopen that rewrites the MANIFEST when the old options were used. Single-CF (Options) and multi-CF (descriptor lists) overloads; sync and async. | `OptionChangeMigrationTests` (Migrate1-4 over the 19 upstream parameter tuples, CompactedSrcToUniversal, BasicMultiCF, DifferentStylesPerCF, ValidationMismatched, FromFIFOMultiCF); compat `OptionChangeMigrationCompatTests` (a native leveled DB migrated to single-level universal opens in native with num_levels = 1; a managed universal DB migrated to FIFO and then to dynamic leveling opens in native). |
| `Utilities/MemoryUtil.cs`, `Db/DbImpl.Properties.cs` (size-all-mem-tables, estimate-table-readers-mem), `Table/BlockBasedTableReader.cs` (ApproximateMemoryUsage) | `utilities/memory/memory_util.cc`, `include/rocksdb/utilities/memory_util.h`, `db/internal_stats.cc` (HandleSizeAllMemTables, HandleEstimateTableReadersMem), `db/version_set.cc` (GetMemoryUsageByTableReaders), `table/block_based/block_based_table_reader.cc` (ApproximateMemoryUsage) | **ported.** Aggregated kSizeAllMemTables / kCurSizeAllMemTables / kEstimateTableReadersMem over the databases and Cache::GetUsage over the distinct caches (a set upstream: duplicates counted once). Property fixes made for it: size-all-mem-tables now also counts flushed memtables still referenced (iterators' super versions, history), like MemTableList::current_memory_usage_ (it equalled cur-size-all-mem-tables before); estimate-table-readers-mem sums open readers' pinned index / filter / range-deletion blocks and UDI memory (it was always 0). **Deviations:** the reader estimate omits sizeof(Rep) and the reader objects; `MemoryUtil.GetCaches` (block and blob caches of every column family, like the test's TEST_GetAllBlockCaches) has no table-cache entry because the managed table cache is not a block cache. | `MemoryUtilTests` (SharedBlockCacheTotal; MemTableAndTableReadersTotal adapted: the port flushes immutable memtables without waiting for min_write_buffer_number_to_merge, so one larger write buffer per CF replaces upstream's one-memtable-per-KV; cache de-duplication, consistency with the properties); compat `MemoryUtilCompatTests` (same workload on native via rocksdb_approximate_memory_usage_*: same direction of every type, memtable usage within 2x). |
| `Utilities/InfoLogFinder.cs` | `utilities/convenience/info_log_finder.cc`, `file/filename.cc` (GetInfoLogFiles, InfoLogPrefix, ParseFileName) | **ported.** Lists db_log_dir (or the DB directory) and keeps names ParseFileName reads as kInfoLogFile with the InfoLogPrefix ("LOG", or the escaped DB path + "_LOG"): LOG, LOG.old, LOG.old.N. **Deviation:** `GetInfoLogList(db)` computes the db_log_dir prefix from the absolute DB path, as the logger names the files; upstream passes DB::GetName() as given (differs only for relative paths). | `InfoLogFinderTests` (rolled logs and decoys in the DB directory; two databases sharing db_log_dir); compat `InfoLogFinderCompatTests` (native's LOG files in both layouts; a managed open continues native's series). |
| `Utilities/Transactions/TransactionDbMutex.cs`, `Utilities/Transactions/PointLockManager.cs` (custom path), `TransactionOptions.cs` (CustomMutexFactory) | `include/rocksdb/utilities/transaction_db_mutex.h`, `utilities/transactions/transaction_db_mutex_impl.cc`, `utilities/transactions/lock/point/point_lock_manager.cc` (LockMapStripe, AcquireWithTimeout, UnLock, GetPointLockStatus) | **ported.** With custom_mutex_factory each stripe allocates a mutex and a condvar from the factory; AcquireWithTimeout follows upstream line by line (Lock for a negative timeout, else TryLockFor(timeout) with kMutexTimeout on failure; cv_end_time from the holder's expiration and the deadline; deadlock detection before each wait; Wait / WaitFor; one more AcquireLocked after a timeout; SetWaitingTxn on timeout); UnLock locks the stripe mutex, unlocks the key and NotifyAll()s. Status results map to `bool` (TimedOut) and thrown RocksDbExceptions (other failures, returned as the lock result like cv_wait_fail). `TransactionDbMutexFactory.Default` mirrors TransactionDBMutexFactoryImpl (TryLockFor honours its timeout instead of ignoring it; the condvar works with any TransactionDbMutex and never reports a timeout early). Without a factory the built-in path is unchanged: one null test per TryLock / UnLock, still allocation-free and asynchronous. **Deviation:** with a custom factory lock waits block the calling thread, also through the async APIs (upstream is synchronous throughout). | `TransactionDbMutexTests` (instrumented counting factory: allocation per stripe, call counts, lock timeout through WaitFor, infinite timeout through Wait woken by NotifyAll, deadlock detection, expired-lock stealing, TryLockFor timeout and Wait failures reported, a concurrent transfer workload); `PointLockBenchmarks` (perf.md). |
| `Env/MirrorFileSystem.cs` | `utilities/env_mirror.cc`, `include/rocksdb/utilities/env_mirror.h` | **ported** as a public IFileSystem in the library (upstream ships EnvMirror in its public utilities). Every operation runs on both file systems; return values, GetChildren listings (sorted), file sizes, Read / sequential read bytes (B re-read until A's length, as SequentialFileMirror) must agree and both must succeed or both fail; results are A's. Upstream asserts (debug builds only); the port throws FileSystemMirrorMismatchException in every build. Not verified, as upstream: modification times, free space, readahead hints. | `MirrorFileSystemTests` (ports of env_mirror_test.cc Basics, ReadWrite, Locks, Misc, LargeWrite over two MemoryFileSystems; async I/O; divergence detection; a DB workload with flush, compaction and reopen over LocalFileSystem mirrored with MemoryFileSystem, whose files then compare byte for byte). |
| `Options/BlockBasedTableOptions.cs` (user_defined_index_factory, index_mode, use_udi_as_primary_index, fail_if_no_udi_on_open) | `table/block_based/block_based_table_factory.cc` (block_based_table_type_info, block_base_table_index_mode_string_map), `include/rocksdb/table.h` (GetEffectiveIndexMode) | **ported.** The factory is a Customizable option by name: "trie_index" (also `{id=trie_index}`) resolves to TrieIndexFactory, unknown names are kept verbatim (UnresolvedUserDefinedIndexFactoryName), unset is `nullptr`. index_mode and the two legacy booleans are typed and persisted as set, which is what 11.12 writes (it keeps index_mode raw and routes by use_udi_as_primary_index only); `IndexMode` returns GetEffectiveIndexMode() and setting it also sets the booleans, so a managed OPTIONS file configures 11.12 the same way. skip_standard_index (kDontSerialize) and 11.14's index_mode_explicit are kept verbatim as unknown options. **Deviation:** a native 11.12 file with `index_mode=kCustomDefault` and `use_udi_as_primary_index=false` routes reads through the trie in the port (effective mode) but not in 11.12. | Compat `TrieIndexOptionsCompatTests` (native OPTIONS files naming trie_index with each mode/boolean: factory resolved, effective mode, section written back with the same lines, a managed DB opened with the parsed options builds the trie as primary or not; nullptr and unknown factories; a managed OPTIONS file loaded by native with rocksdb_load_latest_options builds a primary trie). |

## WAL filter, predecessor-WAL records (track_and_verify_wals) and UserWriteCallback

C++ reference: `main` (11.14); the native comparisons use the 11.12 package, whose C API exports
`rocksdb_options_set_track_and_verify_wals`, `rocksdb_walfilter_create` / `rocksdb_options_set_wal_filter`. The C API
has no `WriteWithCallback`, so `UserWriteCallback` has no native comparison.

Tests: `tests/RocksDb.Managed.Tests/Db/WalFilterTests.cs` (DBTest2.WalFilterTest, WalFilterTestWithChangeBatch,
WalFilterTestWithChangeBatchExtraKeys, WalFilterTestWithColumnFamilies from `db/db_etc2_test.cc`, plus the
wal_recovery_mode and read-only cases), `TrackAndVerifyWalsTests.cs` (DBWALTrackAndVerifyWALsWithParamsTest.Basic from
`db/db_wal_test.cc` for all four recovery modes, the records written at a WAL switch, read-only opens, OPTIONS),
`UserWriteCallbackTests.cs` (WriteCallbackPTest.WriteWithCallbackTest / WriteCallbackTest.WriteCallBackTest from
`db/write_callback_test.cc`, over unordered / two-queue / pipelined / concurrent-memtable / WAL-off writes, sync and
async), `Wal/LogTests.PredecessorWalInfo*` (writer/reader, recyclable type 131); compat:
`Api/WalPredecessorAndFilterCompatTests.cs` (records written by either side verified by the other with the same
messages for a missing / truncated predecessor, native recyclable records read by managed, the option in both OPTIONS
files, and a WAL filter seeing the same records, batch bytes and column family log numbers in native and managed).

| Managed file | C++ source(s) | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `Db/WalFilter.cs`, `Options/DbOptions.cs` (`WalFilter`) | `include/rocksdb/wal_filter.h` | **ported.** `WalProcessingOption` values match the C API constants; `LogRecordFound` defaults to the legacy `LogRecord`; `ColumnFamilyLogNumberMap` gets sorted id -> log number and name -> id maps. Not persisted (upstream: by name, kCompareNever); an exception thrown by the filter fails the open. |
| `Db/DbImpl.Open.cs` (`ProcessLogFilesAsync`, `ReplayNextRecord`, `InvokeWalFilterOnWalRecord`, `InvokeWalFilterOnColumnFamilyToWalNumberMap`, `UpdatePredecessorWalInfo`, `CheckWalFilesExist`), `Db/DbImpl.ReadOnly.cs` | `DBImpl::SetupLogFilesRecovery`, `ProcessLogFiles`, `ProcessLogFile`, `InitializeLogReader`, `ProcessLogRecord`, `MaybeReviseStopReplayForCorruption`, `HandleNonOkStatusOrOldLogRecord`, `UpdatePredecessorWALInfo`, `MaybeHandleStopReplayForCorruptionForInconsistency`, `InvokeWalFilterIfNeededOn*`, `DBImpl::Recover` ("Opening an existing DB with no WAL files") | **ported**, shared by primary and read-only opens like upstream (secondaries replay through `FragmentBufferedLogReader`, without filter or verification, like `FragmentBufferedReader`). The filter sees the record after timestamp-size reconciliation. IgnoreCurrentRecord; StopReplay (the remaining records and WALs are dropped); CorruptedRecord sets the WAL's status, handled per wal_recovery_mode (ignored and replayed without paranoid_checks); other values NotSupported. A replacement batch keeps the original sequence and must not hold more records ("More than original # of records returned by Wal Filter", NotSupported in every recovery mode; the DB is left untouched). The predecessor info of each WAL replayed without error (number, size on disk, sequence of its last record) is checked against the next WAL's record by `LogReader` ("Missing WAL of log number", "Mismatched predecessor log number", "Mismatched last sequence number recorded in the WAL", "Mismatched size of the WAL"; skipped with kSkipAnyCorruptedRecords and once the replay stopped for corruption); WALs below the minimum log to keep are not reported missing. **Fixed while porting:** reader reports are only recorded with paranoid_checks and not kSkipAnyCorruptedRecords (upstream's reporter has no status otherwise; previously paranoid_checks = false still failed the open); a WAL is read only until its status is not OK, and after a point-in-time stop only the first record of each later WAL may resume the replay (`else if (*stop_replay_for_corruption) break;`; previously every record was tried); the corrupted WAL number of the consistency check is the one the reporter names (the missing predecessor), else the WAL; read-only opens also run the consistency check and, with best_efforts_recovery, skip the WALs like upstream. |
| `Db/DbImpl.Write.cs` (`CreateWal`, `SwitchMemtableCoreAsync`, `WriteGroupToWalAsync`), `Db/DbImpl.TwoWriteQueues.cs`, `Wal/LogWriter.cs` | `DBImpl::SwitchMemtable` (PredecessorWALInfo of `logs_.back()`), `CreateWAL` / `StartWALFile`, `log::Writer::MaybeAddPredecessorWALInfo`, `WriteToWAL` (`AddRecord(..., sequence)`) | **ported.** With track_and_verify_wals every WAL created by a memtable switch starts (after the compression type record) with the predecessor record: the previous WAL's number, its size including buffered bytes and the largest *first* sequence number of its records. **Fixed:** the writer recorded the last sequence of each group; upstream (and the reader's last_seqno_observed) use the record's first sequence, so a multi-key last batch would have made native report a mismatch. The first WAL of an open has no predecessor record (an upstream TODO). With WAL recycling (see "WAL recycling and the WAL matrix") the record is type 131 (recyclable). Records are byte-identical with native in both directions (compat). |
| `Wal/LogReader.cs` (`MaybeVerifyPredecessorWalInfo`) | `log::Reader::MaybeVerifyPredecessorWALInfo` | verified; the mismatched-number message now names the WAL file (`FileName`), like upstream's `file_->file_name()`. |
| `Db/UserWriteCallback.cs`, `RocksDatabase.WriteWithCallback(Async)`, `Db/WriteThread.cs` (`Writer.UserCallback`, `JoinBatchGroup`), `Db/DbImpl.Write.cs`, `DbImpl.UnorderedWrite.cs`, `DbImpl.TwoWriteQueues.cs` | `include/rocksdb/user_write_callback.h`, `DBImpl::WriteWithCallback`, `WriteThread::Writer::CheckWriteEnqueuedCallback` / `CheckPostWalWriteCallback`, `WriteThread::JoinBatchGroup`, `WriteGroupToWAL`, `ConcurrentWriteGroupToWAL` | **ported.** OnWriteEnqueued right after the writer is linked into the queue (also as leader); OnWalWriteFinish after the group's WAL record (and sync) for every writer whose write callback did not fail; never with disableWAL or a failed WAL write. Covers the batched, pipelined and unordered paths and the WAL-only queue of two_write_queues (after the WAL lock is released). Zero cost when unused: one null check per enqueue and per writer of a WAL group. **Known deviation:** NotSupported on sequence-per-batch (WritePrepared / WriteUnprepared) DBs, whose plain writes go through the transaction layer (upstream's DBImpl accepts it there; its StackableDB-based TransactionDB returns NotSupported). |

## WAL recycling and the WAL matrix

C++ reference: `main` (11.14); native comparisons use the 11.12 package (`rocksdb_options_set_recycle_log_file_num`).

Tests:

- `tests/RocksDb.Managed.Tests/Db/WalRecyclingTests.cs`: ports of DBWALTest.FullPurgePreservesRecycledLog,
  FullPurgePreservesLogPendingReuse and TrackAndVerifyWALsRecycleWAL (`db/db_wal_test.cc`) and
  DBWriteTest.RecycleLogToggleTest (`db/db_write_test.cc`). It also checks reuse, sanitization, disableWAL, stale tails
  after a crash and the trim on close.
- `Db/WalMatrixTests.cs`: the unit matrix and its regression tests.
- Compat: `Wal/WalRecyclingCompatTests.cs`, `Wal/WalMatrixCompatTests.cs` and `Wal/WalCrashFeatureCompatTests.cs`.

The dimensions of the matrices are in [testing.md](testing.md#the-wal-matrix).

| Managed file | C++ source(s) | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `Db/DbImpl.cs` (`PurgeObsoleteFiles`, `DeleteObsoleteFilesFullScan`, `_walRecycleFiles`, `_minWalNumberToRecycle`), `Db/DbImpl.Write.cs` (`SwitchMemtableCoreAsync`, `PickRecycledWal`, `CreateWal`, `CloseFreedWals`), `Db/DbImpl.Open.cs` | `DBImpl::FindObsoleteFiles` / `PurgeObsoleteFiles` (wal_recycle_files_, MinLogNumberToRecycle), `DBImpl::SwitchMemtable` (recycle_log_number, IsWalInLogs, IsFileDeletionsEnabled), `CreateWALWriter` (ReuseWritableFile, "reusing log N from recycle list"), `DBImpl::Open` (min_wal_number_to_recycle_) | **ported.** Obsolete WALs of this instance go to the recycle list, oldest first, up to recycle_log_file_num ("adding log N to recycle list"). A WAL older than the instance's first WAL is not recycled, and neither is a WAL whose write failed. The full scan keeps recycled WALs. With track_and_verify_wals_in_manifest, the deletion of a recycled WAL is recorded first, because the WAL is renamed when it is reused. The next memtable switch that creates a WAL reuses the front of the list, unless file deletions are disabled or the WAL's writer is still open. A writer still waiting in the free list is closed first. The number stays in the list until the rename, so a concurrent purge cannot delete the WAL. WALs of a previous instance are deleted, never recycled. |
| `Env/FileContracts.cs` (`IFileSystem.ReuseWritable`), `Env/EnvFileOptions.cs` (`TruncateToSizeOnClose`), `Env/LocalFileSystem.cs`, `Env/MemoryFileSystem.cs` | `FileSystem::ReuseWritableFile` (`PosixFileSystem`: rename, open without O_TRUNC), `PosixWritableFile::Close` (ftruncate of preallocated files) | **ported** as an additive contract method; the default renames the file and opens it without truncation. A reused file is cut to its written size when it is closed. Upstream preallocates WALs by default and trims them on close, so a cleanly closed recycled WAL has no stale tail. **Fixed while testing:** without the trim, a closed reused WAL kept its stale tail. The next point-in-time recovery stopped in that WAL while a column family had already flushed into a later, fresh WAL, and the open failed with "Column family inconsistency" (`WalRecyclingTests.ReusedWalIsCutToItsSizeWhenClosed`). After a crash the stale tail stays, like upstream. |
| `Wal/LogWriter.cs` use in `CreateWal`, `Db/DbImpl.Open.cs` (`SanitizeWalOptions`), `Db/DbImpl.Write.cs` (WriteImpl checks), `Db/DbImpl.Checkpoint.cs` | `CreateWALWriter` (`recycle_log_file_num > 0`), `SanitizeOptions`, `DBImpl::WriteImpl` (disableWAL), `GetLiveFilesStorageInfo` (recycling_log_files) | **ported.** Any non-zero value (after sanitization) writes the recyclable record format, so the predecessor record is type 131. SanitizeOptions sets the option to 0 with WAL_ttl_seconds / WAL_size_limit_MB, kTolerateCorruptedTailRecords or kAbsoluteConsistency; the OPTIONS file holds the sanitized value. A disableWAL write fails with InvalidArgument "WriteOptions::disableWAL option is not supported if DBOptions::recycle_log_file_num > 0", except the WAL-less second write of two_write_queues. Checkpoints copy every WAL instead of hard linking it. async_wal_precreate is not ported, so its interplay with recycling does not apply. |
| `Db/DbImpl.Open.cs` (`WalRecoveryReporter.OldLogRecord`, `ProcessLogFilesAsync`) | `DBOpenLogRecordReadReporter::OldLogRecord`, `ProcessLogFile` / `HandleNonOkStatusOrOldLogRecord` | **ported (was missing).** The reader reports a record from a previous use of a recycled WAL in point-in-time mode. Such a record now stops the replay like a corruption: a later WAL resumes it only if it continues the sequence, and the corrupted WAL number is the recycled WAL's. The predecessor info of that WAL is still updated. |

### Disagreements with native found by the matrices (all fixed)

| Finding | C++ reference | Fix and regression test |
|---|---|---|
| The status of a WAL read error included the file name and the dropped bytes ("Corruption: /db/000011.log: dropping 23 bytes; truncated record body"). Native reports "Corruption: truncated record body". | `DBOpenLogRecordReadReporter::Corruption` (status = s; the details go to ROCKS_LOG_WARN) | The status is the reader's. The LOG gets "[(ignoring error) ]FILE: dropping N bytes; STATUS", and "possibly recycled" for old records. `WalMatrixTests.WalCorruptionStatusIsTheReaderStatus` |
| The column family inconsistency message lacked ". WAL recovery stopped at corruption point, but SST files contain newer data." | `MaybeHandleStopReplayForCorruptionForInconsistency` | The message is completed. |
| A stale copy of an old WAL under a newer number, or two swapped WALs, opened in managed. Native fails in every mode with "Sequence number is being set backwards during recovery ... Prev next seqno: P , current next seqno: C". | `CheckSeqnoNotSetBackDuringRecovery` (per record and per WAL); `MemTableInserter::PutCFImpl` TryAgain + `WriteBatchInternal::Iterate` ("two consecutive TryAgain") | Ported. During WAL recovery, a key+seq already in the memtable fails the batch without advancing the sequence. The failure is reported like a read error, or ignored without paranoid_checks, and then the check fails the open. Before, the inserter skipped duplicates. `WalMatrixTests.StaleWalCopyFailsWithSequenceSetBack` |
| With 2PC and track_and_verify_wals, a clean reopen failed with "Missing WAL of log number N". | `FindObsoleteFiles`: `job_context->log_number = MinLogNumberToKeep()` = `versions_->min_log_number_to_keep()` | With allow_2pc, WALs are deleted or recycled only below the persisted min_log_number_to_keep, where recovery also starts. Before, the port used the live minimum, which commits advance between flushes, and deleted WALs the MANIFEST still asked to keep. Unit matrix (WritePrepared rows) |
| With 2PC, the open after a point-in-time stop failed with "Column family inconsistency"; native opens. A 2PC recovery does not advance min_log_number_to_keep, so the kept corrupted WAL is replayed again. | `DBImpl::Open` (recovered_seq: "add a dummy entry with the expected sequence to the first log right after recovery") | After a point-in-time recovery that found a corrupted WAL, the new WAL starts with an empty batch that carries the recovered next sequence, and the record is synced. `WalMatrixTests.PointInTimeStopWritesTheRecoveredSequenceRecord`, `WalRecyclingCompatTests.TwoPcPointInTimeStopIsReplayedByTheNextOpen` |
| In blob direct write crash images, managed reads failed with "Invalid blob file number N", and later with "No such file" once the open had deleted the unregistered blob file. Native reads every recovered value. | `BlobFilePartitionManager::ResolveBlobDirectWriteIndex` (any file unknown to the version, opened by number with footer skip), `DBImpl::ShouldKeepBlobFileDuringPurge` / `ShouldKeepFooterlessBlobFile` | `BlobDirectWriteManager.GetOrphanBlob` reads files that neither the version nor the manager knows. The full scan keeps footer-less blob files; like upstream, orphan files are leaked. `WalMatrixTests.BlobDirectWriteCrashRecoveryReadsUnregisteredBlobFiles`, `WalCrashFeatureCompatTests` (both writers, with and without avoid_flush_during_recovery, two opens) |

Not disagreements (documented outcomes):

- **WAL filter values outside WalProcessingOption.** The C API (`rocksdb_wal_filter_t::LogRecordFound`, db/c.cc) maps
  them to kCorruptedRecord. The managed API follows the default branch of the C++ `DBImpl`: NotSupported "Unknown
  WalProcessingOption returned by Wal Filter", ignored without paranoid_checks.
- **Blob direct write and power loss.** Like upstream, blob files are synced only for sync writes
  (`SyncBlobDirectWriteManagers`). A WAL synced another way (the closed-WAL sync before a flush, SyncWAL) can outlive
  the blob bytes its records point to.
- **Holes in the recovered data.** Damage to an older WAL can leave holes, and so can kSkipAnyCorruptedRecords and
  paranoid_checks = false: the reader drops the damaged block, and without track_and_verify_wals a missing WAL in the
  middle goes unnoticed. Both engines recover the same contents (compat matrix).

### WAL write error recovery with recycling on (deliberate deviation)

Upstream `DBImpl::ResumeImpl` (`db/db_impl/db_impl.cc`, facebook/rocksdb #15193) refuses file-scoped WAL write error
recovery with NotSupported when `recycle_log_file_num != 0`, so the DB stays write-stopped until it is reopened. The
port runs that recovery with recycling on. It never puts a WAL numbered at or below the failed one on the recycle list:
`PurgeObsoleteFiles` deletes it instead (`n > _failedWalNumber`). The failed writer is closed without a flush or sync,
and the replacement WAL may itself be a reused healthy WAL. A later instance never recycles any WAL of an earlier one
(`_minWalNumberToRecycle`). That covers a failed WAL left behind by a crash.

Evidence. These tests use recycle_log_file_num 1 and 4 with kPointInTimeRecovery and kSkipAnyCorruptedRecords. The
other two recovery modes are left out because SanitizeOptions turns recycling off for them. The failed WAL is always a
reused file.

- `tests/RocksDb.Managed.Tests/Db/WalErrorRecyclingTests.cs`. Poisoned writes: a file-scoped append error with nothing,
  half or all of the record written; a file-scoped sync error; NoSpace through the SstFileManager. The tests check:
  - **Recovery.** Writes continue on a replacement WAL, and the failed batch is not in the DB. Its sequence is reserved,
    so later writes start after it.
  - **No reuse.** The failed WAL is never reused: it is not on the recycle list, no rename takes it as its source, and
    the LOG has neither "adding log F" nor "reusing log F". Later healthy WALs are still recycled and reused.
  - **Reopen.** Every acknowledged write survives two reopens and the failed write does not. The sequence never goes
    back.
  - **DisableFileDeletions.** When file deletions are disabled across the failure, the failed WAL stays on disk. A
    tailing iterator opened before the failure and new iterators never return the failed batch. Once deletions are
    re-enabled, the failed WAL is deleted, not recycled.
  - **Crash sweep.** The file system dies at each mutating operation after the failure, with power loss and with a
    process kill. The sweep covers replacement WAL creation (reuse rename and create), the atomic flush's table file,
    the directory sync, the MANIFEST atomic group, the failed WAL's deletion and the first post-recovery writes. Each
    crash is followed by two reopens. Acknowledged writes are always there. The failed write is there only for a
    complete record (fully written, or a sync error), and only when nothing after the failure was acknowledged. The
    failed WAL gets no I/O after the failure.
  - **Concurrent writers.** Eight writers (sync and non-sync writes, sync or async API) run through two poisoned WALs.
    Every acknowledged write survives, no failed write is ever visible, and no WAL batch holds one.
- `tests/RocksDb.Managed.CompatTests/Wal/WalErrorRecyclingCompatTests.cs` (native 11.12). Every crash image of the same
  sweep, and the completed recovery, is copied to disk and opened twice by each engine with the same
  recycle_log_file_num and recovery mode. Both engines recover identical contents. For the complete-record variants,
  some images hold the failed write in both engines: the crash came before the recovery's MANIFEST commit. Native
  cannot run the recovery itself, because it refuses it.

Expected outcome, not a bug: a failed write whose record reached the WAL can reappear after a crash before the recovery
commits, even after a power loss. Its bytes overwrite synced bytes of the reused file in place. This is the usual
outcome of a write that returned an error. Once the recovery's MANIFEST edit is durable, every column family's log
number is past the failed WAL and no open replays it.

Bugs these tests found (fixed):

| Finding | C++ reference | Fix and regression test |
|---|---|---|
| GetUpdatesSince returned the failed batch after a successful recovery when the failed WAL could still be read (file deletions disabled, an iterator already reading it, or the window between publishing the reserved sequence and deleting the WAL). Its sequence is published, and the next batch continues the run. A WalTailer replica would apply a write the primary does not have. | `WalRecoverySequenceTracker`, `WalIteratorImpl::StopBeforeRecoveryGap` (`db/wal_iterator_impl.cc`), `WALIOStatusCheck` -> `RecordIndeterminateWriteSequence` | **Ported.** `Db/WalRecoverySequenceTracker.cs`. The failed range of every failed WAL write group is recorded in the main and the two_write_queues paths. Upstream records only file-scoped errors, but the port recovers every WAL error. An iterator that reaches the range ends its run with NotFound "Gap in sequence numbers after indeterminate WAL write through sequence N". A tailer then restarts after N or rebuilds. `WalErrorRecyclingTests.FailedWalKeptByDisabledDeletionsIsFencedAndNeverRecycled` fails without the fence. |
| A tailing GetUpdatesSince iterator stopped seeing new records in a reused WAL. The reader had buffered the previous use's stale records past the live end, or dropped them on a checksum mismatch, and the writer then overwrote them in place. The iterator later failed with a sequence gap. | `FragmentBufferedReader` (same behaviour upstream) | **Managed fix.** In a recycled log, a read that returns no record remembers the end of the last complete record. `TransactionLogIterator` reopens the WAL there when the DB has published newer sequences (`FragmentBufferedLogReader.AtStaleTail` / `Reposition`). Logs that are not recycled are unaffected. `WalRecyclingTests.TailingIteratorFollowsReusedWal` |
| Deadlock between the SstFileManager's out-of-space recovery and a flush or compaction. `ClearErrorAsync` read the DB's background error, which takes the DB mutex, while it held the manager's lock. `VersionSet.InstallEdit` calls `SstFileManager.OnAddFile`, which takes the manager's lock, while it holds the DB mutex. | `SstFileManagerImpl::ClearError` | The error is read before the lock is taken. `WalErrorRecyclingTests.RecoveryWithRecyclingNeverReusesTheFailedWal(NoSpace)` hung without the fix. |

### Tailing reused WALs: secondaries and replication (deliberate deviation)

C++ reference: `DBImplSecondary::RecoverLogFiles` / `MaybeInitLogReader` (`db/db_impl/db_impl_secondary.cc`) and
`log::FragmentBufferedReader::ReadRecord` / `TryReadFragment` / `TryReadMore` (`db/log_reader.cc`), `main` (11.14).
The same code ships in the native 11.12 package.

A reused WAL keeps its old size, and the primary overwrites it in place. A reader that tails it, such as a secondary
catching up, a GetUpdatesSince iterator or a WalTailer, reads the new records and then bytes of the file's previous use.
The writer later overwrites those bytes. Upstream's tailing reader mishandles them in three ways:

- **Old record.** When the live end is the header of a record of the previous use, `TryReadFragment` returns
  kOldRecord without consuming the record. `ReadRecord` treats kOldRecord like kEof and loops, so it gets the same
  record again forever. `DBImplSecondary::RecoverLogFiles` holds the DB mutex while this happens. So OpenAsSecondary
  or TryCatchUpWithPrimary never returns. The non-tailing `log::Reader` consumes the record and treats it as EOF.
- **Bad length.** A stale header whose length runs past the block makes `TryReadFragment` read the next block. The rest
  of the current block is skipped. The writer then fills that rest with records the reader never returns. A
  stale zero header does the same (kBadRecord clears the buffer).
- **Bad checksum.** A recycled log ends at the first bad checksum, and the buffer is dropped. The next read starts at
  the next block.

The port keeps the record formats. The tailing reader (`Wal/FragmentBufferedLogReader.cs`) never consumes or skips
bytes of a recycled log that it has not validated:

- An old record, a bad checksum, a header whose record does not fit in the rest of the buffered block, and a zero
  header end the read without consuming anything (`StaleData`). Block trailers are still skipped: they are shorter than
  a recyclable header, and an incomplete record at the end of the file still waits for the writer.
- The reader stays stopped (`AtStaleTail`) until `Reposition`. Reposition opens the WAL again and continues from the
  end of the last complete record, or from the First fragment of an unfinished record.
- `TransactionLogIterator` repositions when the DB has published newer sequences. Since the earlier fix, the replication
  layer's `WalTailer` uses this. The secondary repositions each stale reader at the start of every catch-up
  (`DbImpl.ReadOnly.cs`, `TryRepositionSecondaryWalReader`).

Logs that are not recycled are read as before.

Evidence:

- Unit (`tests/RocksDb.Managed.Tests`):
  - `Db/ReplicationTests.FragmentBufferedReaderNeverSkipsStaleBytesOfARecycledLog`. Over 100 seeds, a recycled log is
    overwritten in place while the reader holds buffered stale bytes. Some seeds use same-size records. Every record
    is read, in order. Without the bad-length check, some seeds lose records.
  - `Db/WalRecyclingTests.SecondaryCatchUpFollowsReusedWal(sameSizeRecords)`. A secondary catches up every 13 writes and
    after each rotation into a reused WAL. Before the fix, the same-size variant hung on the old record. With only the
    old-record fix, the secondary missed records and its sequence fell behind.
  - `Db/WalRecyclingTests.TailingIteratorFollowsReusedWalAtOldRecordHeader`. Before the fix, the iterator hung.
- Replication (`tests/RocksDb.Managed.Replication.Tests`): `ReplicationTests.StreamingFromRecyclingSourceKeepsReplicaIdentical`,
  on the local file system and on MemoryFileSystem. A recycling source streams to a replica, first in the middle of
  reused WALs, then with three concurrent writers and rotations. The replica has the same latest sequence, contents
  and digest. Before the bad-length fix, the stream failed with "Gap in sequence numbers" in most runs: the tailer had
  skipped the rest of a block that the writer filled after the tailer buffered it.
- **Limit, not a bug.** Recycling keeps no WAL for tailers, and WAL_ttl_seconds / WAL_size_limit_MB turn recycling
  off. A tailer that falls a whole recycle cycle behind is reading a WAL that the primary renames and overwrites. The
  rest of that WAL's records are gone. The iterator returns only the genuine records it still had buffered, then ends
  with NotFound "Gap in sequence numbers", so a WalTailer replica resyncs.
  `WalRecyclingTests.TailingIteratorGetsNotFoundWhenItsWalIsReused` covers this. Under 8 CPU hogs, the replication test
  above hit this in 3 of 20 runs. The test now waits for the tailer before each rotation, while the other writers keep
  writing.
- Compat (`tests/RocksDb.Managed.CompatTests`): `Api/SecondaryCompatTests.ManagedSecondaryTailsRecyclingNativePrimary`.
  A managed secondary follows a native 11.12 primary with recycle_log_file_num = 2. The data rows use random-size and
  same-size records, and the secondary catches up in the middle of reused WALs.
- Round trip (`tests/RocksDb.Managed.RoundTrip`): `SecondaryRecyclingRoundTripTests.NativeSecondaryOnRecyclingPrimary`.
  The primary is managed or native. The native secondary runs in a child process. While the primary is live and its
  reused WAL ends at an old record, the native OpenAsSecondary never returns for either primary. A native stack
  showed the thread spinning in `FragmentBufferedReader::TryReadFragment` under `ReadRecord`, `RecoverLogFiles` and
  `OpenAsSecondaryImpl`. Once the primary is closed, its reused WAL is cut to its written size. The native secondary
  then reads every record either engine wrote.

Each of these tests passed 20 runs in a row under 8 CPU hogs (net10.0).

So a native secondary cannot follow a live primary with recycle_log_file_num > 0, whichever engine is the primary. A
managed secondary can follow either.

### GenericRateLimiter statistics test: timing assumption, not a race

`RateLimiterTests.StatisticsAreRecorded` failed once in a full net10.0 run. Under 12 CPU hogs plus allocating threads,
the old test body failed in about 22% of 300 iterations. Each time, the third request was not counted as delayed
(RATE_LIMITER_DELAYED_REQUESTS_READ was 0). The refill timer granted the second request and woke the test thread. That
thread took longer than the 10 ms refill period to issue the third request. The request then found a refill due and
was granted at once, which is correct (upstream `RequestImpl` refills on the request path in the same way). The
statistics are recorded under the limiter's lock before the grant is signalled, so no statistic was lost or raced.

Fix (test only, plus a seam): `GenericRateLimiter` has an internal constructor that takes a microsecond clock, like the
`clock` argument of upstream's constructor that `util/rate_limiter_test.cc` sets to a mock clock. The test advances a
manual clock. It checks that each later request waits while the clock stands still and is granted by the refill
after one period. The test is stricter now: it asserts exact wait totals (10000 us per delayed request) and the read
histogram sum. Results: 300 iterations under 12 hogs with allocation load, all passed.

## Read path, table options and read statistics (second correctness review, 2026-11-06)

Earlier claims corrected: `ReadTier.MemtableTier` used to make Get / MultiGet skip the table files (and fail with
Corruption "Incomplete: ...") while iterators still read them; upstream does the opposite (`NewInternalIterator` skips
`Version::AddIterators` for kMemtableTier, point lookups are not restricted). `ReadTier.BlockCacheTier` only applied to
filter checks. `ReadOptions.Tailing`, `ValueSizeSoftLimit`, `VerifyChecksums` (per read), `Deadline` / `IoTimeout`,
`optimize_filters_for_hits`, `verify_compression`, `detect_filter_construct_corruption` and `read_amp_bytes_per_bit`
were parsed but had no effect, and seeks inflated BLOOM_FILTER_PREFIX_USEFUL.

A table that is not open fails the read (per key for MultiGet). `VerifyChecksums = false` skips block checksum verification for blocks the read loads (persistent-cache paths keep the table setting). |

## Second correctness review: compaction, versions and MANIFEST (2026-11-06)

C++ reference: `main` (11.14); native comparisons use the 11.12 package. Unit tests:
`tests/RocksDb.Managed.Tests/Db/CompactionReviewFixesTests.cs`; native comparisons:
`CompatTests/Compaction/CompactionReviewCompatTests.cs` and
`CompactionPickerParityTests.RoundRobinTrivialMovesAdvanceCursorLikeNative`.

| Area | C++ source | Managed | Tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| Write-conflict-boundary snapshots | `DBImpl::GetSnapshotForWriteConflictBoundary`, `SnapshotImpl::is_write_conflict_boundary_`, `SnapshotList::GetAll(oldest_write_conflict_snapshot)`, `TransactionBaseImpl::SetSnapshot`, `WritePreparedTxn::SetSnapshot`, `CompactionIterator` (earliest_write_conflict_snapshot) | **fixed.** `Transaction.SetSnapshot` (all policies) takes a boundary snapshot; the compaction job gets the oldest one (read under the mutex when the job starts: a boundary snapshot taken after the snapshot list was loaded is newer than every input, so the pairs it could need are kept anyway). A SingleDelete + Put pair newer than a transaction snapshot is kept (the SingleDelete and the value-less Put), so the conflict check still sees the write. Flush needs no boundary: the managed flush never drops a SingleDelete or what follows it. Remote compaction workers use kMaxSequenceNumber, as upstream (CompactionServiceInput carries no boundary). | pessimistic: `txn.SetSnapshot`, Put + SingleDelete, flush, history pushed out, CompactRange(kForce), `txn.Put` -> Busy; optimistic: both versions survive the compaction; plain snapshot: the pair is dropped |
| `CompactionFilter::IgnoreSnapshots() = false` | `CompactionJob::SetupAndValidateCompactionFilter`, `BuildTable` | **fixed.** Compactions fail with NotSupported "CompactionFilter::IgnoreSnapshots() = false is not supported anymore." before opening an input; a flush fails the same way when the factory filters flushes (ShouldFilterTableFileCreation(kFlush)). | filter, factory and flush-factory cases |
| ReFitLevel / change_level | `DBImpl::ReFitLevel`, `DBImpl::CompactRange` (DisableManualCompaction + PauseBackgroundWork around it) | **fixed.** Aborted "Moving more than 1 file from non-L0 to L0 is not allowed ..."; NotSupported for ongoing compaction output in between levels and for a second refit; a kRefitLevel compaction is registered (files marked being compacted) while LogAndApply releases the mutex; manual compaction is disabled around the refit, so a concurrent CompactRange / CompactFiles fails with Incomplete(kManualCompactionPaused) like upstream. Deviation: flushes are not paused (PauseBackgroundWork also stops flushes upstream); they only add L0 files with newer epoch numbers, and the refit range is registered. | Aborted check; a CompactRange and a CompactFiles started at `DBImpl::ReFitLevel:PostRegisterCompaction` fail with Incomplete; 40 refits racing CompactRange loops: no background error, all keys readable |
| VersionBuilder consistency checks | `VersionBuilder::CheckConsistency` / `CheckConsistencyDetails` (`force_consistency_checks`) | **fixed.** Checked before a LogAndApply edit is written and on the recovered version: L0 newest first (epoch number, or seqnos when an L0 file has no epoch: kMightMissing), no overlapping L0 files of one epoch, sorted non-overlapping L1+, no blob file that is entirely garbage; Corruption "force_consistency_checks: VersionBuilder: ...". The table/blob link check holds by construction (`BlobFileMetaData.Build` derives the links from the table files). Upstream always checks in debug builds; the managed port follows the option in all builds. | live edit with overlapping L1 files rejected (nothing written); MANIFEST with overlapping L1 files fails to open with Corruption (opens with the checks off); unsorted / same-epoch overlap unit cases. Native 11.12 opens the same MANIFEST without an error but does not present the overlapping files either (0 files at L1). |
| Compaction output validation | `OutputValidator` (`db/output_validator.cc`), `CompactionOutputs::AddToOutput`, `CompactionJob::VerifyOutputFiles` (`verify_output_flags`, `paranoid_file_checks`) | **fixed.** Every output key is checked against the previous key of its output file (one comparison with the output's largest key, which the job keeps anyway): "Compaction sees out-of-order keys." / "Compaction tries to write a key without internal bytes." With paranoid_file_checks or kVerifyIteration the rolling NPHash64 of keys and values is kept per output and compared after reading the file back; kVerifyBlockChecksum reads every data block with checksum verification (meta blocks are verified when the reader opens the file, so the block pass and the iteration pass are one scan); kVerifyFileChecksum recomputes the file checksum. Outputs are verified at the end of each subcompaction. New typed option `ColumnFamilyOptions.VerifyOutputFlags` (`verify_output_flags`, mutable). Deviation: `paranoid_file_checks` does not verify flush outputs (BuildTable's read-back is not ported). Cost: compaction CPU within noise, see docs/perf.md. | comparator reordered after picking -> out-of-order Corruption; corrupted output detected for paranoid / each flag, unnoticed without; the two upstream regression tests (iteration without paranoid, all flags) |
| oldest_ancestor_time of outputs | `CompactionJob::OpenCompactionOutputFile` / `FinishCompactionOutputFile`, `Compaction::MinInputFileOldestAncestorTime`, `FileMetaData::TryGetOldestAncestorTime` | **fixed.** The time comes from the inputs overlapping the subcompaction range, refined per finished output to the inputs overlapping its key range (MANIFEST value; the table property keeps the subcompaction value like upstream); a file without a MANIFEST value uses `rocksdb.creation.time` of an open table instead of its file creation time. | multi-output compaction of old and new data with ttl: outputs of new data are not TTL-expired |
